# EngrCAD

**Detailed design rationale lives in [design.md](design.md)** (architecture, conventions,
algorithm choices, numerical lessons, roadmap); each `src/*` project also has a
`README.md` describing its contents. **[todo.md](todo.md)** is the idea backlog
(open work only. **DELETE a completed item; do NOT strike it through.** The file had
drifted to 16% struck-through entries carrying their own commentary, which is a second
copy of what the commit message, CLAUDE.md and design.md already say — and it is the
copy nobody updates. Where a finished item leaves a genuine finding, such as a measured
DECLINE that should stop someone redoing the work, that finding belongs in CLAUDE.md's
lesson bullets or in design.md, and the todo entry still goes. The one thing to check
before deleting is NESTED CHILDREN: a finished parent often carries sub-items that are
still open, and those are promoted, not removed. Largely harvested from a
survey of geometry3Sharp at `C:\Users\chris\projects\git\geometry3Sharp` — study the
referenced g3 classes before implementing an item).

**Documentation upkeep is part of every change, not an afterthought.** When a feature
lands, the SAME integration pass must update: (1) the owning project's `README.md`;
(2) this file's current-status paragraph and roadmap (including any numerical lessons
learned — they are this project's institutional memory); (3) `design.md` when a design
decision or algorithm rationale was made; (4) `todo.md` (remove the done item, file
residual follow-ups as new open items); and (5) **the docs site** (`docs/`) for
user-facing features — an example page with a ` ```csharp render:<id> ` fence per
`docs/writing-examples.md`, verified with `dotnet run --project tools/EngrCAD.DocsGen
-- docs` (snippets are compiled/executed/rendered, so stale examples fail the build).
XML doc comments on new public API feed the generated API reference. A feature
without docs is not done.

A CAD application in modern .NET built around a **hybrid geometry kernel** that natively supports three representations:

- **B-Rep** — parametric surfaces (planes, conics, NURBS) wrapped in topology, for precision modeling
- **Implicit** — signed distance fields (SDF) as an AST of primitives and operators, for lattices and organic blends
- **Mesh** — discrete half-edge triangle meshes, for rendering, FEA, and 3D printing

A distinctive design goal is **LINQ-native geometry querying**: a custom `IQueryable` provider inspects expression trees and routes spatial predicates to spatial indexes (BVH/octree) instead of linear scans.

## Current status

Phases 1–4 plus Query v1 implemented (~PLACEHOLDER tests): `EngrCAD.Core` (math + spatial incl. `Bvh.Nearest`), `EngrCAD.Mesh` (half-edge engine: booleans with seam zipping, Loop subdivision, QEM decimation, `MeshIntersection` surface-crossing queries; **deformation/analysis foundation**: `LaplacianMeshSmoother` (implicit cotangent fairing, (M + λL)x′ = Mx, dimensionless scale-free TimeStep, degenerate cotangents fall back to uniform weight under the 1e-13-relative sliver guard, negative cot sums sign-clamped so L stays PSD) + `LaplacianMeshDeformer` (bi-Laplacian soft handles, rim/pins hard-substituted bit-identically so `DeformRegion` composes with `MeshRegionOperator` reinsertion), `MeshLocalParam` (Schmidt-style discrete exp map in Dijkstra settle order; trig-free minimal-rotation transport `v·c + axis×v + axis(axis·v)/(1+c)`, stable for all c > −1; exact on planes, ≤2% on a developable tube, ≤5% radial on a 35° sphere cap), `MeshIsoCurves` (per-vertex scalar contours; one crossing per undirected edge from the lower-indexed vertex — the boolean seam lesson — so endpoints are bit-identical and chains assemble combinatorially), `DijkstraGraphDistance` (edge-graph geodesics on `IndexPriorityQueue`), and `MeshIcp` (point-to-plane, 6×6 about the correspondence centroid via `SparseCholesky`; **refuses singular correspondence geometry** — all-planar leaves 3 DOF free — reporting Converged = false rather than Tikhonov-damping, the MateSolver convention; landing it made Core's `SymmetricEigen3` public with BOTH orderings and deleted Mesh's duplicated Jacobi solver). All backed by **`EngrCAD.Core.Solvers`** — dependency-free sparse mini-library (CSR `PackedSparseMatrix` with symmetric-upper storage, Jacobi-preconditioned `SparseSymmetricCG` with convergence as a return value, up-looking `SparseCholesky` with an opt-in `SparseOrdering.Amd` fill-reducing permutation (`AmdOrdering`: quotient graph, element absorption, mass elimination, hash-detected supervariables, assembly-tree postorder) — natural stays the default because reordering is not bit-identical arithmetic — plus **`AnalyzePattern` → `SparseCholeskySymbolic`**, a symbolic factorization analysed ONCE and reused to factorize a family of matrices sharing the pattern (the topology-optimisation loop, whose reduced stiffness has an identical pattern every iteration while only the per-element scales change): the ordering, elimination tree and column counts are skipped and only the numeric pass runs, and it is **bit-identical to a fresh `Factorize` of the same matrix by construction** — the reuse GATHERS the new values into exactly the slots `Factorize` would place them (a pure copy, the value-gather map being `UpperCsc`/`SymmetricPermute` mirrored in INDEX not in value) then runs the SAME numeric pass, so the saving is the symbolic fraction and is BOUNDED, 1.50× on a 2D grid Laplacian down to 1.02× on a 3D FEA stiffness where the numeric pass is the floor — and **`SparseLdlt`**, the symmetric-INDEFINITE L·D·Lᵀ (real or complex symmetric) for exactly the systems Cholesky refuses: the direct per-frequency harmonic system `(K − ω²M + iωC)` is complex SYMMETRIC with a symmetric-indefinite equivalent real form, and the complex spelling was chosen over a real Bunch–Kaufman because for this family the "2×2 pivots" are FIXED BY STRUCTURE rather than searched (a complex pivot r + is is invertible whenever (r, s) ≠ 0), so the symbolic pass is Cholesky's verbatim and AMD applies unchanged — where a magnitude-searched 2×2 pivot merges column patterns and stales the whole symbolic machinery, which is why sparse Bunch–Kaufman is a multifrontal-scale project and stays filed; with any positive-definite damping present no pivot can vanish at ANY frequency (a provable kernel argument in the class doc), the real overload serves saddle systems with constraints ordered last, and exactly-zero pivots refuse naming the caller's column; also the substrate for future sketch constraints and FEA), plus the **general NON-symmetric family — `Gmres` (restarted GMRES(m)), `BiCgStab`, and an `Ilu0` preconditioner** — the CFD campaign's stage 1, since advection makes a flow operator non-symmetric so neither Cholesky nor CG applies. GMRES is **right-preconditioned so the tracked residual IS the original system's** (the silent-CFD-failure guard: it converges on the residual a caller can recompute, and the report recomputes `‖b−Ax‖` exactly every restart), converges in ≤ n steps un-restarted (a theorem, measured 12/35 at 2.7e-13), and treats a happy breakdown as convergence; BiCGSTAB detects its ρ≈0 / ω≈0 breakdowns BEFORE the division and reports `Converged=false` rather than a silent NaN. **`Ilu0` has zero fill by definition, so AMD does NOT apply** — a fill-reducing ordering has nothing to reduce and would only change which entries are dropped (RCM/multicolour, filed for the ILU(p)/ILUT tier) — and it reproduces the exact LU on a no-fill matrix (an identity) while cutting iterations as a preconditioner (GMRES 40→10, BiCGSTAB 37→7 on a 256-DOF convection–diffusion; CG 87→32 on a Dirichlet Laplacian, since ILU(0) of a symmetric matrix is `L·D·Lᵀ` and rides `CgOptions.Preconditioner` additively). `IPreconditioner` is the shared `z = M⁻¹r` seam, null = identity), `EngrCAD.Implicit` (SDF AST, incl. the TPMS and strut-lattice families, uniform and graded), `EngrCAD.BRep` (curves/surfaces/topology foundations), `EngrCAD.Interop` (the full conversion triangle: implicit→mesh via manifold **dual contouring with Hermite data** — the vertex goes at the minimiser of the field's own tangent planes (`SurfaceQef`) rather than at the mean of the crossings, because the mean lies strictly inside every convex corner BY CONSTRUCTION and a polygonized box came back chamfered at every resolution; a box is now reproduced EXACTLY (every vertex reads exactly zero from its own field, volume exactly the box's, at any placement, 4.4e-12 rotated) and smooth fields improve an order of magnitude in volume error, with an opt-in **adaptive** mode collapsing a box to six quads and a drilled one 10.4× — plus `SurfaceCull` visiting only the blocks the surface can reach (bit-identical output, 1.6–3.0×) and an inside component getting **one vertex per SHEET it bounds** rather than one in total (see the lessons below) — B-Rep→mesh via tessellation — whose trimmed-face tiers now put the natural grid's **interior rows into the base triangulation** (rowed strips/periodic bands/pole fans; refinement demoted to residual duty), so tall trimmed bands converge instead of being carried by midpoint bisection —  mesh→implicit via `MeshSdf` with angle-weighted pseudonormals — plus an opt-in `MeshSignSource.WindingNumber` sign source that accepts open meshes, default unchanged; **the fourth edge — mesh→B-Rep RECONSTRUCTION** via `MeshToBrep.Reconstruct`, the one direction that puts information BACK rather than discretizing: a triangle mesh re-recognised as a parametric `BrepSolid` of analytic faces, so a drilled plate comes back as SEVEN faces (six planes + one cylinder bore) rather than five thousand planar facets wearing a `.step` extension — the FACE COUNT being the metric that separates the feature from its impostor. v1 is the tessellated-CAD case (vertices ON the surface, so a fit's residual is the chord error and a cylinder's radius is recovered EXACTLY at every density, never the inscribed `r·cos(π/n)` a chord fit gives); segmentation region-grows across sharp creases, plane/cylinder/sphere fit with the residual REPORTED (cone/torus/freeform/scan refused by name), region boundaries recovered as the EXACT surface∩surface intersection with triple points snapped by `SurfaceCorner`, and `SolidAssembler` welds the shared edges into a manifold `ShapeHealing` orients and `Validate()` accepts — box/cylinder/drilled-plate reconstruct to valid closed solids with matching volumes; a seamless whole sphere is refused by name (one face, no boundary edge)), `EngrCAD.Query` (BVH-backed IQueryable), **`EngrCAD.Modeling` (the unified `Shape` API: model once with one vocabulary — primitives incl. `MakeSphere`/`MakeTorus`, extrude/revolve/sweep, booleans, smooth blends/offset/shell/lattice, transforms — then decide the representation at the end: `ToBrep()`/`ToImplicit()`/`ToMesh()`; lowering bakes accumulated transforms into construction inputs for exact B-Reps, bridges non-native nodes through tessellation + `MeshSdf` or Surface Nets, and `Explain(target)` reports each node as Native/Bridged/Impossible — see `src/EngrCAD.Modeling/README.md` for the support matrix; `Shape.From(...)` wraps raw engine geometry so designs can drop down to any engine API and come back — and `Shape.From(path)` imports .stl/.obj/.off via `MeshReader.ReadAndRepair` (out-param `MeshRepairReport`; hole filling opt-in; docs `examples/import.md`); **sketching**: `Sketch` (fluent builder — `LineTo`/`ArcTo`/`ArcThrough`/`BezierTo`/`QuadraticTo` — plus **`EllipticalArcTo`** and Rectangle/RoundedRectangle/Circle/**Ellipse**/Polygon/Slot primitives, `WithHole`, exact `Area()`) placed by `SketchPlane` and consumed by `Shape.Extrude/Revolve/Sweep`; **sketch constraints**: `Sketch.Constrain()`/`ConstrainedSketch` (SketchConstraints.cs/SketchConstraintSolver.cs) — the full coincident/horizontal/vertical/parallel/perpendicular/tangent/equal/concentric/fix + distance/angle/radius + **point-on-object** vocabulary (`PointOn(point, line)`/`PointOn(point, arc)` — the CARRIER, infinite line and whole circle, since clamping to the drawn stretch would be a branch selector in disguise; point-on-line is the point-to-line dimension AT ZERO, which is the right reduction rather than a shortcut because that residual is SIGNED and stays first order through its own solution, where point-to-POINT distance's zero is a cone point and is refused in favour of `Coincident`; point-on-arc reuses the exact row the solver already applies to an arc's own two endpoints, so it is that rule asked rather than restated; a point drawn AT the centre is refused by name — `|p − c| − r` has no gradient direction there; **and the two CURVED carriers landed too**, `PointOn(point, curve)` over `cs.Curve(i)` for a cubic bézier or an elliptical arc, where the backlog's framing was half wrong in the instructive direction: it filed both as needing "its own foot parameter as a VARIABLE" because the nearest point is a solve, which is true of the DISTANCE and not of MEMBERSHIP — an ellipse is a CONIC, so `|M⁻¹(p − C)| − 1` is the arc's own residual with the radius replaced by the semi-axis matrix, one row and no new unknown, reducing to it exactly for A = (r,0), B = (0,r); a cubic genuinely has no such form, so its foot IS a variable and the residual is `B(t) − p = 0`, two rows against one unknown, which removes exactly the one DOF a point-on-curve constraint means (asserted off the solver's own rank). Both carriers ride the chord SIMILARITY of their joints rather than carrying shape variables, which is the simplification rather than the obstacle: the ellipse's normalized coordinate is similarity-INVARIANT so it is evaluated against the drawn conic through ζ = (p − s)/(e − s), and a cubic is `B(t) = s + (e − s)·β(t)` for a fixed complex β read off the drawn control offsets. The same fact gives the END TANGENCY those two kinds lacked (`Tangent(curve, SketchCurveEnd, line)`, parallel or perpendicular): an end tangent is a FIXED multiple of the live chord, so it is the ordinary direction row with the curve's constant folded in — with the consequence stated rather than discovered, that on a loop where the curve and the line share BOTH joints the relation is a constant no motion can change and the solver correctly names a stationary configuration. **And a LINE tangent to an elliptical arc's whole conic** (`Tangent(line, curve)`) needs no foot parameter either, for the same reason membership did not: writing the conic as p(u) = C + M·u with M = [A B] and |u| = 1, the signed distance to a line with unit normal n̂ through q is n̂·(C − q) + (Mᵀn̂)·u, whose extremes over the unit circle are n̂·(C − q) ± |Mᵀn̂| — so tangency is ONE EXTREME VANISHING, one signed first-order row with no new unknown, reducing to the circular `Tangent(line, arc)` exactly when A and B are perpendicular and equal. Which of the two tangents is meant is the drawing's to say, so a line drawn THROUGH the centre is refused by name (the point-at-the-centre singularity again), and a cubic is refused WITH ITS REASON — no closed-form support function, so its tangency wants the foot as a variable, which is `PointOnBezier`'s shape plus one row and is filed. It is evaluated by pulling the LINE back through the chord similarity rather than by transporting the conic, which is legal because a similarity preserves tangency and is what leaves the centre and both semi-axes compile-time constants) solved variationally by analytic-Jacobian LM with a rank-revealing DOF report (MateSolver doctrine throughout: the drawn configuration is seed AND branch selector, under-constrained is normal and reported, contradictions and stationary configurations are named, a failed solve leaves the drawn sketch unchanged); solved output is an ordinary `Sketch`, so regions/extrudes/features downstream are untouched. Two lessons from it: **adjacent line–arc tangency must be constrained as d̂·(c − J) = 0 at the shared joint, never as center-to-carrier-distance = r** — the distance form leaves the tangency foot only second-order constrained, so a 1e-9-converged solve carries √(2r·1e-9) ≈ 1e-4 of foot slop and the near-zero singular value corrupts the rank; and **the rank floor is 1e-6 relative singular values, not the mate solver's 1e-8** — 1e-8² sits below pivoted elimination's own round-off at sketch sizes (a rank-9 Jacobian over 14 variables measurably reported rank 10, an arithmetic impossibility); **twisted/tapered extrusion** (OpenSCAD `linear_extrude(twist, scale, slices)`): `Shape.Extrude(sketch, height, twist, scale, slices)` — a pure taper is B-Rep-**Native** (a linearly scaled straight side sweeps an exact PLANE through the scaling centre, so the solid is a ruled two-section loft; holed sketches wait on loft-sections-with-holes), a twist is B-Rep-Impossible and lowers to a direct mesh section sweep (`TwistedExtrusion`) wrapped in a mesh SDF for implicit — two lessons: the PROFILE must be subdivided to match the angular step or volume converges only FIRST order in slices (a wall panel lerps a long edge's lateral rotation linearly; measured deficit 286→33→8.2 at 8/64/256 slices, twist-matched subdivision restores second order), and the subdivision points are exactly collinear so earcut filters them from the caps while walls still reference them — the caps re-expand chords over dropped runs (MeshPlaneCut's collinear-chord zip, generalized to the multi-loop layout) so the mesh closes by index; **resize/scale** (OpenSCAD `resize()`/`scale([x,y,z])`): `Shape.Scale(x,y,z)` + `Shape.Resized(newSize, auto?)` over the new measured `Shape.Bounds(quality)` (mesh-lowering bounds, eagerly at the call, scaling about the origin; support rides the existing non-uniform-transform policy — extrusion-family exact, ellipsoid-family Impossible by name, implicit bridged unless factors equal); **heightmap terrain** (OpenSCAD `surface()`): `Shape.Heightmap` over `Heightmap.Mesh/ReadDat/ReadPng` — manifold-by-construction terrain solid (top grid + perimeter walls + ONE n-gon base over the same bottom vertices, welded by index), with a dependency-free grayscale-PNG reader (BCL `ZLibStream` inflate, all five scanline filters, 8/16-bit; color/palette/interlaced rejected by name); lines/arcs→exact NURBS profiles for B-Rep, and the sketch's exact 2D signed distance (`SketchRegion`: per-segment exact distances + even–odd parity over precomputed y-monotone pieces) makes sketch extrusions and full revolutions implicit-**Native** via `Sdf.ExtrudedRegion`/`RevolvedRegion` (`IPlanarRegion` in EngrCAD.Implicit); **elliptical arcs are first-class** (`EllipseSeg` + the new `Ellipse2d` beside `Arc2d`): the segment stores the centre and both semi-axis VECTORS, so a rotated ellipse is the ordinary case rather than a third parameter and an affine map of the axes is an affine map of the curve — `Area()` is πab exactly (the Green's term is ½(C×(End−Start) + (A×B)·sweep), which REDUCES to the circular one for A=(r,0), B=(0,r)), the y-monotone parity pieces invert `y − C.y = R·sin(θ+φ)` in CLOSED FORM (so the ray crossing is as exact as a circle's, not a bisection like a bézier's), and the B-Rep carries an `Ellipse3d`, which the whole chain already spoke (STEP both ways, `BrepArchive`, IGES, `TessellationQuality`, plane sections). The one thing with no closed form is the DISTANCE — point-to-ellipse is a quartic root — so it delegates to `Curve2d`'s shared scan-and-Newton rather than restating it; `SketchRegion` now carries its own kernel transcribing that delegation (see the implicit-engine roadmap entry), held bit-equal to it on the same binary. `EllipticalArcTo` is SVG's `A rx ry rot largeArc sweep` verbatim, out-of-range rule included (F.6.6: semi-axes too small to span the chord scale by the common factor that just reaches, so the ASPECT and rotation survive and no caller has to solve for the minimum ellipse first); it is solved by mapping the ellipse to the UNIT CIRCLE, where the problem is the circular one, and mapping back — legal because the map is linear, so it takes centres to centres and the parameter across verbatim. **Landing it exposed a real tessellation defect**: `BRepTessellator` gave the `segmentsPerCircle` density only to `Circle3d`, so an ellipse — whose parameter is equally an angle — fell to `curveSamples`, and an elliptical prism measured **0.64% under πabh at "256 segments", the deficit of a 23-gon**. That is not a tolerance question but a wrong answer to the density the caller stated, and the same rule reaches the elliptical edges `SurfaceIntersection` already produces for an oblique plane through a cylinder. Fixed by asking `IsAngularlyParameterized` (circle OR ellipse) in both `SampleEdge` and `CurveParams`; the prism now matches the *discrete* truth `(n/2)ab·sin(2π/n)·h` as an IDENTITY, which is available in closed form precisely because nothing is flattened. In a constrained sketch an elliptical arc rides the CHORD SIMILARITY exactly as a bézier does (centre and both axes transported, polar parameters untouched because they are measured in the ellipse's own axis frame), and it carries no centre/radius variables, so tangency to one is not in the vocabulary yet; axis-touching full-turn revolves (vases) work in **all** representations — `RevolveFullTurn` drops on-axis stretches (they revolve to nothing) and treats their endpoints as poles with no junction edge, splitting pole-to-pole generators at their midpoint so every face keeps a boundary loop (the sphere case); full-turn+holes/single-closed-curve stay B-Rep-Impossible and partial axis-touching revolves are rejected at construction; **hole feature**: `Shape.Drill(HoleSpec, points, depth, plane)` — simple/counterbore/countersink tools are axis-touching revolved sketches subtracted per point (exact everywhere; the tool overshoots the surface so booleans never see coplanar faces, and the countersink cone continues its slope so the surface diameter stays exact), with `StandardHoles` supplying metric ISO 273 clearance / DIN 974 cbore / ISO 10642 csk / coarse tap pilots / Tappex Trisert pilots (Trisert table flagged verify-against-datasheet). **Drill points**: `HoleSpec.WithTipAngle` gives a blind hole the cone a real twist drill leaves (`StandardHoles.TwistDrillPoint` 118°, `SplitDrillPoint` 135°) — exact everywhere, since the tool stays ONE axis-touching revolve with the cone as the profile run down to the apex, and the tessellated result is an n-gon prism plus an n-gon pyramid matching the discrete truth as an identity at 32/64/128 segments. **Depth is to the SHOULDER** with the tip reaching `(d/2)/tan(θ/2)` further (ASME Y14.5 / ISO 129), which makes the feature strictly additive — the same depth removes the same cylinder either way, so adding a point can never silently shorten an existing hole; `TipLength` is the number to check against the far face, and the default stays flat. **Cross-plane hole validation**: tools on DIFFERENT placement planes are now checked too (the old comment claiming they "are not compared" sat above a test that was itself building two coaxial overlapping tools). Each tool is covered by bounding cylinders about its axis and a pair is cleared by either of two sufficient conditions — axis-SEGMENT distance exceeding the summed radii, or a separating axis (a finite solid cylinder is convex; support extent `|n·d|·halfLength + r·√(1−(n·d)²)`). Neither subsumes the other: segment distance settles skew and offset-parallel layouts, but two COLLINEAR tools bored towards each other have axis segments at zero radial distance however much web is left, so only the axial projection separates them. Refinement (only when the whole-tool bound is ambiguous) is slab-by-slab over `HoleSpec.ToolSilhouette` — the same source of truth `ToolProfile` is built from — **sound in the accept direction at any subdivision** and exact wherever a slab's radius is constant, so the residual error is a conservative refusal in a near-tangent band, which is what a boolean cannot survive anyway. Kernel work that enabled it: analytic plane⊥`RevolvedSurface` intersection circles (multi-crossing generator bisection, phase-aligned to u=0), `SplitBandByWrapCurve` generalized to full-turn `RevolvedSurface` bands (generator `CurveSegment` split; single-loop pole bands allowed) with **vCut refined geometrically** (projection error ~1e-7 shifts cone rings radially past weld tolerance — solve the generator's axial coordinate against the exact cut curve instead), and `ProbePoint` probing pole-bounded single-loop faces halfway to the pole without the upward-ray parity check (which cannot see a rim below the probe) — halfway from the loop's CLOSEST APPROACH to the pole, which is what makes it right for a cap another solid has CUT and equals the incumbent average on an uncut one (see the roadmap §4 entry). Note: CSG difference SDFs are correct-sign lower bounds near the subtracted tool's fictitious faces — don't assert exact distances there in tests. **The depth guard's coincidence test measures to the FACE's plane, not along the tool axis** (`ShapeCompiler.BottomLiesInFacePlane`, the one rule both `Drill` and `ThreadedHole` ask): two planes coincide iff they are parallel AND a point of one lies on the other, and `n̂·(origin − bottom)` is the same number for every in-plane point while `axis·(origin − bottom)` is not. Since `IsPlanar`'s origin is ARBITRARY — a box cap's is a CORNER — the old axial form was decided by the plate's SIZE inside the admitted 0.081° parallel band: on a 200×150 plate tilted 0.057°, the origin's 75-unit lateral offset leaks 75·sin θ = **0.075 model units** into the measurement, which both hides a genuinely coplanar bottom (the guard silently does not fire, and the failure resurfaces as "Directed edge appears twice" deep in tessellation) and refuses a blind hole with 0.075 of real floor under it. Same shape as the recorded trap that a face must be located by `Bounds().Center`, and the same arithmetic `CoplanarFaces.SamePlane` already uses one layer down — which is the point: **the kernel had the well-formed version of this test all along and the guard restated it instead of asking**. The companion angle stays at acos(1 − 1e-6) and now has a geometric reason rather than a deferral: past that band the tool's flat bottom CROSSES the face in a chord instead of lying in it, which is ordinary transversal boolean input, so widening would start refusing legal models. **And the coplanar-fusion tier does NOT retire the guard**, which was the evidence the backlog item was waiting for: `CoplanarFaces.For` collects only `IsPlanar` faces, while a drill tool is ONE axis-touching revolve whose flat bottom is a `RevolvedSurface` POLE CAP — so the tier structurally cannot see the pair a drill makes, however well it now fuses a flush cylinder. **B-Rep queries** (`BrepQueries` in BRep): `IsPlanar` (incl. extruded-line sides)/`IsCylindrical`/`IsLinear`/`IsCircular`/`Length`/`Bounds`/`IsConvex` + adjacency (`face.Edges()`, `solid.FacesOf(edge)`) + `PlanarFacesWithNormal` + **edge provenance** (`BrepQueries.Provenance(edge)`/`DescendsFrom(edge, tag)`/`solid.EdgesTagged(tag)` — the UNION of the ≤2 faces an edge borders, walked on demand from `edge.Uses → Loop.Face` with no new store, settling the topological-naming note's "both? either?" as EITHER because "the edges of the boss" wants the base rim an intersection would drop) + **`BrepBoolean.Section(a, b)`** (Interop; OCCT `BRepAlgoAPI_Section` — the curve-only intersection wire, the boolean's per-face-pair loop with each curve clipped to the region inside BOTH trims (`ClipToBothTrims`, the symmetric twin of the asymmetric `ClipToFace`) and RETURNED rather than split, inputs not consumed; a drilled-through plate sections to two exact circles of the closed-form total length, analytic pairs exact and tracer pairs sampling-resolution), plus **`BrepSelection`** — the build123d `sort_by`/`group_by`/`filter_by` capability as LINQ extension methods (string selectors deliberately not imitated): `SortAlong`/`Extreme`/`Highest`/`Lowest` (a planar face ranks by its exact plane offset ONLY when the direction is parallel to its normal — a plane's stored origin is an arbitrary in-plane point, so a box side wall grouped along Z must rank by its centre), `GroupAlong`/`GroupByCoplanar` (weld-tier; non-planar faces stay singletons — nothing silently dropped), `FilterBy(SurfaceKind)`/`Kind()` (revolved generators classified by SAMPLING five points, because the kernel spells circular generators three ways — `Circle3d`, `CurveSegment`, rational NURBS arc, the sphere/torus factories using the last — and a curve-type switch would classify identical geometry differently), `GroupByRadius`/`NthByRadius(n)` (negative = from the largest; out-of-range names the radii found), and `Area` (planar exact via the boundary integral with closed-form arc terms; curved faces midpoint-quadrature over the pulled trim loops, ~1–2% — ordering-grade for `LargestByArea`, mass-property grade stays `BrepMassProperties`' job); every query has a serializable GeometryRef spelling (`FaceSetRef.OfKind/NthByRadius/GroupAlong`, `FaceRef.LargestByArea/Largest`, `EdgeSetRef.NthByRadius`; docs page `selection.md`) — the selection vocabulary for **rim features**: `Shape.Chamfer(setback, faceSelector)`/`Fillet(radius, faceSelector)` → `Filleting.ChamferRim/FilletRim` topology surgery (chamfer: straight rims miter with planar trapezoid strips + exact cone bands on circular rims; fillet: tangent-continuous line+arc rims via quarter-cylinder/torus-segment bands sharing junction arcs; **sharp corners miter on exact bicylinder ellipses** — at a rim corner only TWO of the three edges are blended, so the two side faces keep their shared sharp edge and the two equal-radius quarter-cylinders meet in a crease; a sphere there is tangent to all three planes at single POINTS, so the cross-section would jump from round to sharp at the tangency plane and not close. The branch is read straight off the two points the surgery already computed — `Ellipse3d(centre = top − up·r, up·r, bottom − centre)` over [0, π/2], with the circular junction arc of tangent-continuous rims being literally the |bottom − centre| == r specialization (kept on its own rational-arc path so existing rims stay bit-identical). Convex AND reflex corners are exact — a reflex band reaches past its edge's end to meet the miter, an extension that is exactly zero in the convex case so the direction vector stays bit-identical. Filleted prisms are analytic with SIGNED turns: V = A₀h − P₀r²(1−π/4) + (Σ tan(θᵢ/2))r³(5/3−π/2), matched to ~1e-4 relative incl. an L-shape; sharp corners at ARC rim edges stay rejected (torus∩cylinder is not a conic), as does a radius whose mitered offsets cross — naming the edge it consumed. **Edge sets**: `Filleting.FilletEdges`/`ChamferEdges`/`RimFacesFor` + `BrepQueries.RimEdges(face)`/`ConvexEdges(solid)` + `Shape.FilletEdges`/`ChamferEdges` accept any selection covering complete planar face rims; partial runs are rejected BEFORE any surgery (rim surgery rewrites loops in place, so a late failure would leave a half-edited solid). `ChamferRimAtAngle`/`Shape.ChamferAtAngle` take setback-in-face + angle-from-face, exact to 1e-9. **Whole-solid rounding**: `Filleting.FilletAllEdges(solid, radius)` is the exact morphological **opening** (K ⊖ B_r) ⊕ B_r — boolean-free, nothing to seal: each face keeps its own plane with a shrunk boundary, each edge becomes a cylindrical band about the ERODED edge line, each vertex a spherical patch on the eroded vertex bounded by great-circle arcs (a box → 26 faces / 48 edges / 24 vertices, every face full-domain so it all tessellates on the natural grid; volume converges quadratically onto Steiner's V = V₀ + A₀r + (r²/2)Σℓθ + (4π/3)r³, deficit ratio exactly 4.0 per halving). Concave edges, valence≠3 vertices and corners with no incident face perpendicular to the other two are refused — that last restriction is what keeps the patch an exact surface of revolution (the spherical triangle becomes a lune closed by an equatorial great circle), so boxes, convex prisms and sheared boxes keep the exact lune; GENERAL trihedral corners (a tetrahedron's, a drafted block's) now build a TRIMMED spherical-triangle patch instead — one face normal becomes the pole axis, the two arcs meeting its tangency are exact MERIDIANS on the u domain boundaries, and only the diagonal arc trims the interior; a dedicated trimmed tier (TriangulatePoleGrid) meshes it as a structured column grid EXCLUDED from midpoint refinement, because the refiner's flat uv metric overstates u chords near a pole without bound (measured: apex cascades folded 52 facets at 16/8 and half-step slivers broke the 48/24 floor); Steiner on a regular tetrahedron (dimensions of the ERODED body) matches to 1e-4 with quadratic convergence. **The load-bearing detail**: every corner arc is a `CurveSegment` over `Circle3d`, NEVER a rational NURBS arc — a corner patch is a revolve sampled at even ANGLES, so an arc parameterized any other way samples to different points and the patch stops welding to its band. **Variable-RADIUS fillets landed** (`FilletRim`/`FilletEdges` law overloads + `Shape.Fillet(radiusAt, faces)` + `VariableFilletRimFeature`): along a straight run the band is the RULED skin between its two end quarter arcs, and because those are equal-weight rational conics on one frame, lerping their points equals lerping their homogeneous control points (the denominators cancel), so every intermediate section is a TRUE circle of the interpolated radius, G1 with both neighbours. The band's top and bottom boundaries must be RAILS on the band (`LoftRailCurve`) rather than free-standing lines, or the loft's v grid samples the boundary at a density the straight edge polyline does not and the face T-junctions against its neighbours. What is refused is the CORNER: two variable-radius bands are cones (the family of circles with linear centre and linear radius IS a cone, apex where the radius vanishes) that do not circumscribe a common sphere, so they meet in a quartic with no conic miter — while a CONSTANT law across a sharp corner makes them equal-radius cylinders again, which DO share an inscribed sphere, so the exact bicylinder ellipse is back and the refusal is about the law rather than about sharp corners. Variable-SETBACK chamfers landed earlier: a corner law linear along each edge — a linearly varying perpendicular inset of a straight edge is still a straight LINE and a constant top:side ratio keeps each strip's four corners coplanar (s0*d1 = d0*s1), so strips stay exact planes and miters exact intersections; arcs and full circles demand a locally constant law, since a circle offset by a varying amount is a spiral. Partial edge RUNS landed too: a contiguous selection stopping mid-rim blends with exact SETBACK terminations (the band cross-section is already planar), grouped and validated all-or-nothing before surgery; three blended edges meeting at a vertex still refuses toward FilletAllEdges. **Variable laws now reach partial runs** (`Filleting.FilletEdges`/`ChamferEdges` law overloads resolve rims AND runs; `OpenRun` takes the law with the full-rim machinery, and a termination is exact at ANY law value — the end cross-section is a planar quarter arc of whatever radius the law gives at the stop vertex, so a linear-law run removes exactly (1−π/4)·L·(r₀²+r₀r₁+r₁²)/3, corpus member "variable fillet run" converging at ratio exactly 4.0; the sharp-corner refusal gains the run-specific way out of stopping BEFORE the corner; the `Shape` edge-set LAW overloads still resolve complete rims only — filed). Landing it taught the RAIL DENSITY twin of the rails-not-lines rule: a ruled band's `LoftRailCurve` sampled at 25 near-collinear points forces the neighbouring planar face's ear clipping into sliver ears (18 of 23 facets degenerate, non-manifold at 128/96), so `LoftedSurface.IsAffineInV` (degree-1, TWO sections — v-chords lie exactly on the surface) now collapses the natural grid's v to the two section rows and samples rails as the exact 2-point segments they are, one condition on both sides. That collapse exposed a √ε stall in the loft ALIGNER (golden-section seam shift left a 1.09e-8 phantom twist that 24 interior rows had been averaging away — the recorded distance-minimization trap); a Newton polish on dJ/ds = 0 with exact curve derivatives lands the true optimum and two aligned circles take the wrapper-free exact-zero path, restoring the loft-vs-cone tessellation identity to nine decimals). Surgery gotchas learned: derive rim circles from edge samples (`ActualCircle` — `Underlying` circles of translated extrusion tops sit at the base), build all new rim edges in the top face's traversal direction (fixes every sense), TRIM domain-driven neighbor surfaces (extruded/revolved grids ignore loops — shortened bands must get shortened surfaces), and don't double-flip arc spans already measured from traversal-ordered points. **OCCT-parity solid features in the Shape graph**: `Shape.Loft(sections, style)` (sketches placed by `SketchPlane`s or raw `Profile`s; `LoftShape` is a graph LEAF whose inputs are profiles, transform baked into the section curves — B-Rep-Native rigid + uniform scale, sheared placements Impossible because chord-length parameterization and least-twist alignment are metric; section-count mismatches and holed sections fail at the CALL), `Shape.LoftAlong(section, spine, count, scale, twist)` (the evolution-law pipe shell: stations in the SAME `SweptSurface` RMF frames `Sweep` uses, generated profiles feeding `Loft` unchanged), `Shape.Draft(angleDegrees, neutralOrigin, pull, faces?)` (per-face angles by chaining — exact and composable), `Shape.Shell(t, openings)` (exact INWARD hollow via `Shelling.Shell` — deliberately a separate overload from the SDF `Shell(t)` onion `|d| − t/2`, whose skin straddles the surface: two calls, two geometries, never one call with representation-dependent walls; the SDF shell's B-Rep-Impossible message names the exact overload), and `Shape.RoundEdges(r)` (`Filleting.FilletAllEdges`; radius scales with uniform placement). All four bridge implicit through the exact tessellation and refuse what the kernel refuses BY NAME at lowering; docs page `loft-draft-shell.md`. **Direct editing** joins them for the body that has no history to edit at all: `Shape.OffsetFaces(distance, faces)` / `MoveFaces(translation, faces)` / `DeleteFaces(faces)` over the same `FaceSetRef` vocabulary (→ `DirectEdit` in BRep), B-Rep-**Native** under any similarity including MIRRORS — the distance rides the uniform scale, a move takes the translation's LINEAR image (right for a reflection too, because the operation reduces to v·n̂ and an orthogonal map preserves dot products), and a deletion is purely topological so nothing has to commute with it; Impossible under a shear by name. Docs `examples/direct-editing.md`. One finding from the wiring: a typed `FaceSetRef` refuses BEFORE the compiler's own empty-selection gate and names the PARAMETER ("faces: expected at least one cylindrical face, found none"), which is the better message and why the typed overloads pass the parameter's own name down — both paths pinned. **Sheet metal v1** (`Modeling/SheetMetal.cs` + `SheetMetalFeatures.cs`, `BRep/SheetMetalSurgery.cs`; docs `examples/sheet-metal.md`): a sheet part is a flat blank plus a list of bends, and **the declaration IS the model** — `SheetMetalBody` = base sketch + `SheetMetalSpec` + an ordered tree of `EdgeFlange`s, from which BOTH the folded solid and the flat pattern are derived, so there is no second description to keep in step. **One bend model in one place**: `BA = θ(R + K·T)` (bend allowance = the neutral axis's arc length) and `OSSB = (R+T)·tan(θ/2)` (outside setback), both static on `SheetMetalSpec`, with bend deduction `2·OSSB − BA` derived rather than modelled again; K is stored per flange over a per-body default, with a `SheetMaterials` table (0.33 soft aluminium … 0.50 coined) transcribed and flagged verify-against-datasheet like `StandardHoles`' Trisert rows. **The K-factor is deliberately absent from the GEOMETRY** — `SheetBendSection` carries thickness, radius and angle and no K, because K locates the neutral axis, which decides developed LENGTH and nothing about the folded shape; that separation is what makes the volume comparison a real test rather than a tautology. **Bends are topology surgery, never booleans** (the `Filleting` rim doctrine): a bend meets both the parent sheet and the flange wall TANGENTIALLY — cylinder and plane share a tangent plane along the whole bend line — which is exactly the coincident/tangent input the v1 boolean refuses, and there is nothing to compute anyway since every face is a closed form, so two exact full-domain `ExtrudedSurface` arc bands plus three planes are welded straight into the parent's loops. **A flange's two ENDS are independent, which is v2's whole surgery change**: each end is either FLUSH with the wall's corner — where the cross-section chain REPLACES the wall's end edge in the neighbouring loop — or INSET, where the rim is split (`TopologyEditor.SplitEdge` patches every using loop), the leftover wall becomes a stub and the same chain closes against a new vertical edge as a cap. v1 refused one of each as "the corner case in disguise" and **it is not one** — the two rules touch no common coedge — so a flange running to one end of a plate is simply one of each, and the code stopped branching on a `FullWidth` flag (verified in BOTH orientations, since which end is the surgery's Q0 depends on the bend axis, and by closed form: against a full-width flange the half-inset one is short by exactly `(edge − width)·(allowance + wall)·T`). The flush path's non-obvious duty is **re-surfacing the neighbour as a `PlaneSurface`** — the widened loop reaches out past the flange's tip and would escape a domain-driven extruded surface's parameter rectangle (the trim-the-neighbour rule running the other way; a plane triangulates from its loops and has nothing to escape). **Every refusal fires before a single coedge moves**, the rim-surgery rule, and getting there caught the same defect twice from one cause — a precondition written next to its CONSUMER rather than next to the gate: the neighbour-perpendicularity test sat inside the splice, which rewrites the Q0 neighbour's loop before it ever looks at Q1's, and the uniform-thickness test sat after the rim splits had already run. **`Unfold()` is bookkeeping, not geometry re-derivation**: every node carries a rigid 2D frame in the blank and a 3D frame on its own top face for the SAME local coordinates, and the blank is the base sketch with a detour SPLICED into each flanged segment — no 2D boolean either, since the flange rectangle is exactly edge-adjacent; base-sketch holes carry through untouched because the flat pattern's coordinates ARE the base sketch's. `FlatPattern` exports via `ToDxf()` (blank on CUT, both bend-zone tangent lines on BEND with the CENTER line type) or `ToDrawing()` for SVG. **The oracle is an exact DISCREPANCY, not an approximate agreement**: a bend's folded material is `θ·T·(R + T/2)` per unit width while the blank spends `θ·T·(R + K·T)`, so the two volumes are IDENTICAL at K = 0.5 and differ by `Σ width·θ·T²·(0.5 − K)` everywhere else — measured on an 80×50×1.5 plate with one 25 mm flange at R = 2: predicted 10.6029 against 10.6029 for K = 0.44, residual 4.9e-6 on a 7.94e3 volume (6.2e-10 relative, i.e. the grade of the tessellate-then-Richardson mass properties themselves, and the SAME residual at every K since the blank's volume is exact). A blanket "the volumes agree" would have passed a model with K wired to a constant. Three conventions carry every dimension and each was a real choice: `Length` is measured from the OUTER VIRTUAL SHARP (the drawing dimension); the bend is placed **bend-outside**, its tangent line being the named edge so the material continues outboard and the parent's flat region is exactly the outline drawn (the "material inside" alternative would make the base sketch mean less than the blank's base region); and **a flange folds toward the face its edge is quoted on**, that face becoming the inside of the bend, which makes Up/Down mean one thing all the way down a chain. **Bend reliefs are a change to the BLANK, not a cut in the folded body — which is why they needed no surgery at all** (`BendRelief`, rectangular or obround, defaulting to one thickness wide by `R + T` deep): a relief notches `SheetMetalBody.BaseOutline`, the base sketch with every relief cut into it, which is what the sheet is EXTRUDED from and what the flat pattern STARTS from, so one declaration produces both views exactly as an edge flange does. It also makes the geometry simpler rather than harder: between its two notches a relieved flange runs the full width of the shortened wall, so it arrives at `SheetMetalSurgery` as an ordinary FLUSH flange whose neighbours are the notches' own walls. With no relief declared `BaseOutline` **is** `BaseSketch` by reference, so nothing that already worked can move; the notch curves are emitted through the SAME frame-mapped code the flat pattern uses, sound because the base node's flat frame is the identity ("the blank's coordinates ARE the base sketch's", restated as an implementation); a relief is cut at each INSET end (a flush end has no parent material beside it) and only on the BASE flange's edges, since a flange's wall is built as a plain rectangle rather than from a sketch. **A notch is a DETOUR in a loop, so one that runs out of its parent is SILENT** — measured on an 80×50 plate, a 200-deep relief leaves a self-intersecting blank whose SIGNED area still reads exactly base-minus-notches (2800 = 4000 − 2·600, a Green's integral over a bowtie not being the enclosed area) and whose extrusion still passes `Validate` with 18 faces — so every point of the notch below the surface must lie strictly inside the parent's own region (`SketchRegion.SignedDistance`), which also catches a notch reaching into a HOLE; the guard claims no more than it proves, since a notch passing clean through a hole and out into material the far side has its own corners inside and is not caught. **The oracle extends rather than being replaced**: a relief removes the same material from both views, so the folded-versus-flat discrepancy is UNCHANGED and the relief contributes exactly nothing — the assertion that catches a notch reaching only one view, which an approximate agreement waves through — beside two exact statements (the blank's area falls by exactly the notch closed form, which is also what catches an inward arc swept the wrong way since a mis-swept dome ADDS area; the folded volume falls by exactly `area × thickness` per notch). `BaseFlangeFeature`/`EdgeFlangeFeature` put it in the feature history with the bend line named by an `EdgeSetRef` re-resolved per regeneration and mapped back into the tree by `SheetMetalBody.SiteFor` — which now also resolves a PIECE of a site's edge as that site, since a relief notches the blank and a selector can then only pick one of the pieces (exact match tried first, so nothing that resolved before is answered differently). **The optional-parameter rule reaches its enum form**: `Relief` is a `SheetReliefOption` (`None`/`Rectangular`/`Obround`) rather than a nullable `SheetReliefKind`, because `ParamEditors.KindFor` gives an enum a DROPDOWN whose rows are the type's own members, so it can no more say "unset" than a slider can — the same argument that keeps `KFactor`'s sentinel 0 — with the second spelling's drift risk paid the usual way, a test driving EVERY member through a real regeneration and reading the kind back off the flange tree by NAME. `FlatPattern.BendTable()` is the press brake's setup sheet (bend line length, angle, direction, radius, allowance), every column read off the same `FlatBendLine` records the drawing's bend zones are drawn from. **v3 closes the corner and the rest of the vocabulary.** **Closed corners** (`SheetMetalBody.WithCorner` → `SheetMetalSurgery.AddCornerFlanges`) are ONE operation over two flanges, and the miter is a CLOSED FORM rather than the surface–surface re-intersection the backlog costed it as: two bends of the same radius quoted on the same face have axes that MEET over the sheet's corner, and two equal-radius cylinders with intersecting axes meet in two ELLIPSES — the fact `Filleting`'s sharp-corner miters already stand on — so each band's cut is an exact `Ellipse3d` (centre = the shared axis point, one semi-axis the inward radial, the other reaching where the two OUTWARD-OFFSET bend lines cross, itself the closed-form bisector `R(ŵ_A+ŵ_B)/(1+ŵ_A·ŵ_B)`). **The two flanges' cut chains are the SAME curves** — the configuration is symmetric under reflection in the miter plane, which swaps them — so they are built once and used by one face of each: nothing lies in the miter plane, which is what "closed" means (welded, not butted across a gap), and there is no boolean, no new surface type and no tolerance in the corner at all. It is one DECLARATION for a topological reason: a full-width flange consumes its wall and splices into the faces at BOTH ends, so a second flange declared afterwards finds a wall that is no longer four-sided — exactly the refusal this replaces — and locating both before either is built is the whole fix, which also lets the sheet's own corner edge fall away once both walls are consumed. **The oracle is an exact DISCREPANCY again**: mitring makes each flange run to the miter plane rather than stopping at the corner, so the folded body GAINS that flange's cross-section's first moment about the corner line — `((R+T)³−R³)/3` from the annular sector plus `T·L·(R+T/2)` from the wall — while the BLANK is untouched, which is what "an unrelieved corner shares material" means (predicted 10713.242157, measured 10713.244229 = 1.9e-7 relative, the mass-properties grade; exact at 70°/90°/110°). Two build lessons: a mitred band's PARAMETER RECTANGLE must be lengthened past the span (the trim-the-domain-driven-surface rule running the other way), and the reach comes from the arc's own SEMI-AXIS rather than its endpoints, since past a square bend the ellipse's furthest point is at t = 90°, strictly inside the trimmed range. **Hems, jogs and curls** (`WithHem`/`WithJog`/`WithCurl`) are two or more ORDINARY bends — none needed new geometry, and what was missing was the declaration plus the arithmetic turning a shop dimension into flange lengths: a hem is two bends the SAME way rather than one 180° fold (which has no geometry here, its setback diverging, and whose flat return leg is the coincident boundary the tangency argument refuses), its gap is `2R + L` so `2R` or less is a CLOSED hem and refuses by name; a jog is two equal and OPPOSITE bends with `L = (offset − (2R+T)(1−cos θ))/sin θ`; a curl is a chain of hits and is honestly POLYGONAL, said in the API rather than approximated away. **Flange CUTOUTS** (`EdgeFlange.Cutouts`, closed sketches in the flange's own local coordinates) reach both views from one declaration, and the backlog's "one change, two features" was half wrong: a HOLE needs no wall-outline generalisation at all — it is additive surgery on the wall's two planar faces plus one band face per profile segment (the loop structure `BuildSweptSolid`'s verbatim, since a hole through a flat plate IS a swept solid's hole), with which of the two faces it lies on DERIVED from its own plane normal; only a change to the wall's OUTLINE needs the generalisation, which stays open. `Sketch.Placed(origin, xAxis)` is the new rigid 2D placement it needed (an arc keeps its radius and signed sweep, an ellipse both semi-axis vectors, a Bézier its control polygon), rigid rather than affine because an affine map turns an arc into an ellipse and would put a re-fit inside a path whose claim is that nothing is fitted. **Mirrored placements are Native by being re-DECLARED rather than re-placed**: `MirroredInPlane` rebuilds the tree the other way round and the compiler places it on a PROPER frame (`P = P′·FlipX`, so the reflection is spent once — half in the declaration, half in the frame), with three remaps each forced by the reflection's arithmetic (`Sketch.Mirrored` restores winding by REVERSING the loop, so segment `i` of `n` lands at `n−1−i`; a span `[s, s+w]` of an edge of length L becomes `[L−s−w, L−s]`; a cutout mirrors in its flange's local x and slides back by the width) — **FlipX rather than FlipZ**, so the sheet's own +Z, the face every bend line is quoted on, never moves and `SheetBendDirection` keeps meaning one thing; verified by vertex SETS through the reflection (a volume comparison passes a tree flipped the wrong way round) plus `Mirror(Mirror(x))` being the original. **Multi-body sheets and welded assemblies needed no sheet-metal work**, which is the finding: a sheet part is ONE flange tree and ONE blank by design, and a weldment is several PARTS in an `Assembly` the BOM already counts and mates already position — what was missing was the seam that cuts them all (`SheetMetalFeatures.UnfoldAll`, read by both the `--flat` CLI verb and the viewer's Flat button so neither grows a second opinion) plus the boundary stated: a BOOLEAN of two sheet solids is a solid, not a sheet part, since a union node carries no flange tree. **`--flat out.dxf|.svg|.txt`** is its own verb rather than an `--export` extension (a drawing sheet, a section and a flat pattern are three different 2D answers to "this scene as DXF"), one file per sheet part. **And a curved bend line is refused by a THEOREM rather than by a gap** — the backlog's "sweeps a developable band, needs a new swept surface" was wrong in the way that invites building it: folding along a curve is NOT AN ISOMETRY of the sheet, so no flat blank produces it (a circular bend line's band is a torus segment, whose Gaussian curvature is non-zero everywhere a flat sheet's is zero), the material would have to stretch, and the surface would have bought a solid whose flat pattern was a lie. Measured rather than asserted: the same bend run round a circle has a Pappus area ~3% off the straight one's, and if they agreed the refusal would be wrong. **v4 closes the last three, and each turned out to hinge on a fact the earlier refusal had half-stated.** **A cutout may now CROSS the bend line, and the exact tier is the RECTANGLE ALIGNED with it** — a complete answer rather than a budget, and the filed plan (a new curve type, or a sampled edge) was not needed at all: bending preserves the sheet's intrinsic geometry, which is the SAME isometry argument the curved-bend-line refusal stands on read the other way round, so a straight cut ALONG the bend line stays straight (a ruling of the cylinder), one ACROSS it becomes a circular ARC, and the wall each sweeps through the thickness is a PLANE — while a cut at any other angle wraps to a HELIX and an arc in the blank wraps to nothing with a closed form. So no new curve type, no new surface type, no trimmed face and no boolean: `BuildBend` splits both bend bands into THREE (the two full-height stretches either side plus a short one under the slot, whose arc simply sweeps less — all ordinary full-domain `ExtrudedSurface` bands), splits the rims they weld to, and NOTCHES both wall faces (a notch in the outer loop rather than a hole, since the slot reaches their own boundary). **What it costs is the K-factor's independence from the FOLDED shape, and it cannot be otherwise**: a cutout is declared FLAT — punched in the blank and then bent, which is exactly why holes near a bend deform — and the only map from blank to band is the neutral-axis map that K parameterizes, so the angle a slot bottoms out at is resolved where K already lives and `SheetBendSection` still carries none (the geometry stays pure; the ARGUMENT is what K decided). The identity is then the bend's own formula restricted to the angle the slot takes away — blank `w·(y₁−y₀)·T`, folded `w·(y₁·T + (θ−φ₀)·T·(R+T/2))`, and since `−y₀ = (θ−φ₀)(R+K·T)` the difference is exactly `w·(θ−φ₀)·T²·(0.5−K)`, **a SLICE of the very discrepancy the K-factor owns** (measured against the identical un-slotted flange at three K values; residual one tier coarser than a plain flange's, 6.9e-8 of the folded volume against 6.2e-10, because the split arcs are sampled independently of the band's own grid, converging −3.0e-3/+5.4e-4/−5.3e-5/+1.8e-5 at 32/64/128/256). Two orientation errors were made and caught by `Validate`, both the same mistake — OUT of the material at a slot wall points INTO the slot, so the low wall's normal is +axis not −axis and the top wall's is −u not +u, with "an edge's two uses must have opposite sense" as the tell each time. **A relief on a flange's TIP** had its premise right and its conclusion wrong: "a relief notches its parent's OUTLINE, and a flange's wall is built as a plain rectangle rather than from a sketch" is true, and having no outline to notch does not mean there is nothing to do — the notches travel with the PARENT's own construction instead (`SheetTipNotch`), and a parent is built before its children, which is precisely why it has to know about their reliefs. `BuildTip` emits one four-sided planar piece per surviving stretch plus a band per notch segment, so between its notches the child runs the full width of a tip face that is still four-sided, planar and square to the bend line and arrives as an ordinary FLUSH flange — the base-edge relief's own trick one level in, verified through a grandchild flange, which is the only thing that asks. A notch position is stated as its two points on the flange's own tangent line and read as their component ALONG the span, because that is the one thing a coordinate convention could get wrong (`Order` swaps q0/q1 for an Up flange and not for a Down one, since `a = Outward × Inside` is −T in one case and +T in the other), so it is MEASURED rather than derived; with no notches the same calls in the same order build the same edge pair and the same face. **LOUVRES** (`SheetLouvre`/`WithLouvre`) needed a new declaration and NO new surgery: an edge flange's bend line is an EDGE of the sheet and its material grows outboard of the blank, while a louvre's is interior to a face and its material comes out of the sheet — but once the parent gives up the tab's footprint, that bend line is an ordinary edge of an ordinary four-sided planar wall, exactly the flush case `AddEdgeFlange` already builds. **A LANCE has a WIDTH, and that is a theorem rather than a modelling choice**: at zero width the tab's own side face is coincident with the wall of the opening it came out of, everywhere the bend band still lies inside that opening — two coplanar faces with opposite normals touching over an area, which is not a manifold boundary (measured: the mesh came back OPEN with a 4-edge boundary loop on exactly those faces), so `Clearance` is strictly positive and zero refuses by name. **What each view loses differs on purpose and THAT DIFFERENCE IS THE LANCE**: the blank keeps the tab and loses only the U-shaped KERF (a lance separates material rather than removing it) while `FoldedOutline` loses the whole opening, because the tab has left the plane and comes back as its own flange — and since the kerf leaves both views identically (the bend-relief rule) the volume identity is UNCHANGED and INDEPENDENT of the clearance, contributing one more `W·θ·T²·(0.5−K)` term with the lance itself contributing exactly zero (asserted as the two-clearance equality, and measured 1e-12…3e-10 relative over K × clearance × angle). One further refusal is closed form and worth keeping because it inverts the intuition: **a shallow tab reaches FURTHER out than the flat material it came from**, by the thickness the bend swings it through, peaking near `atan(T(1−K)/L)` at about `T²(1−K)²/2L` — a thick-sheet, short-lance, tight-kerf failure, so the fixture states its own kerf and is a 0.048 mm near miss rather than an absurdity. Landing the louvre also **generalised `RequireSquareNeighbour`**, a "a tier's gate should BE its correctness condition" case: it asked for a SIGNED normal, which is a proxy — the sign says convex-or-reflex and every corner of a HOLE is reflex — where the correctness condition is perpendicularity alone, since the tab's cross-section rises out of the neighbour's plane either way; and the companion question answers itself, because which way that neighbour's loop walks is fixed by the WALL's own orientation, identical in both cases, so the chain direction needed no change at all and edge flanges gained reflex corners for free. Still refused with reasons rather than deferrals: a crossing cutout that is not an aligned rectangle, or that runs THROUGH the bend into the parent (it would notch the parent's own faces) or floats wholly inside the band (it would leave the band a trimmed face with a hole in it), a crossing slot on a MITRED flange (a corner band already reaches past its span and the two edits want the same parameter rectangle), two flanges sharing a stretch of edge, and flanges on a flange's SIDE edges; **tear reliefs and spring-back are documented ABSENCES rather than refusals** (both belong to the press — a tear relief is what happens when no relief is cut). What each version removed from that list says what a "corner in disguise" really was: a flange flush at one end only was never one, while a second flange on a wall an earlier flange reshaped genuinely is — and v3's answer to THAT is not a better check but a different granularity, the pair declared and located together. Two filed predictions were also **wrong in instructive ways**: reliefs were filed as "pocket subtractions, mostly plumbing — but they change the FLAT pattern too, which is where the design work is", and both halves invert (no pocket and no boolean, and the flat pattern was the EASY half precisely because the blank is what both views derive from). **Frames & weldments v1** (`Frames.cs`; docs `examples/frames.md`; design.md §6b): `Weldment.Build/Path` puts straight members on a skeleton of runs — `FrameProfile` is a designation + exact section `Sketch` (flat/SHS/RHS/equal-angle/channel/round-tube factories, hollow = outer `WithHole` inner so wall thickness is exact, plus a small ⚠-verify-against-datasheet `StandardSections` catalogue; the run line passes through the sketch ORIGIN, roll rule stated on `WeldmentOptions.Up` with a +Z/+Y/+X fallback), each member is one `Shape.Extrude`, and two-member joints at shared endpoints (weld tier) are trimmed by the **exact bisector plane, whose normal is `a − b` for the unit leave-directions — no division anywhere, so the recorded miter-apex trap is avoided structurally** — built from ONE canonical joint point so both cut faces lie in the bit-identical plane. **Every joint cut is a transversal boolean by construction** (the Drill overshoot doctrine): the member extrudes overlong by the plane's exact reach across its own section (an affine functional whose extremes are closed-form per line/arc outline segment) and a box tool whose base face lies exactly ON the plane subtracts the stub, so the cut curves are all analytic — plane∩plane on polygonal sections, the plane∩cylinder ellipse on mitred tube, verified to CONVERGE on the closed form (−4.99e-3/−1.41e-3/−2.64e-4/+1.31e-5 at 32/64/128/256 segments) where a tracer polyline would be a fixed floor. **The volume oracle is the prism-cut identity** — V = A·(axial distance between the end planes' crossings of the CENTROID fiber), exact for any section — so mitred members of centred profiles measure exactly A·L (1e-9 relative, planar identity), a closed SHS picture frame exactly A·perimeter with the mass closed form through `Materials`, and the heel-datumed angle profile its rational closed form (11712 exactly), with the entry's rectangular wedge h·w²/2·tanβ asserted as the special case. **One part per member, deliberately** ("the same member" would be a tolerance judgement over cut planes in local frames differing by rotation round-off): identical members share the NAME `designation x cut length`, which is `Bom.ByItem()`'s documented rollup key, and the cut length rides **`Part.CutLength` → `BomLine.CutLength`** (the `Material` follow-the-part pattern — CUT column + total-stock footer gated on `HasCutLengths` so frameless scenes print byte-identically; write-only-when-stated in the document envelope, fixed point held). Butt joints trim the later run to the earlier member's flat wall; **coped (saddle) tube joints are refused with the tracer under-seeding reason on `FrameJointStyle.Cope`** (and butting onto a round tube reaches the same refusal); multi-member joints, T-joints, zero-angle joints, members consumed by their own end cuts and Bézier-outline joint trims all refuse by name. **Patterns**: `PatternLinear/PatternCircular` (balanced union trees) + **`LocationSet`** ("place this feature at these N poses" as ONE value — `Grid`/`Polar`/`PolarArc`/`Hex`/`Linear`/`At`, composed by `Translate`/`Rotate`/`+`, each location a point + in-plane rotation; one value feeds `Shape.Drill`, `Shape.Pattern(locations, plane?)` — which stamps an origin-modeled shape by conjugation through the plane frame, exactly `PatternCircular` of the pre-translated shape for a polar set — and `ComponentAssembly.Place`; serializable the GeometryRef way: `Descriptor` (`grid(3,2,10,8)`) parses back bit-for-bit since Parse re-runs the same deterministic constructors) + **`ExtrudeUntil`/`CutUntil`** (build123d's `until=NEXT/LAST`: probe rays from the sketch region's strict interior against the body mesh; the stop must be ONE plane ⊥ the extrusion (hits clustering within 1e-6×extent) — curved/slanted stops, overhanging profiles and tangent grazes refuse loudly naming the candidates; overshoots carry the Drill never-coplanar lessons (a Next boss reaches INTO the body, a Next cut into the void behind the first wall, a cut tool also clears the TOP when the plane sits on a face — and ray-triangle barycentric bounds are INCLUSIVE ±1e-9, because an edge crossing dropped by both triangles leaves a ray that enters and never exits); resolution eager per the `Bounds`/`Resized` policy, result = ordinary extrude+boolean nodes so Explain stays honest) + **`Packing`** (build123d's `pack`: deterministic shelf packer over `Shape.Silhouette` footprint bounds — overhangs get their room — gap honored to parts and plate edges, placements in input order, loud refusal naming the part that ran out of plate; **`PackOptions` now opens ROTATION and OUTLINE NESTING, both opt-in, with the default value the v1 contract and a default pack reproducing the committed v1 placements BIT FOR BIT** (the `ShadingStyle.Lit = 0` rule). **A quarter turn is exact** — `(x, y) → (−y, x)` is a sign swap, never a `cos` (the glTF Y-up-root rule), so a turned part's measured bounds are its transposed footprint to the last bit — and `PackRotation.Free` is **refused by name**, since a continuous orientation has no finite candidate set to search exhaustively nor to tie-break deterministically. **For box nesting a quarter turn is not a per-part decision**: it only TRANSPOSES the footprint, so the four poses collapse to two, and which is better depends on the neighbours because a shelf is as deep as its deepest member — the classical all-landscape rule is measurably wrong sometimes (40×10 bars on a 50-wide plate fit side by side upright and one-per-row landscape), so the packer runs the whole plate under BOTH global preferences and keeps the shallower, tie-breaking on used width then on landscape. **Outline nesting needed no new predicate**, which is what made it tractable: each silhouette is grown by HALF the gap through `Region2dOffset`, so "two grown outlines are disjoint" IS "these parts are at least `gap` apart" — symmetric, one existing operation, nothing to keep in step — and since dilation COMMUTES with a rotation the grow runs once per part and the four poses are exact turns of it (the grow is the expensive step at 9–200 ms on real silhouettes). **The search is a conservative RASTER and the cost measurement is what chose it**: one exact `Region2dBoolean.Intersection` on these regions costs 0.7–2.4 ms, three orders too much for a search visiting thousands of candidates, so each grown outline is rasterized once per pose into a bitmask and a candidate placement is a shifted word-wise AND against the plate's occupancy mask. Rasterization is CONSERVATIVE — a cell is set if the grown outline touches it at all — so an empty AND proves disjointness at any cell size and a coarse grid can only refuse a legal placement, never accept an illegal one (the cross-plane hole validation's sound-in-the-accept-direction rule); interior cells fill by even-odd parity at cell CENTRES, boundary cells by half-cell-step samples, and **the soundness argument fixes the block at 2×2 rather than 3×3** (a boundary point is within a quarter cell of a sample, so it lies in `[s ± h/2]` per axis, an interval of width h spanning at most two cells — the obvious 3×3 is sound too and dilates every mask by a whole cell on each side, measurably about one cell of clearance per part, exactly what a tight fit runs out of). A through hole is a hole in the silhouette, so small parts nest INSIDE a ring. **Three measured findings**: *bottom-left-first nests only when the plate is TIGHT* (with room to spare the lowest free spot is beside the previous part rather than inside its concavity, so a roomy plate reproduces row packing — six L brackets on a 140-wide plate measured 78.97 deep against the shelf packer's 77.00, purely quantization, while the same six on an 86-wide plate go 132.0 → 108.9); *a finer raster is NOT monotonically better* (it refuses fewer placements but changes which one the greedy search meets first — cells 4/2/1/0.5/0.25 give depths 120.0/106.0/112.0/109.5/108.2, a cost/quality knob rather than a convergence parameter); and *the quantization's failure is a REFUSAL* (four 40×10 bars spanning exactly 50 on a 50 mm plate have zero slack and no resolution reaches them, since a mask's width always rounds up to a whole cell — pinned by a test asserting the box packer takes it and outline nesting refuses). Headline numbers, six L brackets on a 96×260 plate at gap 3 (depth used, utilisation): none/box **249.0, 24%**; none/outline 154.2, 39%; quarter/box 144.0, 41%; quarter/outline **120.5, 49%** — and a ring with three discs goes 80 → 64 with every disc inside the bore; cost 68/83/233/435 ms for 4/8/16/24 parts. **The oracle is an exact clearance check rather than a picture**: every placed outline is grown by half the gap and every pair intersected through the same region machinery, which is the strong form (it sees a part nested in a bore that is too close to the wall, where a bounds test cannot), with `PackedArea`/`FootprintArea`/`UsedDepth`/`Utilisation` reported so two settings compare on one meaningful number and the fixture ASSERTED to have room to win before the comparison is believed; docs `packing.md`; **builder-style authoring prototyped and DECLINED** — design.md §6b records why: C# has no ambient context managers, a mutable local with `|=`/`-=` already IS the accumulation mode, and the named `Union/Subtract/Intersect` methods already fix the `|` vs `-` precedence trap; comparison committed as `BuilderPrototypeTests`) + `BrepBoolean` disjoint fast path (no curves → whole-body classification, multi-shell unions, clone-reversed swallowed Difference tools — in-place `ReverseFace` would duplicate edge uses) + face-bounds pre-filtering and identical-carrier curve dedupe (patterned faces sharing planes produced the same split curve per pair, breaking the tracer). **STL export**: `StlWriter` (binary, merged multi-part with transforms) + `--export .stl`. **3MF/AMF/OFF export**: `ThreeMfWriter` (OPC zip package over BCL `ZipArchive` + LINQ-to-XML — content types, `.rels`, `3D/3dmodel.model`) and `AmfWriter` (ISO/ASTM 52915 XML) both take `MeshExportPart` lists (mesh + transform + name + optional RGB): one named object per part, transform BAKED into vertices (the merged-STL flattening; mirror flips winding — 3MF requires outward facets), distinct colors deduped into one material table; `OffWriter` is `OffReader`'s twin (n-gons as-is, R-format doubles, round-trips exactly — remember the reader fan-triangulates, so read-back face count is Σ(deg−2)). All three wired into `--export` and the MCP `export` tool. **glTF 2.0 export**: `GltfWriter` (`.glb` binary + self-contained `.gltf` whose buffer rides inline as a data URI; dependency-free over `Utf8JsonWriter` plus a hand-written GLB container) + `GltfScene` in Viewer.Core, wired into `--export .glb/.gltf` and the MCP `export` tool. **It is the one mesh format here that keeps the assembly HIERARCHY** — glTF has real nodes, so a node per tab/sub-assembly/occurrence with **one mesh per distinct `Part`** however many times it is placed, the same "one product, N occurrences" structure `StepWriter.WriteAssembly` emits and the property the baking writers structurally cannot express. Colours become PBR metallic-roughness materials (deduped), translucent parts declare `alphaMode: BLEND`, and a `FieldDisplay` exports its result colours as `COLOR_0` — the SAME array the viewport uploads, since both come from the new `FieldRendering.SourceColors`, so an FEA plot in a browser and one on screen cannot disagree. Four rules with reasons: **winding is NOT flipped under mirroring** (the spec makes the CONSUMER reverse it when a node's global transform has a negative determinant, so flipping here as well would double the correction and turn every mirrored instance inside out — the one place this differs from every other writer in the repo); the Y-up/millimetre→metre conversion is **ONE root node built from exact values** (`cos(-π/2)` is 6.1e-17 and a diffable file should not carry that), so part transforms below it stay verbatim and `GltfOptions { YUp = false, Scale = 1 }` writes model coordinates; a primitive carrying `COLOR_0` gets a **white** `baseColorFactor`, because glTF MULTIPLIES the two and a part colour left in would tint every field value by it; and the **deformation exaggeration deliberately does not travel** — it is a viewing parameter with nowhere to live in a glTF file, so a file carrying 50×-displaced geometry would be indistinguishable from a model that really is that shape. Testing lesson worth keeping: **the oracle for a binary geometry format is to decode the buffer and sum the signed tetrahedral volume through the node transforms** — one assertion catches a wrong matrix convention (column- vs row-major), a wrong winding, a wrong accessor stride and a wrong unit scale at once, where a structural validator passes all four happily; the structural checks (chunk padding fill bytes, accessor alignment, POSITION min/max actually bounding the data, the node graph being a forest) are the second half, not the first. **Parametric features** (`Features.cs`/`FeatureHistory.cs`/`StandardFeatures.cs`): FeatureScript-in-C# — `Feature` classes with `[Param]` properties (reflection metadata cached per type; init-only setters writable via reflection for JSON overrides), `FeatureContext` (Body + lazily lowered B-Rep for selectors + `TopPlane`), `FeatureHistory.Regenerate` (prefix caching keyed by instance-identity+parameter snapshot — a fresh instance always re-runs, which safely covers non-`[Param]` inputs like sketches/hole specs/selectors; validation-first; failure keeps the last good prefix and skips the rest; suppression; `SaveParameters`/`LoadParameters` JSON). **Feature registry + whole-history persistence** (`FeatureRegistry.cs`): `FeatureRegistry` lists every feature type with instance-free `[Param]` metadata and an honest `CanCreate`/`Reason` pair (the UI-insertion catalogue), `TryCreate` builds one from data, and `FeatureHistory.SaveHistory`/`LoadHistory` round-trip the WHOLE history — type, name, suppression, `[Param]` values (through the SAME `SerializeValue`/`ApplyParameters` seams as `SaveParameters`, so the two files cannot drift) plus **constructor inputs** via the `Feature.SaveInputs` virtual: holes serialize their `HoleSpec` (kind + factory args + tip angle) and points, and sketch extrude/revolve serialize the `Sketch` EXACTLY through the public `Curve2d` vocabulary (`ToCurves`/`FromCurves` — nothing flattened), so the standard vocabulary short of code inputs round-trips with save→load→save a byte-identical fixed point and load→regenerate bit-identical volumes. **A placed catalogue component is data too**: `ComponentFeature` writes its `HardwareComponent` as a KIND plus the factory arguments that built it — never as its `Designation`, which is the wrong key and instructively so, since "ISO 4762 M6×20" carries the size and the length and says nothing about the clearance fit, the seating or whether the hex socket is modelled, so a designation-keyed reload comes back as a plausible DIFFERENT screw with the right name. It is the `HoleSpec` pattern verbatim and for the same reason (the geometry is derived from a standards table, so the arguments ARE the component), and it makes a host prepared by placed fasteners reopen with its bores parametric instead of as a snapshot plus a warning. Two boundaries are pinned by test rather than left to be discovered: a component OUTSIDE the built-in catalogue is refused at SAVE time (`SaveInputs` returns null) rather than written as something a load rebuilds wrong, and `ComponentAssembly(name, shape)` seeds its history with a lambda over an arbitrary `Shape`, so a host built THAT way keeps one opaque record — the `BooleanFeature` limitation showing through rather than a component one, with the test asserting exactly that split (one warning naming the `FuncFeature`, the `ComponentFeature` itself coming back). The catalogue coverage is a CLAIM rather than a fixture — `EveryCatalogueComponentKind_RoundTrips` enumerates the public `HardwareComponent` subclasses from the assembly and asserts the fixture list is all of them — and `DowelPin` writes its inserted length EXPLICITLY rather than relying on the constructor's "null = half", since a file that records the answer cannot drift from arithmetic that may change. What cannot round-trip is named, not hidden: `BooleanFeature` (a `Shape` graph), `VariableChamferRimFeature` (a law that is code), a `ComponentFeature` over a non-catalogue component, `FromFunc` lambdas — still WRITTEN to the file, skipped on load with a per-record warning unless the caller's `resolveOpaque` hook supplies the instance (`HistoryLoadResult.Complete`); user types join via `Register<T>` or `Register(type, factory)`. **The sketch curve vocabulary must cover everything `ToCurves` can emit, and that is a TEST rather than a convention** (`EverySketchSegmentKind_HasAJsonForm` enumerates the segment types from the assembly): the writer's default arm THROWS and `Document.Save` has no catch around `SaveHistory`, so a curve kind the reader learned and the writer did not takes the whole document down rather than degrading one feature. That is not hypothetical — elliptical arcs became first-class after this envelope landed, `Sketch.FromCurves` learned the `Ellipse2d` case and `InputJson.SaveCurves` did not, and any document holding an elliptical sketch feature **could not be saved at all**.
