The measure tool is the pick raycast plus two fields, drawn through the shared `AnnotationGeometry`, and shows whatever the Annot toggle says because it answers a question the user just asked rather than being documentation attached to a part. `DebugFilter.Shown` finally reaches `ResolveInstances`, and with no flags set it is the identity, which is why adding it moved no pixels — every one riding shared code rather than restating it (`SectionClip.Hides` through `ScenePick` for pick-render agreement; `SectionContours`, `ViewCubeMath`/`ViewCubeGeometry`/`ViewCubeAnimation` (the 250 ms constant taken, not re-typed), `StrokeFont` and `AnnotationGeometry`, all moved public into `EngrCAD.Viewer.Core` as **shared-render-model step 2** with the desktop delegating to them, verified by all 53 docs PNGs byte-identical; **step 3 is the per-part upload VALUE** — `PartUpload` + `PartUploads.Build(part, request)` + `PartUploadRequest`, the CPU half of "draw this part" (flat `RenderMesh`, the `FieldRendering.TryBuild` result and its error, the occlusion array, feature-edge and wireframe segments, the pick BVH) that all three front ends used to build by hand. **Two things it deliberately does NOT do, and both are why the larger `ViewerModel` stays declined**: it does not decide WHICH pieces to build (the one-shot offscreen pass skips what its resolved mode cannot use, having no dropdown to change its mind, while the window and the browser build everything so a style dropdown never re-uploads — so the CALLER states its policy in a `PartUploadRequest`, and `All` is a spelling rather than a rule), and it does not own the CACHE (all three key on `Part` reference, but the browser releases on tab switch, the window on GL deinit and the offscreen pass with its context). Occlusion rides as a **delegate** rather than a flag for the same reason: the window asks a never-bake cache read so an upload can never stall the render thread, the offscreen pass bakes inline because a headless render must be deterministic, and the browser has no bake at all — three different questions, one seam. What the shared code DOES own is every rule about the CONTENT, and the payoff is one of them: **a part carrying a displacement draws no feature-edge overlay at any factor** had been stated three times, once per pass. Verified the only way a pure render refactor can be — all 108 committed docs PNGs byte-identical; `TabMeshLoader`/`MeshFlavor` moved too, though the browser deliberately keeps its own single-threaded loader — Avalonia-free but thread-model-bound). The `?report` self-check covers the new rungs as pixel relationships (sectioning 32 374 → 20 593 body px + 676 gold isoline px; annotations toggle 786 px with the silhouette surviving at 32 405 against 32 374 — an overlay may only ADD, and `annotationBody` rides beside `annotationPixels` because a difference count alone cannot say so: the recorded 786 was reported as having "gone stale" at 34 317, and it had not — **the browser was ERASING the model when the overlay went on**, 32 374 → 786 lit pixels, while the check dutifully fired. `glClear` of the depth buffer is masked by `glDepthMask`, and `engrcad-gl.js` applies per-draw state without ever resetting it, so a frame ending in a `depthWrite: false` draw starts the next one with a clear that clears no depth; the stale buffer already holds the model at those exact depths, every fragment fails `LESS` against itself, and only the depth-test-off draws survive. One `gl.depthMask(true)` before the clear fixes it (and the same before the per-draw `clearDepth` the cube uses, so it is one rule — a no-op today). **The filed suspect was half right in the instructive way**: the occlusion-aware PMI work was ruled out because `AnnotationDepth` defaults to `AlwaysOnTop` and the browser never runs the two-pass path — all true, and the defect is still that commit's, which added `DepthWrite = false` to the helper BOTH branches share, for a reason exactly right about draws ("a disabled depth test does not write depth either") and silently wrong about clears. **Ruling out a code PATH is not ruling out a commit.** It hid for a release because the view cube is drawn LAST and does write depth, so it re-enabled the mask every frame — three passes emit `DepthWrite = false` (annotations, translucent fills, the ghost) and three do not (isolines, legend, cube) — and `?report` turns the cube off, so the self-check was the only configuration in the repo that could see it. The desktop is structurally immune and the reason is worth copying: every site there that turns the mask off turns it back on before returning, where a per-draw state applier has no "before returning"; the guard is a source-reading test in `InteropContractTests` (shown to fire) beside the property contract, since this seam has no compiler behind it either. Cube 4 770 px in-region, claims region clicks, `cubeSnapped=1` — the camera lands EXACTLY on the shared pose table because the animation's final step returns the target) — all routed through the SHARED `RenderModes.Resolve` precedence rule and `RenderModes.SortBackToFront`, never restated (the tests read their expectation *from* `Resolve`, since a copied precedence rule agrees with a broken implementation as happily as a correct one). `WireframeEdges` moved to `EngrCAD.Viewer.Core` for the same reason the camera did: it has no GL in it, and **its walk order decides the vertex order in the uploaded buffer**, so two copies would not even upload the same bytes. Measured against `OffscreenRenderer` as the reference at matched size/camera/style, fills, points and translucency agree within 2–10%; **wireframe deliberately does not** (26 228 lit vs 19 980) because the offscreen pass supersamples 2× and box-downsamples, dropping a 1-px line below an absolute brightness threshold — same primitives, different reconstruction filter, reported rather than tuned away. Points needed no `GL_PROGRAM_POINT_SIZE` (WebGL2 is GLES 3.0, where the cap does not exist and `ViewportControl` already skips it), but point size IS in framebuffer pixels, so `viewportSize` now also returns the device pixel ratio — the same correction the window makes for DPI and the offscreen pass for its supersample. Earlier rungs verified headlessly by reading the canvas back (33 912 lit pixels in a 693×420 buffer, and 111 481 pixels changed after an orbit, so the camera provably drives the render; WebGL2 IS available under headless `--disable-gpu`). Architecturally the load-bearing rule is that **no GLSL lives in JavaScript**: `wwwroot/engrcad-gl.js` owns the context, uploads buffers and issues draws, while shader source comes from `EngrCAD.Viewer.Core`'s `ViewerShaders` — the same strings the window and the offscreen pass compile — and every decision about what a frame looks like reaches JS as a plain `FrameDescription` (the background gradient's fullscreen triangle arrives as `Geometry = null, Count = 3` rather than an `if (isBackground)` in JS). **The camera is not forked either**: `CameraState` and the input bindings (`DragOrbit`/`DragPan`/`DragZoom`/`WheelZoom`, plus `PitchLimit`/`KeyStep`) moved into `CameraMath`, and `ViewportControl` now delegates to them with every constant preserved verbatim (−0.01 rad/px yaw, 1.006 and 0.88 zoom bases, 0.0018 pan scale) — so there is one answer to what dragging 100 pixels does and it is tested once; the only front-end difference is that a DOM wheel event reports ~100 px per notch counting DOWN as positive, normalized at the edge. **`ViewportFrame.Build` is a PURE FUNCTION**, which is the point: the window and offscreen passes drifted because comparing them meant looking at pixels, whereas draw order, clear colour, furniture ranges, per-instance matrices and neutral shader state are now asserted as values. Two of those assertions encode traps — **fills deliberately do NOT cull** (a section plane exposes interiors as backfaces, which the shared shader shades as cut material via `gl_FrontFacing`, so culling would look fine today and break sectioning silently), and **`uSectionCount` travels as a typed marker, never a plain number** (an `int` uniform, and the interop marshals every JSON number through `uniform1f`, which GL rejects with no visible error — `IntUniform` serializes as `{"int": n}` → `uniform1i`, and `Vec4ArrayUniform` as `{"vec4": [...]}` → `uniform4fv`, needed because four packed section planes are exactly 16 floats, indistinguishable from a mat4 by shape; WHICH uniforms carry which type stays a C# decision). Geometry crosses as `byte[]` (Blazor marshals that as a binary array; `float[]` would go through JSON) and that packing step is also the single double→float32 narrowing point. Two traps found the hard way: **`<script type="importmap"></script>` is REQUIRED in `index.html`** — the build fingerprints every framework asset and rewrites that map, and without it `dotnet.js` resolves its siblings by unhashed names and the boot 404s with no visible error; and **in-page timings cannot be read from a `--dump-dom` run**, because `--dump-dom` fires at load so the browser needs `--virtual-time-budget` to reach the end of the work, and under virtual time the clock does not advance during synchronous computation — every measurement reads 0 ms. The demo's `?report` beacon exists for exactly that reason and is measured under a real clock — and it is a 1×1 `<img>`, NOT `IJSRuntime.InvokeVoidAsync("fetch", url)`, because that marshals the JS `Response` back across the interop boundary and throws when it cannot, losing the measurement AND tripping Blazor's error UI: **a beacon must not be able to fail.** The former hardcoded showcase now lives in `samples/EngrCAD.Demo` (console app calling the Scene API — the consumer experience). **The live-modeling loop is `dotnet watch` + hot reload, not a custom CLI**: `EngrCad.ShowLive(Func<Scene>)` registers a `MetadataUpdateHandler` (`HotReload.cs`) that re-invokes the factory after each patch and calls `SetScene` (camera preserved; factory exceptions keep the last good scene and surface in the overlay; camera pose persists to a temp file across rude-edit restarts, 30-min freshness). **`.csx` scripting rides the SAME loop** (`tools/EngrCAD.Script` + `docs/examples/scripting.md`): a script defines `Scene scene` — the DocsGen snippet contract, same Roslyn seam, same references/imports, so fences and scripts are interchangeable verbatim — and the runner passes a recompile-the-file factory to `EngrCad.Run` (all switches free) while a `FileSystemWatcher` triggers the new public `EngrCad.NotifySourceChanged()` (= the hot-reload path — debounce, keep-last-good-scene, camera — for hosts whose model source is DATA; no-op unless ShowLive is active). The documented reusable-component pattern is deliberately NOT a module system: a parametric component is a plain C# method returning `Shape`/`Part`, identical in scripts (`#load`-shareable, see `samples/scripts/bracket.csx`), class libraries, and `Feature`/`HardwareComponent` wrappers. `EngrCad.Run(args, factory)` wraps model programs: no args → ShowLive, `--view` → static Show, `--export part.step|part.obj|part.vtu` → headless export (STEP per B-Rep-representable part via `Part.Source`, OBJ merged with transforms, VTU carrying the parts' simulation results as point data), `--render out.png` → **headless offscreen render** (no window; `--render-style points|wireframe|shaded|shaded-edges` and `--section x|y|z <offset>` select view style and section plane — CLI wins over the builder's `WithViewStyle`/`WithSection`; DocsGen `render:` fences take matching `section:<x|y|z>,<offset>` and `style:<name>` options). **Host configuration**: `EngrCad.Configure()` fluent builder (`WithTitle/WithQuality/WithRenderSize/WithLogger/WithLoggerFactory/WithViewportReady` → terminal `Run/Show/ShowLive/RenderToImage`) over a plain `EngrCadOptions` POCO — bindable as `IOptions<EngrCadOptions>` and seeded via `Configure(options)`. **Logging is `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.Abstractions`** — abstractions only, no provider; Viewer/Mcp host the message vocabulary, and it now **extends inward on a weighed per-project basis**: `EngrCAD.Interop` and `EngrCAD.BRep` take the abstractions reference (still no UI/provider — the kernel rule is about UI/rendering deps) so the long operations accept an **optional trailing `ILogger`** — `BrepBoolean.Union/Intersection/Difference` (event **80**, Information, passing the logger to its own sub-steps), `BRepTessellator.Tessellate` (**81**, Debug), the `MeshSdf` ctors incl. the winding build (**82**, Debug), `StepReader.Read/ReadFile` (**90**, Information) — null default, one branch, results stay return values (`StepReadResult.Diagnostics`, `Explain` reports are data the caller acts on; logging complements them). Mesh/Core/Implicit deliberately take NO reference: everything the todo named is reachable at the Interop/BRep seams (MeshSdf wraps the winding build), so the leaf kernels stay dependency-free. This REVERSED the earlier `IEngrCadLog` no-dependency decision, because the shim made every host write an adapter to save a reference most of them already had transitively. `Logging.cs` holds the host message vocabulary as source-generated `[LoggerMessage]` templates with **stable event IDs** (10s usage, 20s export/render, 40s live reload, 50s display, 60s MCP, 70s RPC, 80s Interop kernel, 90s BRep kernel), so sinks get fields not strings and levels can finally distinguish a skipped part (Warning) from a failed export (Error). **The default is `EngrCadLoggers.Console` (Information → stdout, Warning+ → stderr), NOT `NullLogger`** — a library defaults to silence but a program's front door does not, and `EngrCad.Run` is a model program's front door; pass `NullLogger.Instance` to silence it deliberately. `EngrCadLoggers.StandardError` is the MCP sink, and the console logger resolves `Console.Out` per call so it follows `StdoutGuard`'s redirection instead of writing protocol-corrupting bytes. Mesh-quality precedence is explicit `Scene` options > `EngrCadOptions.Quality` > `MeshQuality` defaults, implemented by `Scene.HasExplicitOptions`/`ResolveQuality(fallback)`/`PreMesh(fallback)` and honored by display, hot reload, `--export .stl/.obj`, and `--render` (which also uses the configured image size); all Run/export/render reporting and the live-reload overlay messages route through the log seam. **Adaptive tessellation** (`TessellationQuality`, opt-in via `MeshQuality.Tessellation`, docs `examples/quality.md`): a criterion — max angle/segment ($fa) and/or max chord deviation (OCCT deflection), clamped to [Min, Max]Segments — replaces the fixed counts for B-Rep tessellation; `ResolveFor(solid)` scans curvature radii and sizes ONE count pair at the largest radius (chord binds there, n ≈ π·√(r/2d)), and `Part.GetMesh` + `Part.GetFeatureEdges` resolve through the SAME criterion so the exact edge overlay can no longer detach from the faceted fill on large rims (the fix todo's chord-deviation investigation called for; with fixed counts the overlay stays deliberately finer at ≥ 96 seg/circle). Null keeps fixed counts bit-for-bit (locked by a bit-identity test); `SdfResolution` deliberately untouched. Offscreen rendering (`EngrCad.RenderToImage(scene, path, w, h, camera?, style?, sectionAxis?, sectionOffset?)`/`CanRenderToImage`, backed by `OffscreenRenderer` + a direct EGL-pbuffer `EglContext` over Avalonia's ANGLE natives + `PngWriter`) is the viewer self-verification loop — tests and agents render a scene and inspect pixels instead of screenshotting the demo app; it now has **full parity with the window** (display modes, global view style, axis sections, translucent ordering — only the selection highlight stays neutral; SDF isoline overlays included). **3D annotation rendering + measure tool**: `StrokeFont` (one polyline glyph table — digits, A–Z, dimension symbols — shared by the view cube's labels and annotation text) + `AnnotationLayer.cs` (self-contained like the isoline/cube precedents: `AnnotationGeometry` pure math builds the classic dimension anatomy — extension lines with gap/overshoot, dimension line, V arrowheads, radial/note leaders, datum boxes, billboarded screen-constant text; CPU-side billboarding rebuilt ONLY when the camera pose/viewport/annotation set changes, value-equality on `AnnotationCamera` is the cache key) drawn through the shared line program with the depth test off (always-on-top v1) and never section-clipped; per-part resolution failures surface in the status bar; SceneHost gets an Annot toggle (`ShowAnnotations`, default on) and a Measure toggle (`MeasureMode`: two picks via the pick raycast — `HitTest` gained an out world-point — create a transient point-to-point dimension, Escape clears, `MeasurePick` public for direct-call testing). Annotations DO render offscreen (unlike the view cube) — they are documentation, so docs renders carry dimensions — via one additive `AnnotationLayer.DrawOffscreen` call (supersample factor doubles as the text pixel scale). Infra lesson: Avalonia's ANGLE `av_libglesv2.dll` exports EGL entry points under an `EGL_` prefix (not the standard `egl*`), so a hand-rolled EGL binding must try both spellings. **Ambient occlusion streams** rather than blocking the window: nothing waits for the bake (measured at 12.3 s on the demo scene, and it already saturates every core, so more parallelism had nothing to give). The scene appears **flat-lit immediately** and each part's occlusion arrives as its own background bake finishes (`AmbientOcclusion.BakeInBackground`, cheapest part first, per-part progress as a transient "ao" badge on the tree rows via `ViewportControl.OcclusionBaked`, queued per *visible tab* so a tab you never open is never baked; `TryGet` is a pure cache read, the only lookup on the render thread). This is honest rather than a placeholder: **a mesh VAO with no occlusion buffer reads the context constant 1.0, which is exactly the AO-off shading**, so an unbaked part is the correct flat-lit render and the bake only darkens crevices. Measured time-to-window 27.1 s → 14.2 s in eager mode. `RenderToImage` still bakes inline (one-shot, must be deterministic) and shares the same cache and `Bake`, so window/offscreen parity holds. Also on `Part`: **`TryGetSdf`** joins `TryGetSolid` as the cached implicit lowering (toggling a section no longer re-lowers; deliberately NOT primed by `PreMesh`, since only the section overlay needs it), **`ClippedBySection`** (default true — false renders *and picks* the part whole inside a cutaway, the drafting convention that shafts, bolts, nuts, keys and pins are drawn unsectioned; the model tree carries a per-row cut/whole toggle via `ViewportControl.SetClippedBySection`, and the isoline overlay detects the changed flag itself like a visibility change), and `SectionPlane.On(Frame3d)`/`Through(point, normal)` for oblique cuts (the toolbar's **Cut@View** button places one from the current camera — through the orbit target, normal = eye direction). `SectionClip.Siblings` sits beside `Hides` as the cut-face overlay rule, derived from one sentence — a point on a plane is on the *visible* cut face iff `!Hides(p) && Hides(p + eps·n)` — from which both combine modes fall out with no special case. Underneath, occlusion is baked per-vertex on the CPU (hemisphere rays against the part's existing pick BVH, parallelized) rather than screen-space: no FBO or render-pass restructuring, and it is **identical in the window and offscreen by construction** — the parity rule `RenderCore` exists to protect. It rides as vertex attribute 2 (`aOcclusion`), so a mesh uploaded without an occlusion buffer just reads a constant 1.0; on by default (`EngrCadOptions.AmbientOcclusion`, `WithAmbientOcclusion`, `RenderToImage(ambientOcclusion:)`). Offscreen renders use 24-bit depth (16-bit + `PolygonOffset(1,1)` bit sawtooth notches out of silhouettes) and 2× supersample + box-downsample for AA (MSAA pbuffers are unreliable under ANGLE/WARP). **The render core now lives in its own assembly, `src/EngrCAD.Viewer.Core`** — no Avalonia, no Silk.NET, so it loads in a browser. It holds `ViewerShaders` (ONE shader set; `Header(es)` already emitted both an ES3 and a desktop 3.3 version header, which is exactly what WebGL2 needs), `CameraMath` (scene-scaled `FrustumPlanes` replaced the fixed 0.1/200 planes and 110/120-unit distance clamps in BOTH paths, so large scenes frame fully), `RenderModes`/`EffectiveMode`, `SectionPlane`/`SectionAxis`/`SectionCombine`/`SectionClip`, and `RenderGeometry`'s pure half — **never fork shader or camera code between front ends again**. Everything taking a `GL` stayed in `EngrCAD.Viewer`: `ViewerPrograms` (LinkProgram/CompileShader), `SectionUniforms`, `RenderUploads` (UploadMesh/UploadLines/UploadOcclusion) — a class cannot span assemblies, so those two renames were forced. **The namespace stayed `EngrCAD.Viewer` even though the assembly is `EngrCAD.Viewer.Core`**: `SectionPlane`/`ViewStyle` are public API used by `EngrCadOptions`, the MCP server, docs snippets and tests, and an assembly boundary is a packaging decision while a namespace is API. The refactor's real oracle was not the 1966 unit tests but the **50 rendered docs PNGs compared byte-for-byte** — a shader or camera change survives every unit test and still changes what users see. **Historically `RenderCore.cs` was the single source of truth for both render paths** (`ViewerShaders` — one shader set, offscreen sets neutral uniforms; `CameraMath` — scene-scaled `FrustumPlanes` replaced the fixed 0.1/200 planes and 110/120-unit distance clamps in BOTH paths, so large scenes frame fully; `RenderGeometry`) — never fork shader or camera code between window and headless again. `samples/EngrCAD.LiveDemo` is the parametric-bracket demo (`dotnet watch --project samples/EngrCAD.LiveDemo`); verified end-to-end: body edits hot-apply in ~0.1–0.7 s without restart. **On-demand tab meshing** (`EngrCadOptions.LazyTabMeshing`, default ON; `WithLazyTabMeshing(false)` or `--mesh all` opts out): `EngrCad.Show` no longer `PreMesh`es the whole document before the window — each tab meshes when first VIEWED on a background task (measured on `samples/EngrCAD.Demo`: **53.6 s → 2.3 s** to a visible window). `TabMeshLoader.cs` (Avalonia-free, unit-tested headlessly) publishes a **growing prefix** of the tab's instances as parts land, so the viewport is orbitable while the rest computes, and an already-meshed tab publishes synchronously with no background work — which makes eager mode the same code path rather than a second implementation. Three rules it enforces: a **generation token** checked in the worker between parts *and again after every posted callback*, so a tab switch cannot let a stale result land in the wrong tab; **cancellation stops at the LOWERING, not at part boundaries** — `Part.GetMesh(quality, progress)` passes its `ProgressCancel` to Surface Nets *and* to `BRepTessellator.Tessellate`, but deliberately NOT to B-Rep lowering, whose result is cached in `TryGetSolid` and would be poisoned by abandoning it. So a cancelled part keeps the expensive half and a revisit re-tessellates from the cache instead of starting over; the earlier rule was "the part in flight finishes", which made a tab switch wait out a whole tessellation for a result nobody wanted; and a **part that throws is named, not swallowed** (dropped from the published instances, red tree row with the reason, status bar + log) while the rest of the tab still loads. Progress UI is a bottom-centre panel (hit-test-invisible so it never steals an orbit drag): honest count plus a secondary line naming the kernel route (`MeshFlavor`: `Lowering to B-Rep...`/`Polygonizing the field...`/`Tessellating surfaces...`/`Reticulating splines...` — the last shown only for geometry that genuinely carries NURBS, which sketch profiles and glyph outlines do). Document-model side is additive: `Part.HasMesh` (lock-free probe so the UI thread never queues behind a mesh in flight), `Part.Prepare`, `Tab.PreMesh`, and `ConstructionTree()` moved to its own lock for the same reason. Headless paths (`--export`/`--render`/`RenderToImage`/DocsGen) unchanged. Testing lesson: `SendInput`'s `INPUT` struct must be **40 bytes** on x64 or every call silently returns `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER`; and `EngrCAD.Demo` is `WinExe` (so `MainWindowHandle` is the real window) while `EngrCAD.LiveDemo` is `Exe` (its `MainWindowHandle` is the *console* — enumerate non-console top-level windows or startup timings are meaningless). **MCP server** (`src/EngrCAD.Mcp` — its own package, so viewer/kernel consumers never inherit a protocol stack): `EngrCadMcp.Run(args, factory, title)` wraps `EngrCad.Run` and adds `--mcp`, serving *that program's* scene over the Model Context Protocol on stdio — tools `list_tabs`/`list_parts`/`describe_part` (properties-panel facts + resolved annotations + `Part.ConstructionTree()`)/`screenshot` (a real PNG as an MCP **image block**: standard views, view styles, section plane, tab/part scoping, via `OffscreenRenderer`)/`export`/**`save_document`/`load_document`** (the whole model — tabs, parts with their feature histories, assemblies, mates, annotations, results — as one `Document.Save` envelope, so a session's `set_param` edits SURVIVE it and reopen PARAMETRIC; parts with no construction recipe travel as mesh snapshots and are NAMED by both tools rather than silently flattened, save reading its answer off a load of what it just wrote; a loaded document is a session-lifetime OVERLAY that `reload` still discards, so there is one answer to where the model comes from; `adopt: false` is the dry run)/`reload` (hot-reload semantics: a throwing factory keeps the last good scene), plus an `engrcad://scene` resource; failures are `isError` results naming what *does* exist, never protocol errors. **The server can now DRIVE a parametric model, not just inspect it**: `set_param`/`suppress_feature`/`unsuppress_feature` edit `[Param]` values through the history a part already carries (`Part.History` IS the seam — no registration surface) and regenerate via the new `Part.Regenerate()` (Modeling: a fully successful regeneration swaps the fresh body in and clears every derived cache — mesh, cached B-Rep/SDF lowerings, feature edges, resolved annotations, construction tree — while a failure keeps the previous complete geometry; `Feature.Suppressed` became settable), with values routed through the SAME JSON seam as `SaveParameters`/`LoadParameters` so spellings cannot drift, the `RegenerationResult` surfaced verbatim as the tool result, and a successful edit bumping the session generation; edits die with the session (reload re-runs source, the truth). `screenshot` gained the viewer's full option surface (up to 4 `SectionPlane`s + combine — one `[0,0,1,offset]` plane is byte-identical to the axis spelling, locked by test; explicit camera as yaw/pitch degrees + distance/target or `cameraEye`, mutually exclusive with named views) and `export`'s `.png` takes width/height. **Structured content**: every JSON tool declares an output schema (`ToolSchemas.cs` — the SDK's explicit-`OutputSchema` + `UseStructuredContent` form, needed because the methods return `CallToolResult` directly; pinned 1.4.1 already has it, no bump) and one `JsonObject` feeds both `structuredContent` and the text block so they cannot disagree; `screenshot` deliberately declares none (an image block is not structured content). `StandardViews.cs` is **deleted** — poses route through the now-public `ViewCubeMath.PoseFor`/`CameraMath.FrameDistance` (bit-exact equivalence asserted against both implementations before the deletion; `NamedViews.cs` keeps only the name table, whose vectors now live in `ViewCubeMath.DirectionFor` beside the pose math). Two session-test lessons: **the server dispatches requests concurrently** (as MCP allows), so a client that edits then reads must await the edit's response — the batch-write test driver read the pre-edit model; and the no-GL path is forcible via **`ENGRCAD_NO_GL=1`** (a seam in `OffscreenRenderer`'s availability probe, doubling as an operational kill switch — the probe is a process-wide `Lazy`, so only a child process can simulate a GPU-less machine), locked by a stdio test asserting `screenshot` returns `isError` naming the constraint while every other tool works. **Live-viewer RPC (option (b))**: `RemoteControl.cs` in EngrCAD.Viewer exposes a **loopback-only** newline-JSON-RPC 2.0 TCP endpoint behind `WithRemoteControl(port, token)`/`--rpc [port] [--rpc-token t]` (OFF by default; port 0 = ephemeral, reported via log event 70 + status bar; token checked per request before dispatch), in three separable layers — `RemoteControlServer` (transport/framing/token/error envelopes), `RemoteViewerDispatcher` (vocabulary: ping/list_parts/set_view/fit/set_section/set_display_mode/set_view_style/select_part/get_selection/measure/screenshot over `IRemoteViewer`), `ViewportRemoteViewer` (the only Avalonia-aware layer: **every call marshals through `Dispatcher.UIThread`**, GL never touched — screenshot rides the capture-on-next-frame path) — which is exactly what makes transport+vocabulary+bridge testable headlessly over real sockets with a stub viewer (only the thin window wiring needs a manual pass, filed in todo). The MCP process bridges with `--mcp --viewer <port>`: bridge tools set_view/fit/set_section/set_display_mode/set_view_style/select_part/get_selection/measure/`viewer_screenshot` (per-request connections, so a watch-restarted viewer just reconnects; a dead endpoint is an `isError` naming `--rpc`; without `--viewer` the tools are never advertised). **`viewer_screenshot` returns PIXELS, not a promise**: `ViewportControl.CaptureScreenshotAsync` is `SaveScreenshot` with a `TaskCompletionSource`, and the load-bearing detail is WHERE it completes — not from inside the render pass, which the backlog entry proposed and which is too EARLY (`glReadPixels` runs there but the encode and write are deliberately off-thread, so a task completed there hands back a path to a file that does not exist yet), and not from the `Status` callback, which is a BROADCAST carrying prose for successes and failures alike so a listener cannot tell its own capture from the toolbar button's or a second concurrent request's — synchronising on a string is not synchronisation. It fires from the write, immediately after `File.WriteAllBytes`, and the bridge then reads the bytes back (legitimate precisely because the endpoint is loopback-only) and returns an MCP image block exactly as headless `screenshot` does. **Arming is UI-thread work; WAITING is not and must not be** — blocking the dispatcher is exactly how the frame would fail to arrive — so `ViewportRemoteViewer` awaits off-thread under a 10 s deadline (under `ViewerRpcClient`'s own 15 s, so a caller sees the viewer's own "no frame was produced… a minimised or occluded window may not render until it is shown" rather than a bare socket timeout), a superseded capture fails by name rather than leaving a task nothing will complete, and a capture that succeeds but cannot be read back names the path since the file is still there for a human. Splitting the write out of the GL call (`WriteCapture`: pixels in, no context) is what made the ORDER assertable headlessly — resume on the completion, assert the file is already there — which the entry had written off as untestable. **And the last leg the backlog reserved for a manual pass turned out to be mostly headless too, because a `ViewportControl` needs no window to exist.** The entry said `ViewportRemoteViewer` "takes a concrete `ViewportControl`, so there is nothing to substitute"; nothing needs substituting — a control constructs with no Avalonia application, no window and no GL context, and one that will never be rendered **IS** the fixture the screenshot deadline exists for, since "no frame arrives" is precisely its state. So the arm-on-UI-thread / wait-off-it split (the injected invoker is called ONCE and has RETURNED while the capture is still outstanding — if waiting lived inside the hop, a real dispatcher would deadlock and the frame would never come), the deadline, an unwritable path surfacing its OWN failure rather than a timeout, and the claim-exactly-once handoff are all pinned against a real control. That last one needed one seam: `ViewportControl.TakePendingScreenshot()` is the `Interlocked.Exchange` the render pass used to write inline, so a test can PLAY the render pass — take the capture, hand it to `WriteCapture` — and the render pass ASKS the rule instead of restating it. What genuinely still needs a window is a GL render pass reaching that claim plus a live dispatcher servicing the marshaled calls, and that is `WindowedRpcTests` (EngrCAD.Mcp.Tests): a child process on `--view --rpc 0`, the port read off the log line, the whole bridge driven over its socket, the frame back as PNG bytes — **opt-in via `ENGRCAD_WINDOWED_TESTS=1`**, because it opens a desktop window and stealing focus on every `dotnet test` is a real cost for a leg that changes rarely; that IS the once-per-release pass, reduced to one command. The recorded `SendInput` harness was not needed at all: **the RPC endpoint is a better driver than synthetic input for everything except the pointer bindings themselves**, since it speaks the viewer's own vocabulary and answers with values instead of pixels. One property it measured that no stub could: **`list_parts` is empty until the first frame renders** — instances handed to `SetInstances` are queued and swapped in BY the render pass while the port is announced from `OnViewportReady`, so a client connecting the instant it sees the port legitimately sees nothing. **Resolved with a readiness probe rather than a patch**: `ping` carries `ready` (`ViewportControl.InstancesDisplayed` — true once the render pass has adopted the swap and nothing newer is queued), a part-not-found refusal during the gap says "poll ping until ready" instead of reading as "this model has no parts", and the windowed test polls ready then reads the list ONCE with no blind retry. Reporting the PENDING list stays rejected (it would desynchronize the paths from the INDICES `select_part` and `set_display_mode` address), holding the port announcement until the first frame too (a window that never renders would never announce); honest scope — under lazy tab meshing the list is a growing prefix, so ready means "what is drawn", not "the document is fully loaded". The headless half is pinned against a real `ViewportControl` (one that never renders IS the gap, held open). **`set_animation_time` closes the last parity gap with the headless `screenshot`'s `t`**, and the shape is the interesting part: the headless tool re-evaluates `Animation.At(t)` for one instant, which is exact by construction, while a running window has its own clock and a capture arrives a frame later — so the verb parks the transport AND **pauses** it, reporting `"playing": false` rather than leaving a caller to assume. It is a VERB rather than a `t` on the capture so that "this window has no animation" and "the window produced no frame" stay two messages with two fixes. Playback state lives on `SceneHost`, not on the viewport, so the host passes a **seek delegate** (read through the static `EngrCad.Host` per call and never captured — a live reload re-arms the transport, and a delegate bound to one `AnimationPlayback` would go on seeking a timeline the window has left), and `SceneHost.SeekAnimation` goes through the play TOGGLE rather than `AnimationPlayback.Pause` so the button, the timer and the playback state cannot disagree. `ViewportControl` grew `InstancePaths`/`SelectedPath` (remote hosts speak occurrence paths, not indices) and one-shot `Measure(p1, p2)`; the JsonNode trap worth keeping: **an in-memory `JsonValue<int>` refuses a plain `double` cast**, so numeric RPC params go through a TryGetValue double/int/long ladder. Built on the official C# SDK's `ModelContextProtocol.Core` (not the metapackage — that would drag in `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting` for no benefit). **The stdio lesson: stdout IS the protocol channel** — `StdoutGuard` takes the raw stdout handle for frames and repoints `Console.Out` at stderr *before the scene factory runs*, so a design program that prints while it builds cannot corrupt the stream (a real child process whose model deliberately prints is driven through a full JSON-RPC session in tests, asserting every stdout line parses as a frame). Laziness is the other rule: the listing tools evaluate **zero** geometry (locked by an evaluation-counting `Sdf`), `describe_part` meshes only the named part, and render/export scope-mesh — whole-scene scope goes through `Scene.PreMesh` rather than forking it. **2D views** (`Region2dOffset` in Core, `PlanarSection` in Interop, `Sketch.Offset`/`Shape.Section`/`Shape.Silhouette`/`Shape.Wedge` in Modeling): **offset is ONE algorithm, not two** — outward = the region unioned with a slab per edge and a join per corner (`OffsetJoin.Round`/`Miter`/`Chamfer`, Clipper's miter limit 2 degrading to chamfer), and inward = that same dilation applied to the COMPLEMENT (`B \ dilate(B \ R, d)` with B the bounds grown by 3d), so self-intersection falls out instead of needing a cleanup pass and a shrunk plate returns two regions or none. Round joins are **inscribed** arcs, matching `Sketch.ToRegions`' one-sided contract so error never accumulates outward. **Open-path stroking** (`Region2dOffset.Stroke(path, width, cap, join)` — toolpath footprints, slots, SVG strokes) rides the same union: full-width slabs, corner joins offered on BOTH sides (the inner wedge is already inside its slabs, and a 180° reversal legitimately fills both — the round nose on a doubled-back path), butt/round/square caps; self-crossings just union and a closed circuit encloses its hole. `Shape.Section(plane)` = OpenSCAD `projection(cut = true)`, exact from the B-Rep surfaces when the shape lowers (fidelity set by chord tolerance alone) and mesh-derived otherwise — sealed internal cavities come back as holes; a plane flush with a face or containing an edge is refused (that section is an area, not a curve). **`Shape.SectionExact`/`PlanarSection.CurvedOfSolid` drop the flattening**, returning `CurvedRegion2d`s so a bore's rim is ONE arc rather than however many chords a tolerance asked for (what a DXF `CIRCLE`, an SVG `A` command and `Sketch.FromCurvedRegion` all want): the same edge crossings, the same midpoint containment probes and the same node-index chaining, factored into ONE piece enumeration with two emits, so the two answers cannot disagree about where the section IS. Exactness is decided by GEOMETRY rather than by curve type — a piece is a line when its own samples lie on its chord, and a circle when the axis is parallel to the cutting normal, the centre lies in the plane and eight samples sit at the radius — so a wrapped, reversed or reparameterized curve needs no case. What the tier cannot carry it FLATTENS rather than refusing (an oblique plane through a cylinder cuts an ellipse; a traced intersection is a polyline to begin with), so a mixed section is honest and its exact halves stay exact. The oracle is an AREA against a closed form, which is what separates "the section improved" from "the section is the geometry": a drilled plate's exact section reads 60·40 − 2πr² to 1e-9 where the flattened one is over by the inscribed n-gon's own deficit at any chord tolerance. `Silhouette` has no such mode and that is structural — it is the union of projected TRIANGLES, so there is nothing exact to recover. `Shape.Silhouette(plane)` = `projection(cut = false)`, the union of front-facing projected triangles folded through a **balanced tree over Morton-sorted faces** — measured on a 64-segment torus: **67 ms sorted+tree vs 2.4 s tree-unsorted vs 259 s linear**, because merging face 1 with face 900 gives two disjoint regions and cancels nothing. `Shape.Wedge` (OCCT `BRepPrimAPI_MakeWedge`) **is an extrusion** — a trapezoidal XZ section swept along y — so it carries one internally and every lowering delegates: no fourth code path, native in all three reps, exact under any affine. Four numerical lessons, each of which cost real geometry: **(a)** a miter apex must divide by `sum.LengthSquared`, never `sum.Length` squared — at a right angle those are exactly 2 and 2.0000000000000004, which tilts the apex a few ULPs off both offset lines so the collinear T-junctions stop collapsing and a mitered square returns with 8 corners; **(b)** `Arrangement2d` hole assignment must be by **connected component** (union-find over edges), not by area+containment — a lone convex cell adopted its OWN reversed perimeter as a hole (the two shoelace sums differ by an ULP and every vertex is shared, so the containment probe sat exactly ON the boundary and decided by luck), cancelling the cell to ~1e-16 and silently vanishing a whole operand from a union; loops of one component can never nest, which is the structural fix; **(c)** `FaceGeometry.Contains`' one-sided upward-v ray **cannot see a rim below the probe**, so it calls every point on a pole-bounded face outside (a sphere's cross-section came back empty) — run the parity in BOTH v directions: they agree on a properly closed trim and disagree exactly when one side is a pole; **(d)** independently-evaluated mesh vertices projected to 2D are only ULP-equal, so two edges that should be collinear sit ~2e-16 apart — too small for the arrangement to see as a T-junction, too large to ignore — and the sliver's interior sample rounds onto its own boundary, making bulk unions **order-dependent** (60.42 vs 59.33 on a 16-segment torus; a 64-segment one threw outright). `PlanarSection.SilhouetteGrid` quantizes projected coordinates at 1e-12 of the outline extent (scale-free tier) and every merge order then agrees. **Exact curved 2D booleans** (`CurvedEdge2d`/`CurvedRegion2d`/`CurveIntersection2d`/`CurvedArrangement2d`/`CurvedRegion2dBoolean`/`CurvedRegion2dOffset` in Core.Geometry2; `Curve2d.TryToCurvedEdge`/`FromCurvedEdge` and `Profile.FromCurvedRegion` in BRep; `Sketch.ToCurvedRegions`/`FromCurvedRegion`/`UnionExact`/`IntersectExact`/`SubtractExact`/`OffsetExact` in Modeling): everything built from a `Region2d` used to inherit chord flattening — offsets, sketch booleans, sections, silhouettes, and the `Profile`s an extrusion is built from — because arcs were polygonized before the arrangement saw them. The curved tier carries **lines and circular arcs through the arrangement unflattened**: a disc measures πr², two overlapping discs' union/intersection/difference match the analytic lens formulas to 1e-10, round offset joins are **exact sectors** (which RETIRES the inscribed-arc contract rather than honouring it — an exactly-offset arc IS the true offset), and a 40×20 plate with a Ø12 bore extruded 5 mm comes out within 1e-6 relative of (800 − 36π)·5 where the default-flattened route is 3.6e-5 off — an error that is a FLOOR no tessellation density can lower, since it is baked into the profile before any solid exists. **A PARALLEL type, not an extension of `Arrangement2d`**: the straight arrangement is boolean-critical and pinned bit for bit (now by `Region2dGoldenTests`' committed fingerprints as well as by the rendered PNGs), and teaching it curves would change its fan comparator (positions → tangents), its edge identity (a vertex pair → a vertex pair PLUS a carrier, since two points on one circle are joined by two arcs and by a chord) and its area rule at once, inside the code with the widest regression surface — the same call design.md §5 makes for `FaceSplitter`. **Lines and arcs is a COMPLETE tier, not a budget**: the cell walk orders edges at a node by departure TANGENT with departure CURVATURE as the tie-break (from p(s) = v + s·d + ½s²κ·n̂), and for lines and circles agreeing in both means sharing a carrier — a line and a circle never osculate, and two circles that osculate are one circle — so the tie-break is complete and the walk never guesses, whereas two Béziers can agree to second order and separate only in the third derivative. Béziers therefore stay flattened at the entry points (stated in the API contract, not hidden) and the comparator refuses by name if ever handed a second-order tie between different carriers. **ONE tolerance, and it is a LENGTH** — the arrangement's own vertex snap distance, with no second epsilon in the tier: a line is tangent to a circle when the centre's distance from it differs from the radius by less than it, two circles when their centre distance differs from r₀+r₁ or |r₀−r₁| by less than it, a point is on an edge when its distance is under it. **Near-tangency SNAPS to one touch point rather than refusing or dropping the contact**: both alternatives are unstable (two crossings a nanometre apart is a sliver cell classified by rounding), snapping is area-neutral to O(τ^1.5), and a tangential contact is *representable* here precisely because the two edges leave the node with equal tangents and different curvature. The interior sample gains one thing the straight proof did not need — the push is capped by the edge's own **curvature radius**, or a small circular hole inside a large cell sends the sample past the circle's centre and out the far side — and classification uses the epsilon-free `ParityInside` rather than the closed-set `Contains`, whose on-boundary band would answer "inside both" for a cell thinner than the weld tolerance. Section loop assembly **never welds**: crossings are solved once per EDGE on that edge's exact curve so both adjacent faces get the same point, and endpoints are node positions rather than the curve re-evaluated at a searched parameter (a ternary search left ~5e-11, enough to put a box corner at x = −4.999999999963106). **`CurvedRegion2dOffset.Stroke` completes the family** — the open-path stroke of a chain of lines and arcs (toolpath footprints, slots from centre lines, SVG `A`-command strokes) through the SAME union of primitives, with both new primitives closed form: an arc's slab is the **annular sector** between radii r ± w/2 over the arc's own angular span, exactly the set of points whose nearest path point is interior to that arc, area `(sweep/2)((r+w/2)² − (r−w/2)²) = sweep·r·w` (the squares cancel, which is why every test is an equality rather than a bound), degenerating at `w/2 ≥ r` to the pie SECTOR of radius r + w/2 — still exact, since every point of it sits within max(r, w/2) = w/2 of the circle. Round caps are exact half-discs, so with round caps and joins the stroke **IS** the path's Minkowski sum with a disc rather than being "short of it by the inscribed-arc sagitta", the qualification the polygonal twin carries — and that gap is a FLOOR, not a tolerance: the same quarter arc flattened to 4/8/16/32 chords and stroked polygonally approaches the curved answer strictly from below and is still 1e-3 short at 32. **One contract deliberately differs from the polygonal twin and the reason is the input vocabulary**: a chain that returns to its start is stroked as a CIRCUIT (the closing joint gets its joins, no caps), because a chain of EDGES makes closure structural where a list of POINTS can only spell it by repeating the first — read at the same weld tier the chain's own continuity check uses, so the two questions cannot be answered by two tolerances. It is invisible under round joins + round caps (a full disc at the closing vertex contains the join wedge, so the two readings agree as sets) and is what stops a butt-capped circuit carrying a notch or a mitered one losing its last corner — MEASURED and pinned by test rather than asserted in prose, so the residual filed against the polygonal twin cannot rot into a guess: a 10×10 square at width 2 with miter joins comes back **79** through the points spelling against **80** through the edge one, short by exactly the 1×1 outer corner square at the repeated start point; a gap in the chain is refused BY NAME with both endpoints and the gap printed, and zero-length edges are dropped, which cannot break the chain because their two endpoints are the same point. **The oracle that earns its keep is not an area formula**: stroking a simple closed loop by w is the same SET as growing the region it bounds by w/2 and taking away the region shrunk by w/2, and `Stroke` and `Offset` reach it through different primitives (two-sided full-width slabs against one-sided ones, plus the complement trick for the shrink), so agreement is two constructions checking each other rather than one checking its own arithmetic. **Space-filling curves + 2D infill** (`Geometry2.SpaceFillingCurve` + `Morton2d` in Core, `SpaceFillingInfill`/`InfillPath` in Modeling; docs `examples/infill.md`; design.md §2): finite-order Hilbert / Moore / Peano / Gosper / Z-order curves laid over a region, and the toolpath consumer that clips one to a sketch. **The name overpromises and the API says so** — a true space-filling curve is the LIMIT of a sequence and has infinite length, so the ORDER is the parameter: a caller states a SPACING and gets the order whose cell size is at or under it, with the ACHIEVED `Spacing` reported beside the `RequestedSpacing` (the `BiArcFit.MaxDeviation` convention). **Which quantity quantises is a DECISION**: the order comes from one inequality, `side ≤ spacing·radix^n`, and the surplus goes either into a finer spacing (hold the footprint) or a larger footprint (hold the spacing) — the SAME order either way, so neither is cheaper, and the footprint is held because a pattern's phase must be a function of what the caller stated (the cost, stated: on a long thin plate the spacing is set by the LENGTH). Everything under the placement is INTEGER, which is what makes the bar exact: sites counted in closed form (4^n / 9^n / 7^n+1) and pairwise distinct — the check that catches a flipped recursion — consecutive sites exactly one lattice step apart (Manhattan 1 on a square lattice; one of six Eisenstein unit vectors on Gosper's triangular one, so a DIAGONAL is not a step and the rule is family-aware), Moore's closure asserted rather than trusted (with the honest exception that Hilbert's order-1 four cells are a loop too, so only order ≥ 2 separates them), and `Length == SegmentCount × Spacing` exactly — which reproduces the classic `(4^n − 1)/2^n` on a unit square. Only coverage is measured, against a bound DERIVED from the cell's own circumradius: exactly √2/2·h for the square families and 0.5738·h for Gosper, against the triangular lattice's 1/√3. **Three things were measured rather than assumed.** (a) **The longest straight run SATURATES** — 3 cells for Hilbert and Moore, 5 for Peano, 2 for Gosper, at every order from 3 upward — so "Hilbert is the isotropic member" is a number, and Peano's advantage is 5 against 3, a real difference and a small one. (b) **Z-order is not a curve, and its own arithmetic says by how much**: exactly `2^(2n−1) − 1` of its `4^n − 1` steps are not lattice steps (half of them minus one) and the largest jumps the full grid width, so it is offered as the bijective ORDERING it is and refused BY NAME for a fill; `Morton2d` holds the one interleave `PlanarSection`'s silhouette fold already sorted by, since two spellings of one bijection would let a fold's merge order and a curve's visit order disagree about the same grid (no committed docs PNG moved). (c) **Gosper does not tile a rectangle**, so its island is placed by an inradius COMPUTED from the walk — the nearest unvisited site's distance from the centroid less the lattice's covering radius `1/√3`, sound because a point closer than that has its nearest site closer than the nearest unvisited one — rather than tabulated; the price is reported, its achieved spacing running 2–2.6× finer than the ask where a square family's stays under 2×. The infill consumer clips against `SketchRegion`'s exact signed distance (a COMPARISON against a stated clearance, defaulting to half the achieved spacing, with no tolerance in it), returns the RUNS the clip left (within a run consecutive points are exactly `Spacing` apart), and measures coverage through `Region2dOffset.Stroke` — never inferring it from the path length, which is an upper bound the path violates wherever it turns back on itself; `RegionArea` is deliberately the FLATTENED area so both sides of `CoveredFraction` are measured the same way, and isolated single-point runs are counted AND contribute their round-cap disc. **Both silent misses are refused by name** — a region the clearance erodes to nothing, and a connected PIECE of the eroded region the lattice's phase stepped over (the erosion has already said a pass fits there, so that one is phase rather than geometry; pinned by a fixture built from the known grid phase, the alignment-not-tolerance family). **What is deliberately NOT refused is stated**: a thin NECK inside a piece that is otherwise filled shows up in `CoveredFraction` instead, because the piece as a whole does catch passes and a connectivity test cannot see inside it. **That last one is now a MEASUREMENT rather than a caveat**, and with it the family's two remaining consumers and its two placement residuals: `Region2dThickness` (Core) is the 2D twin of `Manufacturability`'s opposing-ray wall thickness — it reports the PERPENDICULAR distance to the line of the segment the probe hit rather than the raw ray length (exact wherever the opposing boundary is straight, which for a polygon is everywhere, and what makes a tapered slot read its true width), starts the probe exactly ON the boundary because the source segment is excluded by INDEX (a stand-off biases every reading low by its own length — measured, it did), and counts a hole's own segments so the WEB between a bore and a wall is a neck like any other; `InfillPath.ThinnestFeature` reads it, so a caller compares `Minimum` against `Spacing + 2·Clearance` instead of squinting at a coverage fraction. **`SpaceFillingCurve.OverTiled` closes the bounding-SQUARE residual**: `TiledHilbertRoute` moved down from Modeling into Core as the public `TiledHilbertLattice` (its natural home once a tamper mesh and an infill both read it — and `TamperMesh`'s own copy of the block-count rule is deleted rather than left as a second spelling), and the placed form holds the RECTANGLE, so an 80 × 12 plate at spacing 3 keeps 112 of 112 generated cells where the square path kept 128 of 1024 and had its spacing set by the plate's LENGTH. The footprint being held is exactly what makes the cells anisotropic, so `Anisotropy` is REPORTED and the trade is stated (small blocks fit tightly and stretch the cells, large ones stay isotropic and quantise coarsely, `blockOrder: 0` being the plain serpentine); one block reproduces the square form site for site and bit for bit and every incumbent construction reports `SpacingX == SpacingY == Spacing` bit-identically, so it is a generalisation rather than a second mode. **`SpaceFillingCurve3d`** is the volume member — Skilling's transpose algorithm, chosen for the reason the 2D file gives for Peano's digit rule (a closed form has no orientation table to get backwards, and the bijectivity test is what would catch one), Hilbert ONLY because the consumer is a single connected path through a volume while Z-order is not a curve, Peano's 3D member is radix 3 and Gosper has no 3D analogue, and a PARALLEL type rather than a mode because a 3D curve shares every convention and none of the data. Measured rather than taken from the literature: `8^n` sites pairwise distinct, consecutive sites exactly one lattice step apart, `Length == SegmentCount × Spacing` exactly, the two terminals ADJACENT CORNERS of the cube (so 3D blocks would tile), and the longest straight run saturating at **3** at orders 2–5 — the SAME number the 2D curve reports, so "no preferred direction" carries into the volume rather than being claimed of it. Its consumer **`SolidInfill`** clips it to a body's own field: the two silent misses are refused by name there too with the instrument STATED (no 3D erosion exists, so "is there room at all" is a probe grid at half the spacing, and too-thin and stepped-over get different messages), and `Waste` reports what the bounding CUBE cost — under 50% on a cube, over 85% on a 20 × 4 × 4 bar — so the tiled 3D footprint stays a decision with evidence attached rather than a guess (the per-LAYER alternative is a documented recipe, `Shape.Section` then the 2D fill, because one path per layer and one path through the part are different deliverables). **`SurfaceDecoration`** is the other consumer and its design IS the honesty: a flat curve laid on a doubly-curved surface through `MeshLocalParam`'s exp map (inverted per QUERY POINT by a BVH over the triangles in (u, v) plus barycentric interpolation, legal because a triangle's own map is affine both ways; a `Sketch`/glyph overload lays every loop of every region CLOSED in one call — the glyph/engraving convenience — while cutting a groove INTO the solid stays a separate operation the caller strokes/booleans the laid `SurfaceCurve` for) REPORTS the distortion it carried, measured on the curve actually laid rather than quoted — 3.5e-4 on a developable tube, and on a 20-radius sphere cap min 0.9263 / max 1.0005 / mean 0.9882, so the MEAN reads a comfortable 1.2% while the tightest pass is 7.4% closer than it was drawn, which is exactly why the EXTREMES are the report and the mean rides beside them (read `MinScale` for a guaranteed pitch, `MaxScale` for a guaranteed clearance); a point past the map BREAKS the run and is counted, since continuing it would mean inventing surface, and the default radius `reach·√2 + 2·longest edge` is a margin rather than a bound with the reason stated (a query point is placed from the TRIANGLE around it, and the map's radius is a GRAPH distance that over-estimates the straight-line one — on a grid whose quads carry one diagonal, half the directions cost the full MANHATTAN distance). And all three consumers share **ONE linker** (`RunLinker`), which the residual demanded: deterministic greedy nearest-endpoint over (start, end) pairs, dimension-agnostic, a PERMUTATION by construction so it can shorten the travel and cannot lose a pass, a HEURISTIC that says so (`TravelLength` beside `SourceOrderTravelLength`, since this is the open travelling-salesman problem in disguise). **The measured finding is why greedy is the right heuristic here**: on a space-filling fill it reverses NOTHING, because the curve order already leaves each run pointing at its successor — so the incumbent order is a good tour and the linker's job is picking up the ends it left behind, with the reversal capability pinned on a hand-built case rather than expected from a fill. Docs `examples/infill.md`; no committed docs PNG moved, which is the statement that none of it changed geometry anywhere. **Anti-drill tamper mesh** (`TamperMesh.cs` + `TiledHilbertRoute.cs` in Modeling; docs `examples/tamper-mesh.md`; design.md §6b): the conductive serpentine an enclosure wall carries so that drilling through to the protected circuit severs a net and raises a tamper event — the HSM/payment-terminal construction, and **the deliverable is a GUARANTEE rather than a pattern**. Every cell has the route through its centre, so no point of the footprint is further from it than a cell's circumradius `R = ½·hypot(pitchX, pitchY)`; a drill of diameter `d` at `c` misses the copper exactly when `dist(c, route) > d/2 + w/2`, giving the largest drill that CAN pass (`2R − w`) and the smallest that MUST cut a net wherever it lands (`2R + w`). Between them it is position-dependent, so the honest answer is a **BAND**, and the design equation is the sever end, `pitch ≤ (d − w)/√2` (`PitchForDrill`, whose loop closes on itself in the docs: ask for the pitch, build, read the MEASURED guarantee back — a 120×80 wall against a 3 mm drill on 0.2 mm copper lands at 2.9695 mm sever). **The bound being ATTAINED is the whole claim, so it is measured rather than assumed**: a dual-grid corner is only `h/2` from the route when one of the four cell pairs meeting there is consecutive on it and the full circumradius when none is — the **blind corner** — and both cases exist, the footprint's own four corners always (they touch a single cell) and interior blind corners from block order 3 upward, COUNTED at 0 / 1 / 9 / 47 on a plain Hilbert block at orders 2–5. So below order 3 the interior worst case really is `h/2` and only the boundary reaches `√2`; the guarantee is unchanged either way and `WeakestPoint` says which. `Guarantee` is a certified branch and bound (distance to a polyline is 1-Lipschitz — the `SurfaceCull` argument — so a cell's centre distance plus its half-diagonal bounds it, and `Uncertainty` is the bracket), held by test against the closed form AND against an independent dense scan, since a plausible formula is exactly what must not ship wrong. **The route is a TILING of Hilbert blocks** — `TiledHilbertRoute`, the "tiled Hilbert blocks with their ends linked" the infill residuals name: a block enters and leaves at two ADJACENT CORNERS of its square, so the eight symmetries supply whichever (entry, exit) pair the neighbours need and a boustrophedon over the block grid links them into one Hamiltonian path over any `p × q` rectangle, with `1 × 1` reducing to Core's own lattice site for site. That is what makes the achieved pitch land near the request instead of the next power of two, at a **stated trade**: small blocks fit an arbitrary rectangle tightly and keep cells nearly square, large blocks are more isotropic and quantise coarsely (`Anisotropy` reports it, 1.018 → 1.526 across orders 0–3 on a 60×40 wall). **Hilbert's locality is a LIABILITY here and is stated rather than sold**: the open curve is right because a continuity monitor needs two terminals (Moore's loop would have to be cut) and both ends land on the footprint's outer boundary where a connector wants them, but points near in space are near in path order, so a short bridge across a break restores a long stretch and NO curve fixes that — the countermeasure is two or more interleaved nets watched for continuity AND mutual isolation, built as the same route offset evenly across each corridor so every neighbouring-conductor gap is one number (`IsolationGap`). They are deliberately not sold as independent detectors (they run parallel half a pitch apart, so a drill cutting one cuts the other); what they buy is the SHORT. The symmetric offset set `(k + ½)/N − ½` was chosen over one keeping a net on the route because it centres the pattern AND measures better anyway (0.78 / 0.73 / 0.71 of the single-net gap at 2 / 3 / 4 nets). **What the curve actually buys is a CHANNEL**: a plain serpentine at the same achieved pitch measures the SAME circumradius — the bound depends only on the cell and on every cell being visited — so the difference is that a serpentine's free space holds a straight channel the width of the wall while a tiled Hilbert route's `LongestStraightRun` is 4 cells at every block order above zero, and block order 0 IS the serpentine, which makes the comparison a family member rather than a strawman. **The copper is BUILT, not booleaned**: `TamperNet.Outline` is one simple polygon from the two ±w/2 mitered offsets, because `Region2dOffset.Stroke` unions a slab per segment at `O(E²)` — minutes at mesh scale for a shape with a closed form — and the oracle is an IDENTITY rather than a tolerance, a mitered ribbon's area being exactly `length × width` since at every corner the outer miter triangle is congruent to the inner notch, which holds only if the ribbon does not overlap itself (so the area check IS the simplicity check). Refused by name: a trace at or above the corridor share (an electrical short, not a tolerance question), a drill no wider than the trace (`PitchForDrill` has no positive solution — such a drill passes through the conductor without removing a cross-section), a non-rectangular or cut-out wall (the route would break into runs and a broken net cannot be monitored — `SpaceFillingInfill` is the honest answer there), a clearance that eats the wall, and a pitch past the cell cap; **conformal placement on a doubly-curved wall is not offered AT ALL** rather than approximated, since `MeshLocalParam`'s 2–5% distortion would land in the pitch and therefore in the guarantee. **And cross-checking the ribbon against `Region2dOffset.Stroke` found a real Core defect, filed rather than fixed**: `Stroke` offers the corner fill on both sides as `(v, left0, left1)` and `(v, −left0, −left1)`, but negating BOTH normals does not flip the turn's sign (`cross(−a, −b) == cross(a, b)`), so its `cross < 0` gate admits both wedges or neither and every CLOCKWISE corner loses its genuine outer fill — the deficit is exactly `(clockwise corners) × w²/4`, matched on six paths, and the one-line fix is to swap as well as negate. Not fixed in this pass because it moves `infill-hilbert.png`. **Debug modifiers + validation report**: `Part.Ghost/Hidden/Isolated` (OpenSCAD %/*/! analog; `#` highlight stays the selection mechanism) with ALL rules in `DebugFilter` (Modeling) — Ghost renders translucent via `Part.EffectiveDisplayMode` (the property every render path reads: window `EffectiveModeOf`, offscreen, web frame build) but never exports; Hidden neither renders nor exports (byte-identical offscreen render to a scene never containing it — it must not even influence framing); Isolated shows/exports only isolated parts — shared by SceneHost visibility, `RenderToImage`, `--export` (all formats incl. STEP via the filtered instance list) and MCP screenshot/export; no flags = identity, locked by test. `SceneReport.Create(scene)` (assert/echo analog): per-part kind/faces/watertight/volume/area/bounds + named notes (open mesh with boundary-loop count, meshing failure captured not thrown, non-positive volume, active debug flags), `ToText()` aligned table behind the viewer's **Check** toolbar button, `AllClean` for scripts; docs in `examples/viewer.md`. **Manufacturability checks** (`Manufacturability.cs`; docs `examples/manufacturability.md`; design.md §6b): draft angle against a mould pull, overhang area against a print build direction, and wall thickness — one deliverable shape, a **report plus a `MeshField`** the existing `FieldDisplay` colours with no new rendering code (each report carries a ready `Display`). **Every check answers TWICE, at two stated fidelities**: the VERDICT from the most exact source the part has, the PICTURE from the display mesh, and reading the verdict off the mesh instead is wrong in the direction that matters — an inscribed facet is steeper than the surface it approximates, so a 3° drafted CONE reads 2.92° at display quality and a 3° minimum would fail a correct part (`TetQuality`'s cries-wolf rule). Draft is `asin(n·pull)` (0 = a wall parallel to the pull, sign = which mould half): planar faces exact with `Samples == 1`, curved faces SAMPLED over the trimmed domain plus their pulled boundary loops and flagged, a part with no B-Rep falling back to facets with a `Note`; global UNDERCUTS are stated as a non-goal (a face can have ample draft and still be shadowed — a visibility question along ±pull, not a normal question). Overhang is `asin(−n·build)` per facet with the area and the build-plane `ProjectedArea` totalled; there is no build PLATE in the model, so a face on the bed reads like any other ceiling. Thickness is an OPPOSING-FACE ray cast reporting `t·|n·n_hit|` — the perpendicular distance to the plane of the facet hit, EXACT wherever that surface is planar (plates, ribs, bosses, shelled prisms) and what makes a tapered wall read its true thickness where the raw ray over-reports by 1/cos; it under-reports against a locally convex opposite and over-reports against a concave one, and since the whole surface is probed the conservative reading is the one the minimum keeps. The estimator was ASSESSED before it was promised, which changed what got built: "twice the interior distance" is not an estimator at all (the field is zero ON the surface, so it only becomes one by turning into the medial-axis ball), and the ball — better at a fillet or an inside corner — is filed as a NAMED alternative rather than a silent upgrade, because two estimators answering one question must both be nameable. **Three rules the measurements forced.** (a) **A threshold is compared on the DOT PRODUCT, never on the derived angle**: a wall built at exactly 45° reports `SteepestDegrees = 45.000000000000007` because `asin` round-trips 1/√2 an ulp high, so a degrees comparison calls a wall drawn at the stated angle an overhang and reports 848.5 of area — the `PolygonFan` tie-guard lesson in another costume, pinned by a test asserting BOTH halves. (b) **An unmeasurable point takes the conservative end of the scale, not NaN**: `FieldRange` skips NaN when ranging but a NaN still paints as the colour map's BOTTOM stop, which on a thickness plot is the colour of the thinnest wall in the part — so a ray with no opposing face would be drawn as the exact defect the check exists to find; those points carry the model diagonal and are COUNTED. (c) **A curved surface reports the TESSELLATION's angle**: an inscribed n-gon pyramid's lateral faces are steeper than their cone by exactly `atan(cos(π/n))`, so a 45° cone reads 44.8617° at 32 segments and 44.9915° at 128 (matched to 1e-12) and passes a 45° threshold for a reason about the mesh — hence the tie is pinned on PLANAR fixtures (a 45° wall, and a ceiling at a 90° threshold where the sine is exactly 1.0). A field is per-VERTEX and these are facet quantities, so each vertex carries the WORST incident reading — right for a check, and it means a large planar face with no interior vertices is interpolated from its corners, so the report is the verdict and the picture is a locator. Verified against closed forms: a drafted block's walls read the drafted angle to 4.4e-16 of a degree with every sign inverting under a reversed pull, an undrafted one reads exactly 0; a shelled box reads its 2.5 wall to 2.2e-16 relative at scales 1e-3/1/1e3 (every guard RELATIVE — the absolute-epsilon-on-an-area trap); a right-triangular prism reads `ab/hypot(a,b)` = 14.142135623730949 where the raw ray is 14.5297, so the fixture can SEE the correction; a 45° cone's lateral area converges quadratically on √2·πr² (ratios 4.00); and the lower hemisphere is exactly half the sphere at 1e-13 with its projected area converging on πR². **The complement is the general lesson**: a sphere CAP's area does not converge at all, because the region's boundary is a level set crossing the interior of a face and a facet is all-or-nothing, so the answer snaps to a facet band — **an integral over a facet-classified region converges like its BOUNDARY, not like its area**, which makes a closed form for one a sanity band rather than a tolerance. Two testing notes: `Part.GetMesh` caches at the FIRST caller's quality, so a convergence loop reusing one `Part` measures one mesh three times and reports a perfectly flat convergence (a fresh `Part` per quality); and the same work MEASURED a kernel defect filed in todo.md — **`Shape.Sphere`'s meridian refines with `curveSamples`, not `SegmentsPerCircle`**, because the sphere factory's generator is a rational NURBS arc while `IsAngularlyParameterized` recognizes only `Circle3d`/`Ellipse3d`, so doubling the density doubles the facet count instead of quadrupling it (1536/3072/6144/12288 at 32/64/128/256) and any latitude-quantized quantity is frozen (the cap boundary sits at 119.2776° at every one of them). **2D interchange (DXF & SVG)** (`DxfDocument`/`SvgDrawing` in Modeling, docs `examples/dxf-svg.md`): DXF both ways — sketches out as exact LWPOLYLINE bulge arcs (bulge = tan(sweep/4)) / CIRCLE per full-circle loop / cubics flattened at a stated chord tolerance **or written EXACTLY as SPLINE entities** (`DxfCurveMode.Spline`: a cubic Bézier IS a clamped degree-3 B-spline over four control points, so a béziered profile's area survives a round trip to full precision; the cost is structural rather than numerical and is stated — a loop containing a cubic arrives as a CHAIN, LWPOLYLINE runs plus one SPLINE per cubic, since DXF's polyline vocabulary has no cubic vertex, and the reader re-closes it by endpoint. **A sketch with no cubics writes byte-for-byte the same file under either mode**, asserted, which is what makes the option safe to reach for. Reading is deliberately NARROWER than writing — degree 1, and non-rational degree 3 already in Bézier form (clamped ends, interior knots of multiplicity 3, so control points split four at a time with nothing computed) — while a RATIONAL spline (no polynomial cubic form) and a general B-spline (needs knot-insertion decomposition) are REPORTED by name rather than sampled, because the entity list is what the FILE says and the sketch list is what this kernel can carry exactly; the knot-multiplicity tests are EXACT comparisons, since a knot vector is a list a writer either repeated or did not and a tolerance would accept a curve merely NEARLY in Bézier form and then split it at the wrong places), entities (LINE/ARC/CIRCLE/LWPOLYLINE/SPLINE/TEXT, layers) back in as `Sketch`es (closed polylines directly, loose entities endpoint-chained at the weld tier, unclosable chains reported not invented; nesting left to the caller); **`$INSUNITS` both ways** — the writer always declares its unit (millimetres by default) because a file that does not SAY what its numbers mean leaves every reader to guess, exactly the LTYPE-table duty, and the reader HONOURS the declaration rather than merely reporting it: an inch file's coordinates are scaled into millimetres and the document comes back LABELLED millimetres so a re-save is correct instead of declaring inches over millimetre coordinates, with the original unit and factor in `Diagnostics` (where "what the reader did" belongs — it is not a property that could round-trip). `Unitless` is the file's honest "no claim" and is never scaled, since inventing a factor there is the silent mis-scaling the feature exists to prevent (the `IgesReader` unit-flag lesson). One detail with teeth: **a rescale moves VERTICES and leaves BULGES alone** — a bulge is tan(sweep/4), an angle invariant under a uniform scale, and scaling it too would reshape every arc; the test asserts the area scales by exactly the square of the factor, which only holds if the sweeps survived; SVG out with line-CLASS-driven styling (Visible/Hidden/Section → solid/dashed/dash-dot groups per layer — the build123d edge-classification lesson) consuming Section/Silhouette regions and exact sketches (`A`/`C` commands), y-up mm flipped once at the root. **Engineering drawings** (`HiddenLine.cs`/`Drawings.cs`/`SheetAnnotations.cs`/`SheetExport.cs` in Modeling; docs `examples/drawings.md`): `HiddenLineRemoval.Project` turns a scene into classified 2D line work — `HiddenLineRun(Points, Visibility, Source)` — and the fidelity split is stated in the output rather than hidden: what is DRAWN is exact wherever the kernel has it (a B-Rep part's `GetFeatureEdges`, so a bore rim is a smooth circle at any mesh quality) while what is ANSWERED ("is there material in front of this point") comes from the display mesh, and the one gap — a smooth surface has no modelled edge along its outline — is filled from the mesh's view-dependent silhouette and labelled `EdgeSource.Silhouette`. **Visibility is two stages and the first is exact**: the surface immediately around a sample point is read from its own instance's mesh, and if every face there points away from the viewer the point is buried in its own material — hidden, no ray, no non-local query — which settles the majority of a solid's edges for free; only then does a ray go out, started at the point stepped off along the **most eye-facing local normal**, which is what makes the tangencies work (on a bore's bottom rim that normal points into the void, so the ray runs up the empty hole instead of scraping the wall it is parallel to) and simultaneously fixes the exact-edge-inside-the-inscribed-mesh problem (an exact point on a concave surface sits inside the mesh by up to the chord sagitta, which is why the bias is a FRACTION of the model and not a weld-tier constant). Three measured rules: **feature-edge segments must be CHAINED into polylines before sampling** — a segment is the unit a run can split at, so a rim delivered as 96 chords put the boundary on 4.870, exactly the 52.5° sample, where the chained form bisects to 5.000 (the general lesson: check that what a refinement refines is not already quantized by its input); **a run shorter than a pen stroke is absorbed into its neighbour**, because within one bias step of a model VERTEX "the surface here" legitimately includes the far side's faces and no epsilon removes a geometric ambiguity (measured cost: three corner junctions on a box lose 0.037 of an analytic 57.155, one bias each); and **at a tangency the coincident pair is drawn once, solid** — reached structurally, since a cap normal exactly perpendicular to the view is not rejected by the back-face stage. `DrawingSheet` is paper + border + title block + placed `DrawingView`s in sheet millimetres (origin bottom-left, y up — `SvgDrawing`'s convention); `StandardLayout` builds front/top/right + iso at one shared scale, the largest ISO 5455 ratio that fits (`DrawingScales`), third or first angle, with the three directions from the new **`StandardViews`** — the direction table MOVED down from `ViewCubeMath` into Modeling and delegated back, because a sheet needs it and is built with no viewer in the room, so widget and sheet read ONE table. A projection is cached and PLACEMENT applied after, so layout is cheap. **A section view takes a POINT, not a plane** (`SectionThrough`): a section view's cutting plane is perpendicular to its own view direction by definition, which is what makes the exposed faces project in true shape and so worth hatching and dimensioning — an oblique cut is offered as a view along the oblique normal rather than as a foreshortened lie; `ClippedBySection = false` parts pass through whole; cut faces come from `PlanarSection.OfSolid` where the part lowers and the mesh cut's loops where it refuses (a flush plane). `SheetHatch.Fill` clips 45° lines by an **exact even-odd scan** whose one careful decision is a crossing at a vertex — a HALF-OPEN span test, so a vertex is counted by exactly one of its two edges — anchored to the ORIGIN so neighbouring cut faces share one pattern.
