The drafting layer (`SheetAnnotation`: linear/aligned/horizontal/vertical, radial, diameter, angular, notes, BOM balloons) **splits two unit systems, which is the point**: anchors and measured values in projected MODEL coordinates so a dimension reads the part, anatomy in PAPER millimetres so it stays printable at any scale — and `SheetStyle` states each length as a RATIO to its text height, those ratios being `AnnotationGeometry`'s pixel constants over its own 12-px text height, asserted by a test that reads both sides. `SheetWriter.ToSvg/ToDxf/ToPdf` consume one `Compute()` so the writers cannot disagree; SVG gained fixed sheet sizing, open polylines and text (written OUTSIDE the single y-flip with y negated, since inside it the flip mirrors every glyph), DXF gained a TEXT entity both ways and an **LTYPE table** — a file that NAMES a line type its layers use must DEFINE it or every reader shows solid lines and the classification is lost in transit. **PDF export — the deliverable format** (`PdfDrawing` + `SheetWriter.ToPdf`/`SavePdf`; design.md §6c): hand-written, dependency-free, and built for the **byte fixed point** — uncompressed ASCII streams (legal PDF; the BrepArchive text argument) with **no /Info and no /ID**, both optional per the spec and both exactly the fields whose natural values (CreationDate, a clock-salted MD5) would make two writes of one sheet differ, so writing the same sheet twice is byte-identical and asserted so. **PDF's page origin is bottom-left y-up — the sheet's own convention — so NO y-flip exists**: the one transform is a single `cm` mapping mm→points (`PointsPerMillimetre`, the ONE 72/25.4 constant), every coordinate in the stream is the model's millimetre value verbatim, and the SVG writer's text-outside-the-flip machinery is simply not needed (a transform you do NOT need is worth recording). Pens are `SvgDrawing.SvgPen`'s own table (one pen table, two vector writers — cut boundaries are SOLID in both, the layer carries the distinction); text is the **standard-14 Helvetica over WinAnsi** (the SVG writer's real precedent is system-Helvetica `<text>`, not the stroke font — which lives in Viewer.Core, unreachable from Modeling, and duplicating its glyph table would be the two-copies drift), with anchor shifts from transcribed Helvetica AFM widths (verify-against-datasheet) since PDF has no text-anchor. **Characters outside WinAnsi refuse by name** (a silent `?` in a dimension is a wrong drawing) with ONE deliberate substitution: the drafting diameter sign U+2300 travels as O-stroke U+00D8, its standard drafting stand-in, pinned by test. One formatting rule: **PDF's number grammar has no exponent form**, so "R" serves except below 1e-4 magnitude, which takes fixed notation — a grammar constraint, not a tolerance. Verified the OFF/LZW twin-decoder way: an independently written parser walks the xref (every offset checked against its object), tokenizes the content stream and asserts every polyline round-trips **bit-identically**; Poppler's `pdftotext` independently recovers every text run. Deliberately absent, stated: `Add(Sketch)` (PDF paths are lines + cubics, a circular arc has no exact form, so an overload would silently flatten), PDF layers (needs optional-content groups; filed), Flate (filed as opt-in for huge sheets), and a CLI route (sheets are code/docs-produced for SVG and DXF alike). Two layout bugs the rendered sheet caught that no unit test could: sizing the view grid from the three orthographic views alone lets the isometric (always widest, being a diagonal of the whole box) spill over its neighbours, and a label that clears the line work still lands on a dimension VALUE. Core gained **`Intersect3d.RayTriangle`** beside `Distance3d` for the same reason `ClosestPointOnTriangle` moved there — it had been written twice (`ScenePick`, `AmbientOcclusion`) and the copies differed in exactly the place the split now makes explicit: the predicate is shared, the acceptance range is the caller's. **Docs site**: **Astro Starlight** under `docs/site/` (landing page + getting started + writing-examples + **54 example pages**), with **DocFX reduced to the generated .NET API reference** published as a static subtree at `/api/`, and the **live WASM demo** at `/live/`; CI merges the three trees into one `_site`. The content is **executable examples** — `tools/EngrCAD.DocsGen` extracts ` ```csharp render:<id> ` fences from the markdown, compiles/runs each via Roslyn scripting (snippet must define `Scene scene`), renders through `EngrCad.RenderToImage`, and writes `docs/examples/images/<id>.png`; nonzero exit on any snippet failure, so examples can't rot (369 snippets, 177 rendered; PNGs committed so the site builds without a GPU; `render:` fences may declare `sectionPlanes`/`sectionCombine`/`camera`/`explode`/`preview` as snippet variables — `preview` is a `ConstructionPreviewRequest`, so a docs page can render a construction-tree rollback view headlessly). **The markdown deliberately did NOT move into `src/content/docs/`** (a custom `glob` loader with `base: '../'` reads it in place): DocsGen's root is an argument, so leaving the content in `docs/` keeps its contract, the fence syntax and every committed image path untouched — which is what makes **"all 134 committed PNGs byte-identical"** a statement about the migration rather than a coincidence of it. **The migration's real hazard is that a page that was a FILE became a DIRECTORY**, so every relative cross-page link is wrong by one level and 404s silently; two mechanisms answer it — a rehype plugin (`rewrite-doc-links.mjs`) resolves each `*.md` href against the FILESYSTEM, rewrites it to the served route and **throws when the target does not exist** (so the markdown stays GitHub-navigable and a rename fails the build naming both ends), and `check-links.mjs` validates the BUILT site, resolving every `href`/`src` the way a browser does and checking fragments against emitted ids. `starlight-links-validator` was tried and **cannot work here**: it derives page ids as `relative(srcDir + '/content/docs', filePath)`, one hard-coded assumption, so all 134 internal links read as invalid at once — and checking the OUTPUT proved the better instrument anyway (it covers `<img>`/`<iframe>`/raw-HTML too, and it caught a bug in ITSELF that a markdown-AST checker would have hidden: `dirname` of a slash-terminated page URL drops a segment, which made `web.md`'s two genuinely broken `../live/` links pass). Both guards are pinned by being SHOWN TO FIRE. Three smaller rules: `passthroughImageService()` is a CORRECTNESS setting, not a performance one — Astro's default sharp pass would re-encode PNGs and silently drop every frame of the committed **APNGs** (verified by SHA-256 through the build); the **sidebar in `astro.config.mjs` is the one source of navigation order** so `docs/toc.yml`/`examples/toc.yml` are deleted rather than parsed, with the checker asserting every built page appears in the rendered nav; and the **base path is read from the deployment** (`actions/configure-pages`' `base_path`, `/EngrCAD` for a project site and empty for a custom domain) so the repository name is never written into the source, while the demo iframe stays RELATIVE (`../../live/`) because its own artifact is path-portable. New toolchain dependency, stated: **Node ≥ 22.12** (CI pins 24) beside the .NET SDK, versions pinned exactly in `docs/site/package.json` with a committed lockfile; `cd docs/site && npm ci && npm run build` (= `astro build && node check-links.mjs`), `npm run dev` to preview. `.github/workflows/docs.yml` builds + deploys via actions/deploy-pages (windows runner for WARP rendering; Chris must set repo Settings → Pages → Source: GitHub Actions before first deploy). See design.md §8b. **Live documentation examples** (`tools/EngrCAD.DocsGen/LiveExamples.cs` + `src/EngrCAD.Web/LiveExample.cs` + `docs/site/src/live-examples.mjs` + the demo's `?example=<id>`; design.md §8c): every example screenshot whose snippet can run in a browser now carries a **Run it in your browser** button that swaps the picture for the kernel building THAT model in the reader's tab. **The PNG stays and stays the default, and the viewer activates on a CLICK** — the committed PNGs are the repo's regression oracle for anything that changes what a reader sees (an AO change moved 7 of 106; a render refactor was validated by all 108 being byte-identical), the app is **2.84 MB brotli** at ~19× native, and a reader who never clicks should not pay it; with no JavaScript the page is exactly the screenshot it always was. **The browser gets an ASSEMBLY, not source and not a baked mesh**: DocsGen already compiles every snippet, so it compiles each one a SECOND time against exactly the assemblies `EngrCAD.Web` ships and emits what it compiled (mean **6.0 KB**, max 12.0, 710 KB for all 118) — which is the option the backlog's own assessment had rejected as "shipping Roslyn to WASM", wrongly: the compile does not have to happen in the browser, and doing it at build time keeps the property the recommended alternative gives up, that **a page's interactive block IS its code fence, run**, rather than a second representation of the same model. **The reference set IS the rule**, so the C# compiler answers "can a reader run this?" and cannot go stale against a payload change: `EngrCAD.Fea`, the desktop viewer's `EngrCad.RenderToImage`/`ConstructionPreviewRequest` and the docs-only `Scratch` global simply do not compile there and the refusal carries the compiler's own words into the committed manifest (**134 of 150** as measured today; the rest are recorded with reasons). The one thing a reference set cannot catch is an EMPTY browser filesystem, which is a short named list resolved through the **semantic model** — `heightmaps.md` names `Heightmap.ReadPng` in a comment while computing its grid, and a substring scan refuses it wrongly. **Two findings.** A **globals-less script cannot see `object`'s statics**: Roslyn puts the globals type's members in scope, inherited ones included, so a submission compiled with no globals fails on a bare `ReferenceEquals(a, b)` — legal in every ordinary C# class and used by `chamfer-fillet.md` — and passing `object` as the globals type restores exactly that scope and nothing else (`Scratch` still absent, no reference added), taking the live count 117 → 118. And **the trimmer is why it works, by DEFAULT**: a dynamically loaded example calls the kernel by reflection, Blazor WASM trims in `partial` mode leaving assemblies not marked `IsTrimmable` alone, and none of ours is — so the demo now lists them as `TrimmerRootAssembly` to say it out loud, measured to cost nothing. The **submission ABI** is what neither side's compiler checks — a static `<Factory>(object[])` parking the submission INSTANCE in slot 1, with every top-level variable a FIELD on it, exactly how `ScriptState.Variables` finds `scene` — so `tests/EngrCAD.DocsGen.Tests` emits, loads, runs and compares the GEOMETRY against its own closed form. The committed artifact is the deterministic manifest (no timings, no byte counts — the informational-field rule); the assemblies are gitignored build output the demo's publish picks up, with CI asserting every live id has its file (losing them is otherwise silent: the buttons still appear and all 404). The poster's iframe URL is relative AND built in the browser from a `data-src`, so the app stays path-portable and `check-links.mjs` never sees a reference to the `live/` tree CI merges in afterwards — what it DOES check is the one thing a data attribute can still get wrong, that the `../` count resolves to `<base>live/` from a page at any depth (shown to fire). **ECAD stage 1 — code-defined schematics and the connectivity data model** (`EngrCAD.Ecad`, a new kernel-tier project over Core + Modeling, no viewer; docs `examples/ecad-schematics.md`; the campaign's later stages — board + placement, positioning constraints, copper DRC, routing, MID/LDS — are filed in todo.md under the ECAD entry): a `Schematic` declares `Component`s from reusable `PartDefinition`s (an ordered `Pin` list with an electrical `PinType`, a `Footprint`/`Pad` layout the board stage will consume, and an OPTIONAL `Func<Shape>` body hook) and connects their pins into `Net`s, and **the object graph IS the netlist** — there is no second editable netlist to drift, and `Netlist` is a DERIVED read-only view (`ToNetlist()`, recomputed each call). `NetKind` makes NoConnect and Stub first-class states rather than nulls, which is what makes the floating-net check meaningful rather than noisy. Verification is combinatorial and exact (`Schematic.Check`): the **counting identity** — `TotalPins == PinsCoveredOnce`, every terminal on exactly one signal/stub/no-connect net, exposed numerically so it can be asserted — with a floating pin and a short across two nets each NAMED, plus no-floating-signal-net and no-empty-net, every guard shown to FIRE. Persistence follows the document model's seam (a `WriteIndented` JSON tree, write-only-when-stated, no informational field that cannot round-trip): a `PartDefinition` used by many components is written ONCE and shared by identity so a net REFERENCES a pin rather than copying it (the interning `Scene.AllParts`/`BrepArchive` rule), `save→load→save` is a byte-identical fixed point (verified) and two loads produce structurally identical graphs, and a component whose definition the file does not contain — or a net naming a missing component or pin — is refused BY NAME at load (the definition is the source; the `HoleSpec`/catalogue "an instance without it is not loadable" rule). The `Body` is code and does not travel; a `PartLibrary` re-attaches it by definition name on load (the `ResolveOpaqueFeature` pattern), which is why a data-only load is honest and complete for connectivity. One design finding worth keeping: **net names are GLOBALLY unique across kinds**, so `Connect` extends the signal net of its name but a signal net cannot shadow a NoConnect/Stub name and the auto `NCn` names skip any a user already took — a lone name map, checked, rather than one map per kind that would let a signal net and a no-connect quietly share a name. **ECAD stage 2 — the board and its parts as an Assembly** (`PcbBoard`/`PcbStackup`/`CopperLayerSpec`/`BoardHole`/`KeepOut`, `PcbLayout`/`PcbPlacement`/`PlacedPad`/`CopperLayer`, `IdfReader`/`PcbImport`/`IdfWriter`; the stage-1 `Pad` extended with `PadKind`+`DrillDiameter`; docs `examples/ecad-pcb.md`, design.md §6d): the same one-declaration rule lifted onto copper — the schematic graph is the single source and the board plate, the footprint placement and the 3D bodies all DERIVE from it, with pin `1` ↔ pad `1` as ONE identity. **The plate is built with the existing `Shape` API and its volume is a closed-form oracle**: a `PcbBoard` is a polygon outline + thickness + copper stackup (two-layer default, N via `Layers`) + its own mounting-holes/vias/keep-outs, `Plate()` is `Shape.Extrude` of the outline drilled by `Shape.Drill`, so `ExpectedPlateVolume` = `area·t − Σπr²·t` (matched to ~1e-4 relative through `Part.MassProperties`' Richardson route, the un-drilled prism exact to 1e-6). **The placement transform IS the assembly's own transform math**: `PcbLayout` places components at `(x, y, rotationDegrees, side)` and `WorldOf(placement)` is **bit-identical** to the `PartInstance.World` that `Assembly.Flatten` produces (both `Posed(occ,0).Then(root).ToMatrix() * partTransform`), because the pose is a proper `Frame3d` and the bottom-side reflection lives on the component's `Part.Transform` (`Matrix4d.CreateScale((1,1,-1))`, a reflection across the board plane) — the **FlipX-not-FlipZ** doctrine in this domain: the reflection is spent on the PART so the board's own +Z (world up) is never negated, the body hangs below the board, `Mirror(Mirror(x)) == x`, and a through-hole pad keeps the same world `(x, y)` on both faces (a plated hole serves both). A through-hole component **drills the plate by exactly its hole cylinders** while an SMD component **drills nothing** (its plate is bit-identical to the bare board). **`Check()` is the geometric lift of the schematic's pin-counting identity** — every pin of every placed component resolves to exactly one placed pad at a known copper location (`PlacedPinCount == PlacedPadCount`, every pin covered once), with a pad-without-a-pin / pin-without-a-pad / pad-off-board / hole-in-keep-out / missing-footprint each NAMED, an unknown or repeated placement refused by name at `Place`, and `PadsOfNet(net)` resolving a net to its copper regions (the seam DRC/routing consume). `ToAssembly` is one `Part` per (definition, side) at N occurrences (so `Bom.For` counts correctly), a body-less component still places its pads. `Save`/`Load` **embeds the schematic** (the source, not a copy — the `SchematicWriter.BuildRoot`/`ReadObject` object seam) and is a byte-identical fixed point, with the `Pad` extension **backward-compatible** (`Kind`/`DrillDiameter` write-only-when-stated, `PadKind.Smd = 0`, so a stage-1 SMD footprint saves byte-identically). **IDF 4.0 import** reads a board `.emn` into a `PcbImport` (outline/thickness/holes/placements/keep-outs), honouring the header unit (MM/THOU → mm, recorded in `Diagnostics` — the `IgesReader`/`$INSUNITS` lesson) and refusing a malformed `.SECTION`/`.END` structure BY NAME (the `StlReader` rule); IDF carries **no connectivity**, so `ToLayout` synthesizes a data-only schematic (a component per placement) which is honest (the identity check reports the missing footprints), and `IdfWriter` closes the loop so `read → write → read → write` is a byte fixed point for the geometry IDF carries (v1 flattens arc outlines / drops cutout loops / ignores the `.emp` with a diagnostic). **ECAD stage 3 — placement constraints** (`PcbConstraints.cs`/`ConstrainedLayout.cs`/`PcbConstraintSolver.cs`/`PcbConstraintFile.cs`; docs `examples/ecad-constraints.md`, design.md §6d, README): components placed by CONSTRAINT rather than typed coordinates — the variables are each free placement's rigid 2D pose `(x, y, θ)`, `layout.Constrain()` builds a `ConstrainedLayout`, and `Solve()` returns a NEW `PcbLayout` at the solved poses (the copper/drills/nets/bodies DERIVE from it, so `Solved.Check()` still passes and `Solved.PadsOfNet` returns the moved copper — the one-declaration identity survives, asserted). **The load-bearing decision was to build a FOCUSED solver rather than reuse the Modeling one, and it is about the VARIABLE MODEL not effort**: the sketch and mate LM engines are internal/private and bound to their own variables (`MateSolver`'s `Solver` to 3D 6-DOF `Occurrence` frames, `SketchLevenberg` to free 2D POINT coordinates), and a placement is neither — a rigid 2D pose whose rotation moves the WHOLE footprint about its origin — so `PcbConstraintSolver` is that LM core rebuilt at 2D following the MateSolver doctrine EXACTLY (analytic Jacobian; every residual a LENGTH, angular ones scaled by the board diagonal and the rotation variable divided by it so one linear tolerance covers the system and every column is O(1); rank/DOF from a diagonally pivoted Cholesky of JᵀJ at the **1e-6 relative floor** — the sketch-constraint floor, not the mate 1e-8, since 1e-8² sits below elimination round-off at layout sizes; the drawn layout as seed AND branch selector; under-constrained reported, contradictions and stationary configurations NAMED, a failed solve leaving the source bit-identically unchanged), with no Modeling solver touched and a shared generic 2D-rigid core FILED (no third consumer yet). The vocabulary: `Lock`/`Fix` (a datum grounds a body), `Group`/`Cluster` (several placements as ONE rigid body — a THEOREM not a special case: each member carries a fixed offset from the body frame off the drawn layout, so a singleton reproduces its own pose bit-identically and a group rotated 90° carries a (5,0) offset to (0,5) preserving the relative pose exactly), `Orient`/`FixRotation`, `Distance`/`Spacing`, `AlignX`/`AlignY` (share a column/row), `Parallel`/`Perpendicular`, `PointOnLine` (a SIGNED offset — the point-on-line-is-distance-at-zero rule), `AlignEdge` (a component side flush or at a gap to a board edge/another side, the side read off the drawing), `InsideRegion`/`InsideBoard` and `ClearOf`/`ClearOfRegion`/`ClearOfKeepOut`. **Inequalities are ACTIVE-SET residuals, not fake equalities** — `min(g, 0)` pushes only when violated and adds no rank while inactive (so it leaves DOF free, the honest report) — over a footprint modelled by the smallest circle about its origin enclosing its pads (rotation-invariant, conservative, so keeping the circle clear keeps the copper clear, verified against the true pad regions via one point-to-polygon signed distance). Body creation order is a pure function of the layout, so the solve is deterministic (two identical solves produce byte-identical solved layouts). Verified higher than usual (ECAD fails plausibly): a satisfied set converges to the weld tier with the DOF reported (a Distance leaves 2 of 3 free, named); `AlignEdge` makes the edges EXACTLY parallel (cross → 0) and collinear (geometric assertions); a stated pad `Spacing` met exactly; `ClearOfRegion` leaves every pad disjoint with the footprint standing the full clearance clear (measured against an INDEPENDENT point-to-polygon distance); a `ClearOf`/`Group`/scale-1000× family all converge; the contradiction/stationary refusals fire; a failed solve is bit-identical through the serializer; determinism to the bit. **Persistence extends the stage-2 seam**: `ConstrainedLayout.Save`/`Load` adds a `constraints` array write-only-when-stated, so a layout with NO constraints is byte-identical to a stage-2 file and a constrained one is a `save → load → save` byte fixed point (every captured value — a signed offset, an `AlignEdge` side — rides as DATA, a stored direction kept VERBATIM per the `AxisRef` ulp rule). **ECAD stage 4 — the copper DRC** (`DrcRuleSet`/`PcbCopperModel`/`CopperFeature`/`DrilledHole`/`PcbDrc.Check` → `DrcReport`; docs `examples/ecad-drc.md`, design.md §6d, README): an exact 2D-region query over a board's copper against a standard rule table (min copper clearance / trace width / annular ring / drill-to-copper / copper-to-edge + an acute-angle acid-trap threshold, nominal IPC-2221 defaults ⚠ verify-against-datasheet like `StandardHoles`), every rule NAMING, LOCATING and MEASURING its offender against its limit (a report that only said "fail" would be useless — the `PcbLayoutCheck` house style). **The load-bearing rule is that the DRC reads the NETLIST to decide what should connect** — a SHORT is copper of DIFFERENT nets electrically connected, SAME-net copper touching is the intended connection and is never flagged (the one-declaration identity: a pad's net IS its pin's net, resolved by `PcbCopperModel.FromLayout`; only Signal/Stub nets JOIN their pins, a NoConnect/unconnected pin carries a null net — its own group — so two floating pads must clear each other, and a drill never clears its OWN pad, skipped by SOURCE not net so an unconnected through-hole pad is not flagged against its own copper). **Clearance is the tamper-mesh construction** — group each net's copper, grow it by HALF the clearance (`CurvedRegion2dOffset`), require different-net grown regions DISJOINT (`CurvedRegion2dBoolean.Intersection` empty PROVES the clearance); a positive-area overlap of the UNGROWN copper is a SHORT (the stronger failure), an AABB gap ≥ clearance is a SOUND broad-phase skip (an AABB is a superset of its region, so the region gap is at least the box gap), and the MEASURED gap for the report is a BISECTION over the SAME grow-and-intersect the pass/fail rests on (run only on the few violations) so the number and the verdict cannot disagree. Annular ring is arithmetic `(min pad − drill)/2`; drill-to-copper grows the drill disc against other-net copper on EVERY layer (cross-layer); copper-to-edge subtracts the outline shrunk by the clearance from each feature (exact polygon inward offset, catching copper past the edge too); trace width is `Region2dThickness`' opposing-wall neck; the acute-angle rule is the wedge angle between two copper edges at a joint measured EITHER SIDE (a copper spike AND an etchant-side notch both flag, a smooth arc joint and a 90° pad corner passing under the default 90° strict-`<` threshold). Multi-layer: clearance/shorts/width/acute are PER LAYER, drill-to-copper CROSS-LAYER. **The ratsnest is the INVERSE of a short** — a signal net whose copper is >1 connected region is UNROUTED (union each net's copper across layers, a through-hole pad overlapping itself so a via connects layers), reported as INFORMATION not a fault (a bare-pads board before routing is unrouted, not wrong; `DrcReport.Ok` ignores it). Traces arrive in stage 5 and the DRC needs no change to reach them — a trace is a stroked centre-line region through the same `CopperFeature` type, and `PcbDrc.Violates(model, candidate, rules)` is the incremental seam a router costs a candidate route with (clearance/short on its layer, edge, width, acute), so the DRC is a routing cost function rather than only a final gate. A `DrcRuleSet` is a STANDALONE checking parameter (a plain record of six numbers), NOT baked into the layout file — a board's rules belong to the fabricator's capability sheet — so the stage-2/3 layout files stay byte-identical (nothing persisted changed). Verified higher than usual (ECAD fails plausibly): a known clearance violation FOUND and a near miss at clearance + ε PASSED, both against the CLOSED-FORM gap (two Ø1 pads, gap = centre distance − 1); the clearance proof asserted DIRECTLY (grown intersection empty on a pass, non-empty on a fail); a short NAMING BOTH NETS while two same-net overlapping pads are never flagged, and two UNCONNECTED pads ARE (the one-declaration assertion with teeth); annular ring / copper-to-edge / drill-to-copper checked from both sides of their limit; a drill not flagged against its own pad; scale-invariant at 1e-3× and 1e3× (relative tolerances); deterministic; and the placed stage-2 fixture DRC-clean with an unrouted ratsnest of both its signal nets. **ECAD stage 4b — multilayer stackups and embedded components** (`LayerStackup`/`StackLayer`/`Embedding`/`EmbeddedCavity`; the `PcbPlacement` extended with `Layer`/`Embedding`/`CavityClearance`; `DrcRule.CavityClearance`; docs `examples/ecad-pcb.md`, design.md §6d, README): the copper-only `PcbStackup` (named copper PLANES at z-heights) generalizes to the full physical build-up — an ordered list of copper AND dielectric `StackLayer`s each with a thickness — while the copper-only / surface path stays **byte-identical** (a board built the old way carries a null `LayerStackup`, and its plate + serialization are unchanged). **`TotalThickness` is the sum of every layer and each copper plane's z is DERIVED by ONE contact rule**: the outer coppers at the board's faces (top at `TotalThickness`, bottom at exactly 0), inner coppers at their own midplanes — and the accumulation is BOTTOM-UP so the endpoints are exact (accumulating from the top and subtracting leaves the bottom face at `total − Σt`, which is 0 only to round-off, measured −2.2e-16 on a six-layer stack). `CopperStackup` is the derived `PcbStackup`, so every stage-2..4 consumer reads a multilayer board through the seam it already reads. **A placement generalizes to also carry a seat `Layer` and an `Embedding`** (`Surface` = the struct default so the record serializes byte-identically; `Enclosed`/`OpenCavity` via a new `Embed` method), seating a component on an inner copper layer at its z inside a cavity milled into the plate — an ENCLOSED cavity is a box tool fully interior (an internal void, build-up intact above and below, so the die is buried) and an OPEN cavity a box overshooting the placement's face (a well), **both exact** because a box cuts planar faces the B-Rep boolean handles at machine precision (measured rel ~1e-16, closed manifold), with the removed volume a closed form (`lateral area × depth-inside`) so `ExpectedPlateVolume` stays the plate's oracle less each cavity. **Three properties carry it**: the containment oracle is against the outer extruded prism (an enclosed body strictly inside `outline × [0, total]`, a surface body proud); overlap is a 3D test — z-intervals AND a separating-axis test on the two rotated cavity rectangles — so two cavities on DIFFERENT inner layers with disjoint z are allowed (stacked dies) while two on one layer are refused, which yields an EMERGENT minimum-spacing property (a cavity is footprint-plus-clearance, larger than the pads, so non-overlapping cavities put pads ≥ 2·clearance apart — which is why a short between two embedded parts on one layer is unreachable through `Embed`, the overlap refusal firing first); and every refusal fires at `Embed` before the placement is recorded (unknown layer, negative clearance, missing footprint/body, a cavity breaching a surface or the outline, an overlap — all BY NAME). **The one-declaration identity holds across layers and the DRC is N-layer aware for free**: the DRC already iterates all stackup coppers, so once `FromLayout` puts an embedded SMD pad on its inner seat layer (`TargetLayerName`), inner-layer clearance/shorts are checked with no new code (an inner-layer clearance violation found, an inner short named both nets, a clean multilayer board zero); the one new rule is `CavityClearance` — a cavity wall is a milled edge, so OTHER copper on its seat layer clears it by the copper-to-edge minimum (the embedded part's own pads exempt, inside their own cavity by construction), v1 checking the SEAT layer only. **v1's identity is per the pad's own layer** — cross-layer via/microvia stitching is a later stage — so a net whose pads sit on different layers reads as an unrouted ratsnest until routing; stated rather than hidden. Persistence writes the full `layerStackup` (or the copper `stackup`) and the placement's `layer`/`embedding`/`cavityClearance` write-only-when-stated, so a stage-2..4 file is byte-identical and a multilayer/embedded one a `save → load → save` fixed point, a placement naming a missing layer refused BY NAME at load. **Exploded views** (`PcbLayout.ToExplodedAssembly(spacing?, name?)` + `LayerStackup.Extents`; `PcbExplode.cs`; docs `examples/ecad-pcb.md` animate fence + committed APNG, design.md §6d): a `LayerStackup` board is a SANDWICH the kernel takes apart — the plate sliced into ONE slab per physical `StackLayer` (outline extruded over that layer's own z-range from `LayerStackup.Extents`, the SAME bottom-up accumulation the copper z's come off exposed rather than recomputed, drilled by every through hole and milled by every overlapping cavity) and assembled with the placed components, fanned along the STACKUP NORMAL. Sibling of `ToAssembly` (untouched); returns an ordinary `Assembly`, so the explode slider, `ExplodeTrack` and every exporter drive it with NO new code (the offsets are Modeling-level `Occurrence.ExplodeOffset`/`ExplodePath`, so no viewer dependency). **The explode is decided by the one relationship a board has, its z-stacking**: layers fan up from the BOTTOM layer as the DATUM (it stays put — the natural datum since the stackup accumulates from z = 0, and a centroid is meaningless for a sandwich), a layer's offset `n·gap·rank` making STACK ORDER = explode order, and because the offset adds to the layer's ORIGINAL contiguous position the thicknesses CANCEL so `gap` is the clean empty gap between consecutive layers whatever their thickness (a 4 mm core and a 35 µm film get the same gap); surface components lift off their face pure-Z (top up clear of the fan, bottom down below the datum); embedded components DOGLEG out of their cavity (leg 1 pure ±n straight out, the FINAL offset carrying the lateral step so the die does not tunnel through the layers above it — the lateral leg IS the dogleg, which is why an embedded offset is the one not pure-Z, a pure-Z endpoint with a "straight up first" leg being a provable collinear no-op). **The reassembly is EXACT**: the extents tile `[0, TotalThickness]` (endpoints on the faces exactly, contiguous), so the slabs are DISJOINT and their union IS the plate (`Σ slab volume == ExpectedPlateVolume`, closed-form and verified to mass-properties grade on the tessellated slabs); at factor 0 every component's world transform is bit-identical to `ToAssembly`'s, the instance count/order are factor-independent (matrices only), and the build is deterministic. A copper-only board (null `LayerStackup`) and a negative spacing are refused BY NAME; explode offsets are a VIEW concern kept OUT of the layout file (the save→load→save byte fixed point untouched, the `DrcRuleSet` rule). **ECAD vias + cross-layer connectivity — the routing prerequisite** (`Via`/`ViaType`/`PlacedVia`; `PcbLayout.AddVia`/`WithVia`/`ViaTypeOf`/`ViaLayers`/`Connectivity`; `PcbConnectivity`/`NetConnectivity`/`ConnectivityReport`; `DrcRule.ViaToVia` + `DrcRuleSet.MinViaToVia`; docs `examples/ecad-pcb.md`, design.md §6d, README): a `Via` is a net-carrying plated cross-layer connection at `(x, y)` spanning copper layers `[From, To]` with a drill and an annular pad diameter, and **the via TYPE is DERIVED from the span, not stored twice** — `ViaGeometry.Resolve` against the stackup decides THROUGH (outer face to outer face) / BLIND (one outer face) / BURIED (neither) / MICROVIA, THROUGH first then MICROVIA (a single dielectric hop, adjacent copper layers) taking precedence over blind/buried since a microvia is a physically distinct single-hop via however its ends fall; there is no explicit microvia a caller declares across non-adjacent layers, and `AddVia(..., require:)` validates an INTENT against the derived type and refuses a mismatch BY NAME (the "non-adjacent-for-microvia" refusal, discarded rather than a second copy). **Via copper feeds `PcbCopperModel`, so most of the via DRC is FREE**: a via places an annular pad (a disc of the pad diameter with the drill removed — a `CurvedRegion2d` whose one hole is the drill circle, so its `Area` is exactly π(pad²−drill²)/4) on EVERY touched copper layer tagged with its net, plus one drilled hole — so the general clearance rule spaces a via pad against different-net copper (a via pad is copper), drill-to-copper spaces the via drill (a via drill is a hole), the annular-ring rule checks the via (a via is a drilled pad), and a same-net via on its own copper is the intended connection, never flagged; the ONE new rule is `ViaToVia`, the minimum drill WEB applied to all via pairs regardless of net (a manufacturing spacing; different-net via PAD clearance is already the copper-clearance rule), measured by the SAME grow-and-intersect the clearance rule uses so a web AT the limit passes robustly, `MinViaToVia` riding `DrcRuleSet` as a value with a default OUTSIDE the positional constructor (a stage-4 caller is unaffected). **`PcbConnectivity` is the heart and CLOSES the multilayer caveat**: a per-net graph over the net's copper features where two features join when they TOUCH on a layer (an exact `CurvedRegion2dBoolean.Intersection`, no tolerance — the same query the DRC calls a SHORT) OR are the two ends of a PLATED BARREL (features sharing a source across layers — a via's annular pads, or a through-hole pad's per-layer copies, obey the identical rule, needing no flag on `CopperFeature`), a net CONNECTED when all its COMPONENT PADS (not the via pads — connectors, not terminals, so a floating via never makes a connected net read unconnected) lie in one component; `PcbDrc.Ratsnest` now DELEGATES to it, so "a net whose pads sit on different layers reads as an unrouted ratsnest until routing" is answered by geometry — a via that touches each pad is a real connection. The old ratsnest was a 2D-projection union across layers (right for a through-hole pad by luck, wrong in general); the layer-aware graph is strictly more correct and bit-compatible for every no-via board (verified by the whole stage-4 suite passing). A via on the WRONG net does not connect it; a via elsewhere does not connect distant same-layer pads unless its copper reaches them. **Vias are LAYOUT TRUTH** (unlike the view/analysis `DrcRuleSet` and explode offsets), so a `vias` array round-trips write-only-when-stated — a via-free layout byte-identical, a via one a save→load→save fixed point — verified higher than usual (type derived per span order-independently, through touching all N layers and buried only its inner span, the annular pad area exact, every refusal by name, the connectivity headline from BOTH sides, wrong-net/floating/third-location vias not connecting, connectivity as EXACT region touch, via annular-ring/via-to-copper/via-to-via from both sides, same-net not flagged, scale-invariant, deterministic). **v1 does NOT cut the via drill into the 3D plate B-Rep** (vias are the copper/connectivity/DRC model; the plate stays the mechanical outline + mounting holes, bit-identical), the safe scope that satisfies every layer-touching oracle. **ECAD stage 5 — the autorouter** (`PcbTrace`/`PcbRouter`/`RouterOptions`/`RoutedResult`; docs `examples/ecad-routing.md`, design.md §6d, README): the genuinely hard stage — turn the ratsnest into DRC-clean copper. `PcbRouter.Route(layout, rules, options)` is a DRC-aware grid/maze A* router: a uniform routing grid `(x, y, layer)`, through-vias to change layers, 2-pin MST decomposition of multi-pin nets, and rip-up-and-reroute. **The bar is that an autorouter that connects while VIOLATING clearance is the classic silent failure**, so the router is built so that cannot happen — every routed net CONNECTS its pins AND passes the exact DRC, or it is reported UNROUTABLE by name. **The grid is an ACCELERATOR; the exact DRC is the SOURCE OF TRUTH**: a candidate route is committed only after `PcbDrc.Violates` (plus the drill/via rules the incremental seam does not carry — drill-to-copper, via-to-via, mirroring `PcbDrc.Check` so the two cannot disagree) confirms it adds NO violation, so a grid rounding error can never produce a violating trace, the PARTIAL result of a board it cannot fully route is still clean, and the exact check wins over the grid on disagreement. **A `PcbTrace` IS the DRC's clearance model** — a net's routed copper on one layer, a polyline centre-line stroked (`CurvedRegion2dOffset.Stroke`, round caps/joins = the Minkowski sum with a disc of radius `width/2`) into exactly the region the clearance rule grows against, so the two cannot disagree and the round joins carry no acute corner (the acid-trap rule passes with nothing arranged); it feeds `PcbCopperModel` and `PcbConnectivity`, which reads a trace (and a via) as a CONNECTOR not a terminal, so a net is CONNECTED when its component PADS end up in one copper component. **Rip-up is negotiated congestion, not reorder-restart, and that was MEASURED**: a reorder-restart re-routes all nets in a new order when one fails, and a search over thousands of small congested boards found it almost never completes a board a greedy pass cannot (A*'s own per-net detour already resolves a 2-net conflict, and where it cannot a reorder cannot either), so the router routes a blocked net ACROSS the traces that block it (SOFT grid obstacles — a per-cell bitmask of committed nets; base pads and the board edge are HARD, never rippable), rips those up and re-queues them, and re-routes cleanly without them — bounded per net so a boxed-in net terminates and is reported unroutable rather than looping. **Verified higher than usual because ECAD fails plausibly** (`PcbRouterTests`): a 2-pin net connects + DRC-clean + ratsnest empty; several parallel nets all route clean; a net that MUST cross another gets a via, both clean/connected (and WITHOUT vias the crossing is unroutable by name, the rest clean); a congested board a greedy pass leaves unrouted is COMPLETED by rip-up (both clean, `RipUps ≥ 1` — the measured demonstration that rip-up earns its place); a walled-in pin is unroutable by name with the rest routed and clean; a dense one-layer knot cannot fully route and whatever it manages is DRC-clean with failures named (never a silent violation); two runs byte-identical (determinism); nothing-to-route returns byte-identical `Save()`; routed traces round-trip `save → load → save`; scale-invariant at 1e-3×/1e3×. Traces are LAYOUT TRUTH, so a `traces` array rides in the layout file write-only-when-stated (an un-routed layout stays byte-identical). **v1 scope**: through-vias only (all copper layers — always valid, exactly right for a 2-layer board); NOT topological/shove/push routing, length matching, differential pairs, copper POUR/planes with thermal reliefs, teardrops, cavity walls as obstacles. **ECAD stage 6 — Gerber (RS-274X) + Excellon fabrication export** (`PcbGerberExport`/`GerberWriter`/`GerberReader`/`ExcellonWriter`/`ExcellonReader`; docs `examples/ecad-fabrication.md`, design.md §6d stage 6, README): the fab output that makes a routed board manufacturable — one copper Gerber per layer + a board-outline Gerber + an Excellon drill program, from a routed `PcbLayout` (or a raw `PcbCopperModel` for pours). Pads FLASH (`D03`), traces DRAW (`D01`/`D02` with a round aperture, whose swept stroke IS the copper model's trace region, so a trace and the DRC's clearance model cannot disagree), via pads flash as solid discs, anything else (a rotated pad, a copper pour) is a region FILL (`G36`/`G37`) exact for any shape. **The oracle is the TWIN-DECODER round trip** (the repo's rule — the geometry must survive the round trip, not merely a structural validator): a matching `GerberReader`/`ExcellonReader` parses the output BACK, and the recovered copper equals the copper model's per layer to the region-area grade — by area AND by symmetric difference through the DRC's own `CurvedRegion2dBoolean` — on a hand-built AND an autorouted board, with the drill hits exact. **The imaging order is the whole design and reproduces a UNION exactly**: the copper on a layer is a union of feature regions, so a via drill (or a pour hole) is a hole ONLY where nothing covers it — a via under a routing trace, or a via directly under a pad (a via-in-pad), is FILLED — so the writer lays all the solid copper down (dark) then clears exactly the HOLES OF THE FINAL UNION (which the caller already computed), which stays correct at every via where the naive "clear each via drill" opens a hole the model fills. **The crossing fixture demonstrates it**: its SIG via lands under a pad and its partner under the trace ending on it, so the top layer has ZERO exposed drills and a correct exporter emits NO clear there at all (the first draft cleared them and came back one drill area short, 0.126 mm² = πr²). **The coordinate format is derived from the board's own magnitudes** (`GerberFormat.For`, scale-invariant — the epsilon ladder in a file format), and each `%FS` digit field is a SINGLE digit so the two counts are anti-correlated (large coordinates → fewer fractional digits, sum ≈ 11–12, exact in a long); a first version allowed a two-digit fractional count, overflowed the field, and decoded a 1e-3-scale board's coordinates 10^5 too large — the area oracle caught it as a recovered 48000 against a model 5.28e-6, a 10-orders-of-magnitude tell that reads as a format bug rather than a tolerance one. Shape recognition is GEOMETRIC (a pad region read for a disc / axis-aligned rect / axis-aligned obround and flashed, a rotated one region-filled) so a placement rotation never threads through and correctness never depends on recognition (an unrecognised feature region-fills, exact for any shape); a straight axis-aligned trace stroke IS an obround, so the model path recognises it and the obround aperture path is exercised naturally. Refused BY NAME: a Bézier copper boundary (RS-274X region contours carry only lines and circular arcs), and — in the reader, the round-trip oracle scoped to what the writer emits — a truncated file, a missing format/unit spec, and an aperture macro. Not in v1 (each filed): solder-mask / silkscreen / paste layers (the board carries no mask/silk model yet), Gerber X2 attributes and the job file, and a Gerber IMPORT of a foreign board (this is export). **ECAD copper pours — ground / power planes** (`CopperPour`/`CopperPourBuilder`/`ThermalRelief`/`HatchStyle`/`PouredPour`; `PcbLayout.AddPour`; docs `examples/ecad-pcb.md` (Copper pours), design.md §6d): a pour floods a layer on one NET, is LAYOUT TRUTH (rides in the file), and `PcbCopperModel.FromLayout` DERIVES it into copper features the DRC and connectivity read like any other copper — so a GND pour JOINS every GND pad it touches and the GND ratsnest EMPTIES (the headline, a ground plane's whole purpose), needing NO downstream change (the pour is just copper). **The fill is the tamper-mesh construction and clears every other net BY CONSTRUCTION**: (the board area, or a stated outline) inset from the edge, MINUS every other-net copper feature grown by `Clearance` and every other-net/netless drill grown by `DrillClearance` (cross-layer), through `Region2dOffset`/`CurvedRegion2dOffset`/`CurvedRegion2dBoolean` with no tolerance — growing the OBSTACLE by the clearance is the same offset the DRC's clearance rule grows with, so a poured board passes `PcbDrc.Check` and the grown-region intersection with any other net is EMPTY (the proof, asserted directly). **The clearances live on the POUR, not on a rule set** — the fill is a pure function of the pour and the base copper (nothing threads a `DrcRuleSet` through `FromLayout`), the defaults exceed `DrcRuleSet.Default` so a default pour passes the default DRC with margin. **Thermal relief is the substance**: a same-net THROUGH-HOLE pad stays solderable via an annular gap `disc(padR + Gap)` carved out and thin radial SPOKES (four on the diagonals by default) unioned back, overlapping the pad (inner) and the flood (outer) as a robust AREA overlap (a tangent touch is measure-zero and would NOT connect — the connectivity engine's own exact-region-touch rule); the two classic bugs are a relief that DISCONNECTS the pad and one that FLOODS, so a test asserts BOTH — the pad CONNECTED through the spokes (one component in `PcbConnectivity`) AND a point in the gap between spokes carrying NO copper. SMD pads and vias direct-connect (flood); `ThermalRelief.None` floods a THT pad. The one stated cost: a spoke meets the plane at a ~90° corner, so the acid-trap rule's default strict-`<`-90° threshold is borderline on reliefs (a realistic board sets it under 90°; a same-net-SMD pour is smooth and passes the default). **Dead copper** — a component touching NO same-net feature (walled off) — is REMOVED by default (`DeadCopperPolicy.Keep` opts in) and always REPORTED (`PouredPour.DeadCopperArea`); each kept component gets its OWN source (`pour{i}.{j}`) so two disjoint pieces stay disjoint in connectivity (a shared plated-barrel source would force-join pads the copper does not bridge), and pour sources are CONNECTORS (like traces), not terminals. `PourFill.Hatched` intersects the fill with a crosshatch grid (region ∩ a `Stroke`d line pattern), spokes unioned AFTER the hatch so pad connections stay solid. **A pour exports to Gerber** as a `G36`/`G37` region fill and round-trips — which forced a real imaging fix: a pour's clearance hole around an OTHER-net pad contains that pad as a copper ISLAND, so the true air is a RING; clearing the whole hole erased the pad (measured, one anti-pad's copper short per other-net pad), so `PcbGerberExport` now clears the union's holes MINUS the copper and re-DARKENS the island (a via drill is the same with an empty island, so a non-poured board's Gerber is byte-identical). Verified higher than usual (`PcbPourTests`): the GND-pour headline, the DRC-clean empty-grown-intersection, the THT relief connected-AND-gap, the island removed/reported, the pour area a closed form of board/clearance/hole, determinism (bit-identical area), the Gerber round-trip by area and symmetric difference, a save→load→save byte fixed point (pour-free byte-identical), the refusals by name, and scale invariance (area ∝ s²). v1 does no inter-pour PRIORITY (each pour filled against base copper, not other pours), custom relief geometry beyond the spoke default is filed, and conformal placement on a curved MID wall is not offered. **ECAD stage 7 — enclosure fit, the MCAD/ECAD boundary** (`Enclosure`/`EnclosureFit`/`PanelCutout`/`PanelWall`/`EnclosureKeepOut`/`FitReport`/`FitIssue` in `PcbEnclosure.cs`; docs `examples/ecad-enclosure.md`, design.md §6d stage 7, README): does the placed board go in the box? An `Enclosure` is a housing built from the ordinary `Shape` API — a shelled box with the panel cutouts drilled, deliberately NOT a new solid type — with an interior cavity, wall/floor thickness, a board seating height (standoffs), a lid at a stated height (the headroom ceiling), named panel cutouts and interior keep-out volumes; `EnclosureFit.Check(enclosure, layout)` (or `enclosure.Fit(layout)`) returns a `FitReport` naming/locating/measuring every problem (the `DrcViolation` house style). **It REUSES the landed clash machinery** — every body pulled into the interior frame, an instance-bounds broad phase then the transversal `MeshIntersection.Crosses` narrow phase — so a part resting flush on the lid or seated on its standoffs is NOT a clash (touching, not interpenetrating; the flush-lid boundary reads headroom exactly 0). Where the geometry is a plane/rectangle the number is CLOSED-FORM (the board outline against the four cavity walls, each too-large wall named with its overhang; a part's top against the lid, deficit `top − lidZ`, always reported as the scalar `Headroom`); the mesh clash serves the arbitrary cases (a component body vs a wall/floor, a keep-out via surface crossing OR winding-number containment since a small part wholly inside a keep-out crosses no surface). Panel connectors are EXCLUDED from the wall clash (passing through a wall is their job) and checked against the cutout whose `For` names them — the body must reach the wall AND its cross-section fit through the opening, or it is named (centre offset, reach deficit). `Enclosure.SeatFrame()` seats the board in the cavity (one geometry, not two poses); the fit pulls bodies back through `Enclosure.Frame`, so enclosure and board placements are independent. `Enclosure.SmallestFor(layout, clearance, standoff, headroom, wall)` sizes AND places the smallest box the layout fits in place (a starting point, not a generator). Deterministic; refused by name at construction (seat outside cavity, lid below seat, non-positive dims/cutout); round cutouts checked against their bounding box in v1 (exact corner fit filed). MID/LDS stays open. **ECAD stage 8 — thermal coupling, the ECAD/thermal boundary** (`PcbThermal`/`PcbThermalModel`/`PcbThermalSpec`/`PcbThermalBoundary`/`PcbThermalResult`/`PcbMaterials` in `PcbThermal.cs`/`PcbThermalModel.cs`; `EngrCAD.Ecad` now references `EngrCAD.Fea`, a clean leaf; docs `examples/ecad-thermal.md`, design.md §6d stage 8): where does the heat go? `PcbThermal.Solve(layout, spec)` turns a powered board into a heat-conduction problem on the **landed FEA thermal solver** rather than a lumped estimate, so the answers are verifiable against closed forms — each component's dissipation is a volumetric `Generation` source over its footprint, the copper spreads it, a held cold edge or convecting face carries it away, and the result is a temperature field `FieldDisplay` colours plus a hot-spot temperature per component (`PcbThermalResult.ComponentTemperature`/`PeakTemperature`/`SampleOnto`). **v1 is the standard board-level model: an effective conductivity over a SLAB, and the mixing rule is the physics of the sandwich** — the copper is SMEARED (not meshed as discrete traces) into a high in-plane conductivity (the layers are PARALLEL heat paths, `k_in = f·k_Cu + (1−f)·k_FR4`, the Voigt rule of mixtures) and a low through-thickness one (they are in SERIES, `k_th = 1/(f/k_Cu + (1−f)/k_FR4)`, the Reuss harmonic mean), with `f` the copper VOLUME fraction the one honest knob (`CopperFraction`, or `.FromCoverage(board, coverage)`). **A bare board (`f = 0`) collapses both to the isotropic dielectric conductivity** and takes the scalar path bit-for-bit (the anisotropic `ConductivityLaw.Orthotropic` is only set when the two values differ), which is the verification simplification; the slab has NO holes (the smear ignores the copper geometry, so it ignores the drills, keeping the closed-form oracles clean). **One unit crosses the boundary and is converted once**, the `ModelUnits` discipline: power is stated in WATTS (nobody specs a chip in model-milliwatts) and converted to mW at the edge, a film coefficient in SI W/(m²·K) converted ×1e-3, both pinned by the hand-calc-resistance test (a forgotten 1000× shows immediately); held temperatures cross verbatim. Per-component power is a step `Generation` field over the footprint (resultant reported, since a step straddles elements) while a board-wide `BoardPower` is an EXACT uniform generation; BCs (`FixedTemperature`/`Convection`) name a `BoardSurface` (Top/Bottom/Edges/All) or a raw `Facets` selector (the escape hatch to clamp one edge). **Verified against closed forms in the FEA house style** (`PcbThermalTests`, since an ECAD thermal answer fails plausibly): a uniformly-dissipating board to a fixed edge settles into the PARABOLA `T0 + (q/2k)(L²−x²)` reproduced to **3.16e-12 relative** (round-off, quadratic-exact) with applied heat exactly `P × 1000` mW (the units check); a single hot component's far-field matches the series-resistance line `T0 + Q(L−x)/(kA)` to **3.6e-5** (the honest accuracy of a localized step source's 3D discretization) with the ENERGY BALANCE exact (all generated heat leaves the cold edge); real FR4 (k = 0.3) against 2.6 % copper lifts `k_in` 0.3 → 10.4 and drops the peak rise **1129 K → 32.6 K (34.7×)** with the far-field ratio EXACTLY `k_copper/k_bare`; convection is verified by the film unit (a near-isothermal board lands 0.14 % from the lumped `P/(hA)`) plus the exact convected = generated balance; a no-boundary board is refused BY NAME (the `ThermalSolver` convention), a zero-power board is isothermal exactly, and a solve is deterministic to the bit. Filed: thermal VIAS as discrete paths (v1 smears them), a TRANSIENT warm-up (`SolveTransient` exists; the slab already carries a volume-weighted heat capacity), detailed die/package models, and airflow/CFD. **ECAD component interchange — the symbol, and loading from KiCad** (`Symbol`/`SymbolPin`/`SymbolGraphic`, `PinIdentity`, `ComponentLibrary`/`KiCadSymbolReader`/`KiCadFootprintReader` over a hand-rolled `SExpr` parser; docs `examples/ecad-library.md`, design.md §6d "Component interchange", README): a `PartDefinition` used to carry pins + footprint, and the piece a component was MISSING is its 2D schematic **`Symbol`** — the drawn shape a schematic SHEET places, wires to and labels: graphic primitives (`SymbolPolyline`/`SymbolRectangle`/`SymbolCircle`/`SymbolArc`/`SymbolText`) plus one `SymbolPin` per terminal whose load-bearing property is its **`Anchor`** (the connection point where a wire lands), with a `SymbolPinDirection` (KiCad's pin-angle convention — from the anchor INTO the body) and a length; `Symbol` is an OPTIONAL last constructor parameter so every existing positional construction is byte-for-byte unchanged. **The THREE representations are ONE identity by pin NUMBER** — symbol pin "1" == footprint pad "1" == netlist pin "1", the schematic's one-declaration source-of-truth rule extended to the drawn symbol — verified by `PinIdentity.Check` anchored on the definition's `Pin` list, naming every declared pin with no symbol pin/no pad, every symbol pin that is not a pin, and every pad that is not a pin (all four lists empty PROVES the three number-sets equal); absent representations are simply not cross-checked, so the check is only as strong as what the part carries. **Loading is the real use, and KiCad is the interchange**: `.kicad_sym`/`.kicad_mod` are S-expression text, so the readers ride a dependency-free `SExpr` parser in the `StepReader`/`IgesReader` ethos — structure validated up front (unbalanced parenthesis, unterminated string, top-level atom, wrong root tag, absent named symbol, all refused BY NAME), the COMMON subset mapped (symbol `Reference`/`Footprint` properties, nested unit sub-symbols recursed for graphics and pins, `rectangle`/`circle`/`arc`/`polyline`/`text` graphics, `pin`s mapped to the SAME `PinType` the netlist uses; SMD + plated through-hole pads of the standard `circle`/`rect`/`roundrect`/`oval` shapes with `at`/`size`/`drill`), and everything else ignored/approximated WITH A NAMED diagnostic (a bezier, an alternate pin function, a `no_connect`/`free`/`open_emitter` type with no exact `PinType`, a pad rotation, a `trapezoid`/`custom` pad, an oval drill). **No change to `Footprint`/`Pad`/`PadShape` was needed** — the finding: the drill a through-hole pad wants was already added additively in stage 2, and KiCad's four common shapes map onto the existing `PadShape`, so the board side that READS footprints is untouched and the stage-1 SMD footprint round-trips byte-identically; a pad is built via the record constructor rather than the validating `Pad.ThroughHole` factory, so a malformed pad IMPORTS and the DRC reports it (the "readers never throw on dirty geometry" culture). Because a KiCad pad's coordinates are STATED in the file, pad centres and sizes are carried EXACTLY, not to a tolerance. `ComponentLibrary.LoadFromPretty` resolves the `.kicad_mod` from a `.pretty` directory by the symbol's referenced footprint name; a `LoadedPart` carries the assembled `PartDefinition`, its `PinIdentityReport` and the readers' diagnostics. **Persistence extends the seam**: a loaded `Symbol` is DATA now, so `SchematicFile` serializes it write-only-when-stated (graphics by `kind`, pins with number/name/anchor/direction/length/type) with the writer's default arm THROWING on an unknown graphic kind (the Feature-persistence rule), so a `PartDefinition` carrying a symbol is a save→load→save byte-identical fixed point while a symbol-less definition writes no `"symbol"` key at all and every stage-1..5 file stays byte-identical. Verified higher than usual (interchange fails plausibly): a transcribed real 0805 resistor and generic SOIC-8 parse with pin count/names/numbers round-tripping, symbol pins and footprint pads sharing the numbers (identity `Ok`), a deliberately mismatched footprint REPORTED by number, pad geometry matched EXACTLY to the file, the symbol's primitives and pin anchors matched, malformed input refused by name, the persistence fixed point, and determinism. Filed: IPC-7351 footprint GENERATION from a designation (a generator, not an import), EDIF, and the KiCad 3D model reference (`.wrl`/`.step` — path noted, not loaded). **ECAD component interchange — the Eagle `.lbr` reader** (`EagleLibraryReader`/`EagleLibrary`/`EagleDeviceInfo`; docs `examples/ecad-library.md`, design.md §6d, README): the SECOND interchange, producing the SAME `LoadedPart` the KiCad reader does. An Eagle library is one XML file, so it rides the BCL's `System.Xml.Linq` (`XDocument`, dependency-free — the `ThreeMfWriter`/`AmfWriter` precedent for XML) rather than a hand-rolled parser, same refuse-by-name ethos. **The `<connect gate pin pad>` map is what unifies the three, and it is the STRUCTURAL difference from KiCad** (where the file numbers pins directly): an Eagle symbol's pins are named in the symbol's own vocabulary (`"1"`, `"VCC"`), a package's pads are numbered, and a **deviceset**'s `<connect>`s bind them (`"VCC"` → pad `"8"`) — so the loaded pin's NUMBER is the pad, its NAME is the symbol pin's name, and its symbol pin/footprint pad/netlist pin all carry that pad number, which is exactly what `PinIdentity.Check` verifies. `Read(xml)` returns the library's `.Devices` (each named deviceset + device); `.Load(deviceName)` resolves one into a `PartDefinition`. Eagle stores coordinates in the XML in MILLIMETRES, so pad centres and pin anchors are carried EXACTLY, and a pin's `rot` gives its direction (`R0` points +x into the body — confirmed against a real `rcl.lbr` R-EU resistor, left pin `R0`, right pin `R180`) and its `length` token its length (short/middle/long = 0.1/0.2/0.3 inch). **The deliberately-inconsistent fixture is REPORTED, not refused, and comes from the PACKAGE**: the footprint is built from the package's own pads while the pins come from the connects, so a device whose connects name pad `"8"` while its package defines only `"1".."7"` and a stray `"99"` surfaces as a `PinIdentity` mismatch by number — the KiCad missing-pad-8 analogue exactly. What IS refused by name (structural, not a report): a multi-gate deviceset (a gate array), a symbol pin with no `<connect>` (an **unmapped pin** — an Eagle symbol pin has only a name, so with no connect it has no number and cannot become a pin), malformed XML, a non-`<eagle>` root, and a `.brd`/`.sch` rather than a `<library>`. **No additive change to `Symbol`/`Footprint`/`PartDefinition`/`PinIdentity` was needed** — the Eagle primitives all mapped onto the existing vocabulary (KiCad's own `Footprint`/`Pad` finding again) — so the KiCad path is BIT-IDENTICAL by construction (nothing shared moved) and an Eagle-loaded part round-trips through the schematic file as the same byte-identical fixed point. Filed: whole Eagle `.brd`/`.sch` import (a different, larger job), Eagle 3D package models, and the newer Eagle/Fusion XML variants beyond the classic `.lbr`. **Schematic drawing output** (`SchematicSheet`/`SchematicDrawing`; docs `examples/ecad-schematic-sheet.md`): the drawn SHEET that REPLACES `Netlist.ToText()` as the human-readable view — placed symbols, orthogonal (Manhattan) wires with junction dots, net LABELS for rails, reference designators + values, a border and a title block, to SVG/DXF/PDF through Modeling's `SvgDrawing`/`DxfDocument`/`PdfDrawing` writers (NOT `DrawingSheet`, which projects 3D — a schematic is caller-placed line work, so it owns its own 2D sheet). **A deterministic function of the graph + placement** (same schematic + placement → byte-identical SVG), so it cannot disagree with the netlist — and `Verify()` PROVES it, reconstructing connectivity from the drawn PRIMITIVES (union-find over the wire segments + label equality, never the router's bookkeeping) and asserting BOTH directions: every signal/stub net's pins joined (wire path OR shared label), no two pins of different nets joined. Three router decisions carry it. A **rail** (a Power/Ground pin, a recognised rail name, or a net past the fanout threshold) is drawn as LABELS not a long wire and joins by the shared label — a labelled pin never takes the wire branch, so a label and a wire cannot cross-join two nets. A **wire** is an L for two pins and a median-y TRUNK+stubs for three or more, so an interior stub makes a T the junction-dot renderer marks (endpoint multiplicity ≥3; a mid-segment CROSSING is not a junction, the schematic convention). A **junction dot** is a filled disc via a round-capped zero-length stroke in SVG/PDF but an outline circle in DXF (the 2D DXF writer has no fill or width) — the per-format spelling difference the writers already carry. Placement is hand-done (`SchematicPlacement`, EXACT quarter-turn poses so a pin anchor coincides with its wire endpoint to the bit) with `Grid` a labelled stand-in; refused BY NAME at construction — a component with no symbol, a net on a pin the symbol does not draw (the "pin with no anchor"/`PinIdentity` case), a component the placement does not cover. Filed follow-ons: a real auto-placer (a GOOD layout) and an obstacle-avoiding wire router (v1 may cross symbols), plus hierarchical sheets/buses/off-page connectors and back-annotation. Remaining big rocks: SIMD passes, nuget.org publish (`Directory.Build.props` URLs still placeholders — a real remote exists at github.com/veggielane/EngrCAD).


















- .NET SDK 10.0.302 installed **user-local** at `%USERPROFILE%\.dotnet` (win-x64 — the dev box is an i9-9900K; historical arm64 benchmark labels are from the previous laptop, and NEW measurements must be labeled win-x64), on the user PATH with `DOTNET_ROOT` set. Build with `dotnet build EngrCAD.slnx`, test with `dotnet test EngrCAD.slnx`.
- Git repository initialized; commit only when Chris asks.
- Target framework: **.NET 10 (LTS)** via `Directory.Build.props`.
- **NuGet**: all `src/*` projects pack (shared metadata — `Version 0.1.0`, MIT, package READMEs — lives in `Directory.Build.props`; license/URLs are placeholders Chris must confirm before any nuget.org push). `src/EngrCAD` is a meta-package whose ProjectReferences become package dependencies (viewer kept separate so headless consumers don't pull Avalonia). Local feed workflow: bump `<Version>`, `dotnet pack EngrCAD.slnx -c Release -o C:\Users\chris\nuget-local`; the folder is registered as source `engrcad-local`, so consumers just `dotnet add package EngrCAD` (+ `EngrCAD.Viewer`). Note: NuGet caches by version — after repacking the *same* version, delete the cached copies under `%USERPROFILE%\.nuget\packages\engrcad*\<version>` (or just bump the version).

## Architecture

Three engines with different mathematics and data structures, plus interop and query layers on top of a shared core.

### Core (foundation for everything)
- Zero-allocation math: `readonly struct` `Vector3d`, `Matrix4x4d`, quaternions, `AABB`
- Central tolerance/epsilon policy for robust floating-point comparison
- Spatial acceleration: BVH and octree (used by all engines and by the query layer)
- Central **unit** policy: `ModelUnits` (mm/N/MPa/tonne/s, densities in tonne/mm³, conversions at the edges) and the `Material`/`Materials` both the document model and `EngrCAD.Fea` share — the only assembly both see

### Mesh engine (discrete)
- Half-edge data structure for O(1) topology traversal
- Algorithms: booleans, decimation, subdivision
- Bulk data stored data-oriented (SoA) for cache locality

### Implicit engine (volumetric)
- SDF evaluator: `(x, y, z) → distance`
- Primitives (sphere, box, cylinder, …) and operators (union, intersect, smooth blend) composed as an AST
- SIMD-batched evaluation; later, compilation of C# expression trees down to SDF graphs / IL / compute shaders

### B-Rep engine (parametric) — hardest, built last
- Geometry: planes, cylinders, cones, NURBS surfaces/curves
- Topology wrapper referencing geometry: Solid → Shell → Face → Loop → Edge → Vertex
- Surface–surface intersection engine; booleans and filleting on top

### Interop layer — conversion triangle complete
- Implicit → Mesh: `SurfaceNets.Polygonize` (manifold dual contouring)
- B-Rep → Mesh: `BRepTessellator.Tessellate` (shared edge polylines + ear clipping + welding)
- Mesh → Implicit: `MeshSdf` (BVH nearest-triangle + angle-weighted pseudonormal sign; requires closed mesh; composes with all `Sdf` operators)
- Field → Mesh quality: `SdfProjectionTarget` implements EngrCAD.Mesh's `IProjectionTarget` over any `Sdf` (`p − d·∇d/|∇d|`, iterated; difference step relative to the field's bounds). **The guarantee is one-sidedness, not convergence**: a 1-Lipschitz lower bound puts the surface at least `|d|` away in every direction, so a step of that length can never cross it — but `|d|` need not decrease, and near a CSG difference's **fictitious faces** (the subtracted tool's own surface, inside removed material) two branches trade the point back and forth forever (measured on `Box(2,2,2) − Sphere(1.2)`: six steps leave a probe exactly where it started, true distance 0.45 vs a field reading 0.14). Harmless for the job it exists for — a remeshed vertex is always within a fraction of an edge length of the surface, where every field here is locally exact — so project near-surface points only, never as a general closest-point query

### Simulation layer (`EngrCAD.Fea`) — tet meshing + linear-static structural + thermal + modal + buckling/harmonic + transient dynamics + fatigue post-processing + topology optimisation done
- Closed surface mesh → `TetMesh`: exact-predicate Delaunay, winding-number classification, verified boundary recovery, optional quality/sizing refinement
- **Anisotropic boundary layers** (`BoundaryLayerSpec` on `TetMeshOptions`): a graded stack marched inward from a `Facets`-selected wall — the same selector a boundary condition uses — with the isotropic pipeline filling what is left, so no new volume algorithm and every existing guarantee inherited
- `QuadraticTetMesh` (10-node), `TetQuality` (dihedral + radius-edge + slivers, partitioned by measured stretch so the isotropic rules are never applied to a deliberately flat layer element), per-element region ids, per-facet source-triangle tags
- **Model-fed boundary conditions**: `BRepTessellator.TessellateWithProvenance`/`TessellateForTetMesh` (Interop) lower a `BrepSolid` to a mesh AND the per-triangle B-Rep-face tags in one call — a by-product carried through welding (the mesh is bit-identical to a plain `Tessellate`), so `TetMeshOptions.FacetTags` populates itself and a support/load is named with `Facets.Tag(faceId)` off the `BrepQueries`/selection vocabulary instead of matching triangles to faces by hand (a solve so named is bit-identical to one named by a geometric selector). And `TetMeshDiagnostics.MinBoundaryFacetSize` reports the finest boundary facet — the surface's own element-size FLOOR — because `MaxElementSize` is a MINIMUM size that only refines coarser facets and cannot make a mesh coarser than its surface tessellation (measured: `Box(60,20,8) − Cylinder(4,40)` gives 84 k elements at a coarse h = 20 where the Ø8 bore's ~0.5 mm facets floor it)
- **`TetSmoothing`** — the sliver post-pass radius-edge refinement provably cannot be: interior-vertex optimization-based smoothing against a worst-incident-dihedral objective, with the boundary and every deliberately anisotropic vertex FROZEN, so the surface-fidelity contract, the volume identity, the connectivity and the exact orientation invariant are all inherited rather than restated (measured on a 20³ box: **every sliver removed** at three sizes, worst dihedral 0.00° → 10–17°, volume drift 7.8e-15 … 2.1e-14)
- **Structural**: `AnalysisMesh` (one view over 4- and 10-node elements) → `StructuralModel` (materials per region, supports and loads over facet selectors) → `StructuralSolver` (AMD-ordered `SparseCholesky` or CG) → `StructuralResults` (displacement, strain, stress, von Mises, `MeshField` publishing, `.vtu`)
- **Thermal**: the same triple with one temperature DOF per node — `ThermalModel` → `ThermalSolver.Solve`/`SolveTransient` → `ThermalResults`/`ThermalTransientResults` — over the same `AnalysisMesh` and the same `Facets` selectors, plus one-way coupling back via `StructuralModel.ThermalLoad`. Shared rather than parallel-built: `FeaGuards` (the element-Jacobian guard both solvers ask), `SurfaceSampler` (the display-mesh correspondence both results types publish through), `StructuralSolver.AdvisoryFor` (the direct-vs-CG note), and the **(node, region) slot table** — `ThermalResults.NodalFluxIn(region, node)` is `NodalStressIn`'s twin over the same `AnalysisMesh.RegionsAt`, with `ComputeNodalFlux` taking the same `perRegion` parameter (design.md §3d), plus an opt-in **superconvergent flux recovery** (`FluxRecovery`) and a thermal **`ErrorEstimate`** — the same ZZ machinery §3i runs at three components rather than six (see the recovery bullet below) — and per-region **directional conductivity** via `ThermalModel.SetConductivity(region, `ConductivityLaw`)` (the `ElasticLaw` twin; see the directional-materials bullet)
- **Modal**: `ModalSolver.Solve` → `ModalResults` (`VibrationMode` frequencies/shapes/participation, `RigidBodyMode` kept separate) over the same `AnalysisMesh` and the same supports — a consistent mass matrix that IS `ThermalElement.Capacity`'s integral under another name (one implementation, `TetElement.ConsistentMass`, and one rule selector `TetQuadrature.ForMass` two degrees above the stiffness's), plus shift-and-invert Lanczos (`LanczosEigen`) over ONE `SparseCholesky` factorization. `RigidBodyModes` is shared with `StructuralSolver`, which REFUSES on the same computation this one REPORTS
- **Buckling / stress stiffening / harmonic**: `BucklingSolver.Solve(staticResults)` → `BucklingResults` over `TetElement.GeometricStiffness` (the mass matrix's shape with `∫grad N_a·σ·grad N_b` in it), `ModalSolveOptions.Prestress` for a preloaded modal solve, and `HarmonicSolver` → `HarmonicResponse` for a steady-state sweep by modal superposition over `RayleighDamping`/`ModalDamping`. **Base (support) excitation landed on the modal harmonic sweep** (`HarmonicSolveOptions.BaseExcitation`, `BaseMotionKind` accel/vel/disp): a shaker/seismic input driving the model through its supports, whose modal force is exactly `-Γ_d·a_g` (the participation factor already carried) so it needs no new math — the response is RELATIVE displacement (`HarmonicResponse.IsRelativeToBase`, the right quantity for stress), the whole base moving together (independent support-group motion is v1's boundary), verified by the modal force being exactly `-Γ_d·a_g`, the resonant relative displacement matching `a_g/(2ζω_n²)`, and a base accel `a_g` equalling an inertial nodal force `m·a_g` to 2.2e-16. `FeaAssembly` is the shared stiffness/mass/geometric assembly so no two solvers can build different `K` or `M`
- **Direct per-frequency harmonic**: `DirectHarmonicSolver` → `DirectHarmonicResponse` factors `(K − ω²M + iωC + iηK)` per sweep point over Core's `SparseLdlt` — the non-proportional-damping answer modal superposition structurally cannot give. The damping VOCABULARY lives on `StructuralModel` because it is geometry-attached data no per-mode ratio can carry (`SetDamping` model-wide/per-region — two differing regions are already non-proportional — and `Dashpot` grounded or node-to-node, whose pair coupling block can land where the stiffness pattern has no entry: the union-pattern case); the modal route REFUSES a model carrying it rather than silently ignoring it (the transient integrates viscous C but refuses loss factors), and `FeaAssembly.Damping` is the ONE viscous damping matrix in the project (the factorization consumes values, not products). Verified against the corrected modal route (3.5e-6 relative; 1e-9 where one mode is the whole basis), hand-built complex Cramer oracles for dashpot and per-region damping, exact `1/(2ζ)` amplification, and the exact-resonance refusal reproduced through the solver's own pivot arithmetic verbatim. **Hysteretic (structural) damping landed here**: `SetLossFactor(η)`/`SetLossFactor(region, η)` enter as the frequency-INDEPENDENT imaginary stiffness `η·K` (the complex modulus `K(1+iη)`, `FeaAssembly.HystereticStiffness`), the total imaginary impedance being `ωC + ηK`; this is the only solver that carries it, since it has no causal time-domain form (transient refuses) and no per-mode real ratio off resonance (modal refuses). Verified on the SDOF fixture: resonant amplification exactly `1/η` (the pivot stays away from zero at resonance, so no refusal there), a sweep against `f/√((k−ω²m)²+(ηk)²)` to 3e-16 (the imaginary part is CONSTANT, not scaling with ω), and a crossover against a viscous model tuned to the same peak (hysteretic smaller below resonance, larger above)
- **Transient dynamics**: `TransientSolver.Solve` → `TransientResults` steps `M·a + C·v + K·u = f(t)` by the Newmark / HHT-alpha family at a CONSTANT step, so one factorization serves the run (plus one against **M** for the initial acceleration, which is solved rather than assumed). `TimeIntegration` carries beta/gamma/alpha as ONE value with both stability conditions enforced at construction; each `TransientState` is a full `StructuralResults` plus velocity, acceleration and energies. **Several load patterns with independent histories landed** (`TransientSolveOptions.LoadPatterns`): the gravity-held-while-a-shaker-runs case `f(t) = Σ g_i(t)·f_i` as a `(model, law)` list sharing one factorization (`RequireOneOperator`, the `SolveAll` contract), the single-pattern form being a one-entry list bit-identically (`ComputeLoad` reduces to `Scale`); verified by SUPERPOSITION — the two-pattern run equals the two single-pattern runs added, to 7e-14 relative. **For the proportional RUN OPTION no damping matrix is assembled** — the backlog predicted this would be the one consumer that needed one, and it is not (every use of `C` is a product `alpha·(M·x)+beta·(K·x)` or a scalar fold). **But MODEL-carried damping now IS integrated** (`Dashpot`, per-region `SetDamping`): non-proportional damping has no product form, so the transient assembles `FeaAssembly.Damping` once — the one place a `C` is built, now shared with the direct harmonic solve — folds it into the effective stiffness as `(1+α)·a1·C` and adds one `C·x` per step. The total is `C = α·M + β·K + C_model` and a model stating no damping assembles nothing (Rayleigh path bit-identical). Verified on the SDOF fixture with a grounded dashpot (`ζ = c/(2mω)`): decay lands on the damped closed form to 0.1513% (= the predicted phase error), energy balance `∫v'Cv dt` reads 9.6e-14, and a uniform-Rayleigh model (matrix path) reproduces the same statement on the options (products path) to 2.2e-13 relative — the general path IS the special case by another route. **Base excitation landed as the RELATIVE formulation** (`TransientSolveOptions.BaseMotion` = ground acceleration `a_g(t)` along a direction): `M·u''+C·u'+K·u = -M·ι_d·a_g(t)`, one inertial load pattern over fixed supports, answer is RELATIVE displacement (`TransientResults.IsRelativeToBase`, the stress-correct quantity), COMPOSING with the model's own loads. **The todo asked which of relative-vs-absolute is cleaner and the measurement chose relative**: prescribing the absolute support motion (internal `AbsolutePrescribedMotion` seam) recomputes a `-Aeff·u_c` correction each step, needs the seismic acceleration DOUBLE-integrated to displacement, and gives absolute = relative + ground; the relative form needs no per-step operator touch and takes the acceleration a record already is. The two are proved EQUAL through the seam — for an undamped body under average acceleration the change of variables `u_abs = u_rel + ι_d·u_g` is exact at the discrete level, so the two integrations agree to **6.1e-12** (the "if you offer both they agree to round-off" oracle). Verified against the transmissibility as an AMPLIFICATION so the fixture's participation factor cancels (its consistent-mass inertial load is NOT its reduced mass): steady relative over static base deflection `1/√((1−r²)²+(2ζr)²)` = **1.94403 vs 1.94257** (0.075%), resonant `1/(2ζ)` = **9.9938 vs 10** (0.062%); a ZERO base motion is a plain run bit-for-bit (0 differing bits/401 states); whole base moves together (independent foundations filed). **Adaptive time stepping landed as a small DYADIC set factored per size** (`SolveAdaptive` + `TransientAdaptiveOptions{Levels, Tolerance}`, sizes `dt/2^L`): the constant step is what lets one factorization serve the run, so the honest adaptive form is NOT a continuously varying step (which refactors at every change) but a fixed set each factored ONCE and cached, spending the fine step only where a local error `dt²·|Δa|` demands it.
