Three rules came out of settling it (design.md §2). **(a) A quantity is either something an EQUATION consumes or something a REPORT prints, and only the second can be converted after the fact** — so the convention belongs wherever a conversion has nowhere to live (an FEA mass matrix must balance a stiffness in MPa against a length in mm, and the factor would have to reappear in the gravity load, the heat capacity, the frequency, the buckling load and the transient time constant), and the readable units become ACCESSORS. A consistent internal unit with a documented accessor is not the same thing as two conventions. **(b) A stored constant must be ASSERTED in the form a human can check** — the catalogue stores tonne/mm³ and the tests assert kg/m³ against the datasheet, because comparing 7.85e-9 with 7.85e-9 verifies typing, not physics (the same test caught a quoted thermal diffusivity of 68.7 that the catalogue's own rows make 69.03). **(c) The way to fix a duplicated convention is to delete one of the types, not to add a note** — a cross-reference warning had been left in `PartMassProperties` as a stopgap and it did nothing, because a reader who already believes their own figure does not go looking. And the regression oracle for the merge is worth naming: **every FEA verification number depends on density**, so if a unit had slipped anywhere, the cantilever deflection, the modal frequencies (∝ 1/√ρ), the buckling loads and the thermal transients would have moved. None did.
- **A warm-up COUNT does not establish steady state, so an allocation test must take the MINIMUM over several batches rather than a looser threshold.** The recorded JIT-tiering lesson is about benchmarks; it applies to *tests* too, and it had put four allocation-freedom tests (`Bvh`, `Predicates3d.InSphere`, `MeshSdf.Evaluate`, the SDF batch entry) on the same fragile shape — warm up N times, measure ONE batch, assert a total. Tiered compilation is promoted on a background queue and therefore on a WALL CLOCK, so under machine load the promotion slips past the warm-up into the measured window; and `GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread` is perturbed by a collection triggered on ANOTHER thread, which xUnit's parallel collections supply. Measured: the BVH test failed a loaded gate at **8168 bytes over 2000 iterations** — ~4 B/iteration, nowhere near a real per-iteration cost (one closure would be 176 000) and suspiciously close to the 8 KB allocation context. Both artifacts are ONE-TIME and neither scales with the iteration count, so `AllocationProbe.SteadyStateBytes` takes the minimum over five batches, and the argument is exact rather than statistical: a genuine per-iteration allocation is in EVERY batch, so the minimum is still `iterations × cost` and cannot be escaped by retrying, while a one-time artifact spoils a bounded number of them. **Raising the threshold would have bought the flake off by making the test blind to a small real regression** — the opposite trade. The probe is held to a guard's bar (it must be shown to FIRE), and its immunity is pinned DIRECTLY by a fixture that allocates 64 KB on the first two measured batches and never again, rather than left to machine load to reproduce.
- **A shared seam is not the caller's tolerance to choose, which is why biarc fitting stops
  at face boundaries.** Adopting a fitted curve is a caller's decision wherever the curve
  belongs to one face — the deviation is reported and the caller weighs it. A SEAM edge is
  different in kind: it is shared geometry that must WELD, so replacing it with a fit moves
  BOTH adjacent faces' boundaries at once, and the tolerance stops being a property of the
  curve and becomes a property of the joint. That is the reason "lighter B-Rep seam edges"
  is not a third application of the biarc fitter but a different question, and it is filed
  as a scoping decision rather than as open work.
- **A vector `sin`/`cos` that is not correctly rounded cannot enter this kernel**, so the
  elliptic refinement stays scalar per lane: writing one is a substantial numerics project,
  and the measured ceiling it would buy is the ~1.2× the vectorized SCAN already
  demonstrates, on the segment kind rarest in real sketches. The barrier is exactness, not
  effort — recorded so the arithmetic is not re-derived (see also the measured 11 858 /
  19 172 of 200 000 doubles where .NET 10's `Vector.Cos`/`Vector.Sin` differ from scalar).
- **A finite-difference stand-in can be doing a second job, and replacing it with the exact
  quantity deletes that job.** `FaceSplitter`'s arrangement walk orders the edges at a node by
  `DepartureAngle`, the chord to a point 2% along the edge, and the long-standing follow-up was
  to make that an EXACT tangent now that every analytic curve overrides `Curve3d.DerivativeAt`
  — removing the approximation the `1e-12` turn guard exists to tolerate. Building
  `CurvedArrangement2d` showed it is backwards. **A 2% chord is a SECANT, and a secant over a
  finite span encodes CURVATURE**: for `p(s) = v + s·d + ½s²κ·n̂` the chord to arc length s
  points along `d + ½sκ·n̂`, i.e. the tangent rotated by ~½sκ. So two edges leaving a node
  TANGENTIALLY — not exotic here but the normal case, since every fillet band meets its
  neighbour tangentially — have secants separated by `½s|κ₁ − κ₂|`, FIRST order in the curvature
  difference (about 0.015 rad for an r = 2 band against a planar neighbour at band length ~3),
  where their exact tangents are separated by nothing but round-off. With exact tangents the two
  would tie, the `1e-12` guard would read the tie as the back-along-the-same-edge case and add a
  full 2π, and the walk would choose by rounding — which is precisely the failure
  `CurvedArrangement2d` had to add a **curvature tie-break** to fix (a disc tangent to a plate's
  edge departed at `(−1.22e-16, −1)` and the union came back EMPTY). The chord is doing the
  tie-break's job for free; the item is retired rather than deferred, and the general rule is
  that before replacing an approximation with the exact quantity, check what the approximation's
  ERROR was carrying.
- **"Is this discretization fine enough?" is a measurement, and the honest answer can be
  "no, and here is by how much".** `Fitting3d.MinVolumeBox` finds each edge-pair family's angle
  by a 48-step sweep (3.75°) plus golden-section refinement rather than by O'Rourke's algebraic
  critical angle, and the filed question was whether a family's optimum can hide inside one
  bracket. Re-running a 43-hull corpus (random tetrahedra — few faces, so each family's volume
  is a max of very few sinusoids with sharp kinks — plus the regular tetrahedron, a sheared box
  and a flattened one) at 8× and 32× the density with 8× the brackets: it CAN, and the best a
  finer search ever finds is **5.905e-8 relative** (12.566414756 → 12.566414014). So the sweep
  is not exact, and what exactness would buy is 2e-8 of a linear dimension on a box that packs
  plates and selects stock — inside an O(E²·h) inner loop. The sweep stands, the number is in the
  doc comment instead of a caveat, and the A/B seam (`Fitting3d.SweepSamples`/`SweepBrackets`,
  the `flatBlockLimit`/`UseParityIndex` pattern) stays so the claim can be re-measured rather
  than re-argued.
- **A performance residual retires on the SHAPE of its own workload, not on a bigger machine.**
  Two of these settled together. (a) `Bvh.Build`'s follow-up was "reuse a hierarchy across a
  boolean cascade" — and no cascade this repo produces has anything to reuse: a chain
  `A − B − C`, a balanced union tree and `Shape.Drill`'s N tools all give every operand exactly
  ONE appearance, since each stage's left operand is freshly produced and each right operand is
  a fresh clone. The ceiling is therefore zero on the shape of cascade that exists, and bounded
  by the broad phase's own 10.0 ms of a ~199 ms exact union (5%) in general. (b)
  `Region2dBoolean.ContainedIn`'s O(cells × operand vertices) term was already measured at 1.0×
  after a full point-location index was built and declined; the owed re-benchmark on a quiet
  machine now reads **89.8 / 154.6 ms** minima for 120 and 480 overlapping 32-gons over four
  interleaved runs in one sitting (spread ~12%), and the number that carries the argument is the
  RATIO: 4× the polygons for 1.72× the time. The balanced fold keeps the CELL count tiny exactly
  where the operand vertex counts grow, so the product the term is quadratic in never gets
  large — which is the structural reason the index bought nothing, restated as a scaling
  measurement. Both stay retired until a consumer with the opposite shape is measured first.
- **The epsilon ladder** (audited repo-wide; every site is documented in place): new epsilons must pick a tier — **1e-9 absolute weld** (= `Tolerance.Default.Linear`: exactly-constructed shared geometry — tessellation welds, seam breaks, clone dedupe) / **1e-7 seam** (geometry built independently on two sides of one curve — `SealSeams`, boundary-vs-grid matching; `MeshWelder`'s T-junction zip expresses this tier **relatively**, as 1e-7 × the soup's extent, since every source of independent-construction error — a chord's sagitta, a float32-quantized STL's cracks — is proportional to the model, and an absolute form was wrong in both directions away from unit scale) / **1e-6 inverse-evaluation** (named: `FaceGeometry.InverseEvaluationTolerance` — Gauss–Newton pullback; three decades looser than weld, do not tighten; the 1e-7 seam tier is likewise named `FaceGeometry.SeamTolerance`, and `SurfaceIntersection`'s marching constants live together in `TracerSettings` because they must be tuned as a set) / **scale-free algorithmic guards** (relative machine-epsilon degeneracy tests, exact-zero division guards, exact-zero *semantic* tests — these deliberately bypass `Tolerance` and say so in a comment). Never invent a fresh literal. Shewchuk `Predicates2d` error-bound constants are the algorithm — off limits.

## Roadmap (bottom-up — do not skip ahead)

1. **Core math & spatial acceleration** ✅ done — `Tolerance`, `Vector2d`/`Vector3d` (implicit conversion from tuples), `Matrix4d` (column-vector convention), `Quaterniond`, `Aabb`, `Ray3d`, `Bvh` (static, median-split; the build sorts each node's item range through a **contiguous key array** rather than an `IComparer<int>` chasing scattered centroids, and **forks sibling subtrees onto the thread pool** above 4096 items, then replays the canonical sequential node numbering so scheduling is unobservable — the tree stays **bit-identical** to the original builder's, locked by `BvhBuildOrderTests`' fingerprints of the item permutation and every node's range/leaf-flag/bounds BITS. Measured 32 400 boxes 22.6 → 4.6 ms, 130 000 boxes 142.5 → 33.6 ms, and the exact mesh boolean's broad phase 46.8 → 10.0 ms. Quickselect instead of a full sort is 3.9× on its own but *repermutes items*, so it was rejected: `Query` appends in leaf-visit order, `Nearest` breaks ties by traversal order, and the imprint boolean interns seam points in `QueryOverlap` order, so "an equally good tree" silently moves downstream geometry. Serves box/ray/nearest plus t-ordered `QueryAll`, tree–tree `QueryOverlap` (the imprint-boolean broad phase), allocation-free struct-metric `Nearest<TMetric>` — `MeshSdf.Evaluate` measured 104 B/call → 0 — and exposes per-node contiguous ranges via `NodeView`/`ItemsInTreeOrder`, so future hierarchies need not fork the way `MeshWindingNumber` did), `Octree` (dynamic), **`Predicates2d`** (adaptive-exact Shewchuk `Orient2d`/`InCircle` — sign exact for ALL finite doubles, stackalloc expansions, locked against BigInteger ground truth incl. the Kettner ulp-grid where naive determinants provably lie. **Lesson on WHICH inputs are actually hostile**: a translated cross product is Sterbenz-exact when points cluster, so the Kettner grid does NOT break a monotone-chain hull — the family that does is near-collinear points spread over a wide *exponent* range, where the differences themselves round. Measured: the naive chain returns a non-convex hull on ~7% of such clouds, the predicate version on none. `ConvexHull2` routes its turn test through `Orient2dSign` for exactly this reason; its internal error-bound constants are the algorithm — never "clean them up"), **`Geometry2.Arrangement2d`/`ArrangementCell2d`** (2D segment arrangement: exact-incidence decisions via the predicates, rounded coordinates under a snap tolerance, tightest-turn cell extraction with CCW outers + CW holes — the sketch-engine robustness foundation; FaceSplitter's planar tracing is the same dance and could one day sit on it, deliberately not unified — boolean-critical), **`Geometry2.SpaceFillingCurve`/`Morton2d`/`TiledHilbertLattice`/`SpaceFillingCurve3d`** (finite-order Hilbert/Moore/Peano/Gosper/Z-order over a region at a stated spacing, with the ACHIEVED spacing reported; `OverTiled` holds the RECTANGLE rather than its bounding square and reports the `Anisotropy` that costs; `SpaceFillingCurve3d` is the volume member, Hilbert only, over Skilling's transpose walk; the integer lattice underneath is where the exactness lives — see the status paragraph and design.md §2), **`Geometry2.Region2dThickness`** (the 2D opposing-edge wall thickness, reporting the perpendicular distance to the hit segment's LINE — the local measure a connectivity test cannot make), **`IndexPriorityQueue`** (O(1) id→slot, decrease-key; upgraded `MeshDecimator`'s lazy PQ — equal-or-better quality; lesson: never key dictionaries by packed `(min<<32)|max` longs — `long.GetHashCode` is `lo ^ hi`, collapsing structured edge keys into a handful of buckets, measured 4× whole-algorithm slowdown; tuple keys hash properly), **`ProgressCancel`** (optional-trailing-param cooperative cancellation, wired into Polygonize/Decimate/`BRepTessellator` and the whole exact mesh boolean — imprint, broad phase, and the per-patch winding probes, with **polling following cost rather than code structure**: linear passes get one checkpoint at their boundary, superlinear ones poll per unit of work), **`ParallelFor.Blocks`** (deterministic block-parallel-for — each index writes only its own slot; SurfaceNets sampling ~1.6× and dense GridSdf bake ~2.5–5× faster, output bit-for-bit identical), **`ConvexHull2`** (monotone chain) + **`Fitting2d`/`Fitting3d`** (`MinAreaBox`/`MinCircle` (Welzl)/`FitBox3` (PCA heuristic)/`FitPlane` → `Frame3d`; **`MinVolumeBox(hullVertices, hullTriangles)`** is the exact minimum-volume OBB, and the place this project learned that **the 2D rotating-calipers theorem does NOT lift to 3D**: the minimum-volume box need not have a face flush with a hull face — Freeman–Shapira, a great many implementations since, and until now this repo's own `FitBox` doc comment all assumed it does. The regular tetrahedron on alternate corners of [−1,1]³ fits its cube at volume **8** while EVERY face-flush candidate measures **16** (equilateral section's min rectangle a²√3/2 = 6.93 × height 4/√3 = 2.31). What does hold is **O'Rourke's** characterization — at least two ADJACENT box faces each contain a hull EDGE — so the search runs a one-parameter family per unordered hull-edge pair (48-step sweep + golden section on the best three brackets), with exact face-flush candidates and PCA/AABB seeds on top so it can never lose to `FitBox`. **The hull is the caller's to supply**: Core owns no polyhedron type and the 3D quickhull lives in EngrCAD.Mesh because it speaks `HalfEdgeMesh`, so passing the hull as plain data keeps the layering intact rather than dragging half-edge topology into the dependency-free foundation. O(E²·h): 3.6/22/122 ms for 18/42/78-vertex hulls. **What the per-family SWEEP costs against O'Rourke's algebraic critical angle is now MEASURED rather than filed as a caveat**: re-running a 43-hull corpus at 8× and 32× the sweep density with 8× the refined brackets, the best a finer search ever finds is **5.905e-8 relative** (12.566414756 → 12.566414014 on a random tetrahedron). So a family's optimum CAN hide inside a 3.75° bracket — the sweep is not exact — and it hides by about 2e-8 of a linear dimension, on a box whose consumers pack plates and choose stock in millimetres; the algebraic solve would buy that and nothing else, inside an O(E²·h) inner loop, so the sweep is the engineering answer and the corpus test is the evidence that fails loudly if some future hull family hides a real minimum), **`Distance3d`** (`ClosestPointOnTriangle` — Ericson's Voronoi-region form, six barycentric sign tests, no tolerance anywhere bar exact-zero division guards, plus an `out TriangleRegion` overload the plain one delegates to; it had been written TWICE, privately in Mesh's `MeshProjectionTarget` and again in Interop's `MeshSdf`, and the copies had already drifted — only one carried the degeneracy guards, so the other returned NaN on a collapsed triangle. The region matters to exactly one caller and cannot be reconstructed from the point: `MeshSdf`'s sign is the angle-weighted pseudonormal of whichever *feature* won), integer grid types (`Vector2i`/`Vector3i`/`Interval1i`/`AxisAlignedBox3i`; `DVector`/`MemoryPool` deliberately not adopted — List/ArrayPool cover the call sites), **`Frame3d`** (rigid pose: origin + orthonormal basis; `FromXY`/`FromNormal`/`FromZX`(STEP AXIS2)/`FromOrthonormal`, `ToWorld`/`ToLocal` for points/vectors/rays, `Then`/`Inverse` (transpose-exact), `ToMatrix`, `Renormalized`. **`FromNormal` delegates to `Vector3d.ArbitraryPerpendicular`** — the codebase's single arbitrary-perpendicular convention, locked by tests; the phase-alignment lesson is orthogonal: when a *natural* frame exists (a cylinder's own), use it, never an arbitrary one. Adopted by `SweptSurface.Frame`, `StepReader.Axis2`, sweep caps; `SketchPlane` wraps it; frame ctors on `PlaneSurface` + all conics; **`BrepQueries.Frame(face)` + `SketchPlane.On(face)` enable sketch-on-face** end-to-end through the Shape API)

2. **Mesh engine** ✅ done — half-edge structure (`HalfEdgeMesh` + `Vertex`/`HalfEdge`/`Face` handles for LINQ traversal), manifold-validating `Build`, boundary loops, metrics (area/volume/Euler), primitives (box, uv-sphere, n-gon-capped cylinder), triangulation, Loop subdivision, booleans — **the imprint boolean is now the ONLY one** (`MeshMeshCut` imprint + per-patch winding-number classification + exact-equality weld; scale-free relative guards, survives near-tangency). The BSP/csg.js clipper behind `BooleanMethod.Bsp` is **deleted** (`Csg.cs` and the enum both gone), retired once coincident surface was classified — the last thing it did that the imprint path could not: the comparison was one-sided in every dimension, so maintaining two algorithms had become a liability rather than a hedge. **Coincident (coplanar-overlapping) surface is classified by normal agreement**: agree → keep one copy for union/intersection and drop both for difference; oppose → drop both for union/intersection and keep A for difference — the surviving copy is always the *first* mesh's, and that asymmetry is deliberate since both meshes cover the region. What makes per-face centroid membership legal there: **the coincident region's rim is already imprinted by the ordinary transversal path** (a coplanar patch ends where the other solid's surface leaves the shared plane, which is a transverse pair and does produce a segment), and coincidence is also a patch-flood boundary, so a ½ can never leak into an ordinary patch's probe. The imprint path is **1.6–2.2× faster than before and beat BSP on every case measured** (spheres 7.4 ms vs 262 ms; a 32k+32k sphere union 0.71 s closed against BSP's 74.9 s for an *open* 347k-face shell). Two BSP failures it exposed remain locked by tests as hostile inputs worth pinning, now asserting only the imprint path's own correctness: a bore stopping 1e-9 from a wall (BSP returned an **open shell** — absolute plane epsilon), and 1e-5 scale (BSP returned **zero faces** — `Tolerance.Default`'s absolute 1e-9 applied to a *cross product*, an area threshold that fails quadratically with scale). Cross-checks that used to compare against BSP now assert **inclusion–exclusion identities** instead (|A∪B| = |A| + |B| − |A∩B|), which hold exactly for the polyhedra the boolean produces and need no second algorithm, QEM decimation (`MeshDecimator`: quadric edge collapse with link-condition and normal-flip guards, boundary preserved exactly; the topology layer is now **`EditableMesh.CollapseEdge`** — the port replaced a private indexed-face-set scratch state after a measured comparison committed as `MeshDecimatorQualityTests`' baseline: **bit-identical output** on twelve fixture/budget pairs, **0.84×** the time, and correct at 1e-5 scale where the old path lost **91%** of the volume), `RenderMesh` extraction, OBJ export, plane cutting (`MeshPlaneCut.Cut`: keeps the side the normal points away from, exact canonical-direction crossing points, Sutherland–Hodgman face clipping — non-convex crossing faces are Newell-plane triangulated before clipping, not fanned from vertex 0 — returns ordered boundary loops, optional earcut caps with collinear-chord zip), winding-number classification (`MeshWindingNumber`: exact Van Oosterom–Strackee solid-angle sum + Barill/Jacobson order-2 multipole `FastWindingNumber` over its own contiguous-range hierarchy — built before `Bvh` exposed per-node ranges; could migrate now, stays put with locked tests — robust inside/outside for non-watertight meshes); **EditableMesh** (mutable companion over free-list SoA — `HalfEdgeMesh` stays immutable-after-build STRUCTURALLY, not by discipline; guarded Euler operators SplitEdge/FlipEdge/CollapseEdge/PokeFace/MergeEdges where guards never partially mutate, refusal = untouched mesh + enum reason; `MeshChange`/`MeshChangeSet` = journaled slot-write undo records — the COMPLETE journal incl. free-list links and counters, so do→revert is bit-identical by construction and replay verifies each slot before writing; fuzz-tested with Validate after every random op; g3's per-element records rejected because they don't restore IDs. **All five operators REARRANGE existing material, and that is what makes an in-place hole fill or extrude a different project rather than an overload** — the correction to the way those were filed ("once callers want them"): a fill makes a face closing a boundary loop and an extrude makes both vertices and wall faces, neither expressible in this vocabulary, so each needs a NEW operator carrying the journaled record that is what makes do→revert bit-identical by construction. Hence `HoleFiller`/`MeshExtrude` are SOUP-level (`ToIndexed` → append → the manifold-validating `Build`), which is the right shape for an operation that invents geometry and wants revalidating; the demand side agrees, `MeshExtrude` having no consumer outside its own tests and `HoleFiller`'s one production caller — `MeshRepair.AutoRepair` — standing to save a single `ToMesh` on the branch that runs only when a crack was welded); **readers + repair** (`StlReader`/`ObjReader`/`OffReader` + `MeshReader` facade → `MeshReadResult` carrying mesh-or-soup + diagnostics, never throwing on dirty geometry; `MeshRepair.Clean`: weld → degenerate → duplicate → per-component winding flood with outward vote → zip → Build; **`MergeCoincidentEdges`** (crack closing on an `EditableMesh` — spatial hash over boundary-edge midpoints, opposite-direction pairs only so a merge can never fold the surface, positions never move, and `MergeEdges`' guards refuse anything unsafe so the tolerance may exceed a safe vertex weld) and **`AutoRepair`** (Clean → crack weld if still open → `HoleFiller.FillAll`, reporting cracks merged / holes filled / holes skipped; `MeshReader.ReadAndRepair` exposes it opt-in since closing holes invents geometry). Note the ordering constraint: crack merging cannot sit among Clean's soup passes — no half-edge mesh exists yet — so it runs after Clean's `Build`, where a valid-but-open mesh first exists. Lessons: STL binary detection is the exact 84+50n size test BEFORE any "solid" sniffing — binary exporters write "solid" into headers; binary STL cracks below one float32 ulp cannot exist, so STL welding needs tolerances at/above float quantization; open-component orientation votes must probe BOTH sides of faces via solid angles — a single behind-the-normal probe is blind to inward sheets). **Exact-boolean lessons** (from the imprint rewrite, all institutional memory): a seam welds by *equality* only if every shared point is computed from a **canonical (edge, plane) pair** — derive the crossing from the edge's lower-indexed endpoint so both meshes and both adjacent faces produce bit-identical coordinates; recomputation must be bit-reproducible, not merely close (the boolean then welds via a `Dictionary<Vector3d,int>` on exact bits and the 1e-9 weld tier never enters). A crossing that coincides with an **existing vertex** must be reconciled to one coordinate **across both meshes** before either imprint runs, honoring any mapping the crossing already carries, or the seam silently stops welding. Inside/outside must be decided **per patch at the largest triangle's centroid**, never per face — seam slivers sit exactly where the winding number approaches ½ (the same lesson `BrepBoolean` learned about sagitta-safe probes). And degeneracy guards here are `1e-13 × operand extent` — the scale-free tier, never the absolute weld tier — which is what makes small models and near-tangent pairs work. **Two more traps, both found by the coincident-surface work, both the same defect in new clothes**: never call `Vector3d.TryNormalize(Tolerance.Default)` inside a scale-free algorithm — it applies the absolute 1e-9 to a **cross product**, i.e. an *area*, so mating faces stopped being recognized below ~1e-4 scale (measured: a non-manifold union at 1e-5 — the BSP defect reintroduced inside the very algorithm that exists to avoid it; read only the *sign* of the area-weighted normal instead). And **`Orient2d` returns twice an AREA**, so an epsilon compared against it must be scaled by the edge length — unscaled, coplanar triangles that merely *touch along a shared edge* (the commonest relation between neighbouring solids) report as overlapping. **Performance lesson, the same shape as the B-Rep boolean's**: the two biggest costs were an O(k²) scan of every fragment ever cut from a host face when locating interior seam points (96% of the imprint for a 64-sided bore), and **building an O(n log n) winding hierarchy to answer eight questions** — below ~32 queries the exact O(n) sum is the faster algorithm (`MeshWindingNumber.Direct`; two 32k-triangle spheres went 65 ms → 6 ms). Also: `HalfEdgeMesh.Triangulated()` was rebuilding meshes that were already triangles, revalidating everything per boolean stage in a cascade. **Region seams may now be REFINED** (`MeshRegionOperator`): a replacement may subdivide a seam edge, and reinsertion carries the split into the neighbours (base triangles re-fan from the corner between their two unrefined edges — exactly what an edge split leaves — and the split point is created ONCE and shared, since two vertices at one position *is* the crack), so `LoopSubdivision` now round-trips through a region operator as `MeshDecimator` already did. **The order of the two seam checks is the load-bearing part**: every original seam vertex must be shown present BEFORE any chain is walked, because otherwise a replacement that MOVED a rim vertex is indistinguishable from one that removed it and inserted a new one nearby — and would be accepted as a "refinement", welding a crack silently, which is precisely the failure the seam contract exists to prevent. `LoopSubdivision.Subdivide(mesh, n, preserveBoundary: true)` is the new pinned-rim mode that makes it usable (the default Warren boundary rule smooths the outline, i.e. moves it, and stays refused). **Isotropic remeshing** (`Remesher.Remesh` + `IProjectionTarget`/`MeshProjectionTarget`): g3's `Remesher`/`RemesherPro` rebuilt on `EditableMesh`'s guarded Euler operators — one pass is a per-edge collapse→flip→split sweep on a **modulo-prime stride** (index order makes a tessellated cylinder erode away edge by edge), then a double-buffered uniform/cotangent smoothing pass, then a projection pass; fully deterministic (fixed stride constant, no RNG, bit-identical positions across runs). **Constraints are expressed on VERTICES, not edges** — a deliberate departure from g3 forced by our topology: an undirected edge is named by the smaller of a twin pair and a collapse *merges* edge pairs, so the survivor gets a different canonical index and freed indices are recycled; an edge-keyed constraint table goes stale — worse, silently aliases a different edge — after the first collapse, while vertex indices never do because a collapse always removes the *unpinned* end. Everything g3 spells with edge flags then follows: two pinned ends means neither collapse nor flip (a flip destroys the edge), splitting stays legal and the midpoint inherits the pin (so a constrained chain keeps its geometry while gaining resolution), and boundary/crease pins are re-derived from geometry every pass so they need no bookkeeping at all. Split/collapse thresholds are **1.33 L / 0.66 L**, NOT Botsch's 4/3 and 4/5, which thrash — a fresh split lands below the collapse threshold. Measured: 90% of a coarse sphere's edges outside the length band → **0%** after 40 passes, 73 ms. **`PreventLongEdgeFlips`** (opt-in) is the bounded-maximum mode: a flip whose new edge would exceed the split threshold is refused *unless it is strictly shorter than the edge it replaces* — the monotone form, because refusing every such flip strands the sliver whose only remedy it was (0.02° worst angle on a box against 31.7°). **RemesherPro scheduling** (`RemeshOptions.Scheduling = Queue`, `FastSplitPasses`, both opt-in): the modified-edge/vertex queues and the split-only prepass, deterministic (FIFO, fixed stride, no RNG; queued ids are recycled so every pop re-validates AND re-canonicalizes, since a collapse merges edge pairs and the survivor is usually named by the other half). Measured on 12 096 faces → target 0.05 at 40 passes: sweep **777 ms**, queue **532** (1.46×), queue + 4 fast-split passes **331** (2.35×) — but **neither feature pays alone under sweep scheduling** (240 vs 243 ms at 12 passes for the queue; 239 vs 243 for fast split), because a queue with nothing quiet to skip is pure bookkeeping and a sweep visits everything however the density got there: they are complementary, since reaching the target density sooner is what makes regions go quiet sooner. Cost is a slightly less relaxed mesh (0.6% of edges out of band vs 0.2%) — a different answer, hence opt-in. **The wake-up threshold is 1e-2 × the target edge and the obvious 1e-3 makes the whole feature pointless**: damped smoothing still moves a *converged* vertex ~5e-3 L per pass, so every vertex re-woke its own ring, the active set stayed the entire mesh, and queue scheduling measured **slower** than the sweep it exists to beat (783 vs 747 ms) — a scheduler whose wake-up rule never fires is pure overhead. **Face-aligned (RZN-flow) reprojection** (`RemeshProjection.FaceAligned`): each TRIANGLE moves rigidly onto the target (centroid to closest point, plane rotated to the target normal there) and each vertex takes the `area × (n·n')³`-weighted average of where its incident triangles put it — **the cube of the normal agreement is the crease-preserving mechanism**, since a triangle straddling a crease agrees with neither side and contributes almost nothing, so a vertex ON a crease is pulled by the two flat groups either side onto their intersection, where plain closest-point projection would drag it onto whichever face is nearer. Measured on a 2×2×2 box, target 0.35, 12 passes, feature pinning OFF: volume **7.2244 → 7.6964** (corner erosion 9.7% → 3.8%). `IProjectionTarget` grew an oriented `Project(point, out normal)` whose **default reports a ZERO normal** — the exact-zero spelling for "unoriented", since no unit normal is ever zero — so old targets keep working and face-aligned degrades to vertex projection rather than failing; both targets here override it from work they already do. **Face-aligned projection now COMPOSES with queue scheduling** — it was the one stage that did not, walking every face every pass whatever the scheduler decided: since a face contributes only to its OWN vertices, the accumulation skips every face with no vertex in the active set, measured (`UvSphere(1, 48, 32)`, target 0.08) 124 → 123 ms at 12 passes, 322 → 285 at 40 and **623 → 302 at 100** (2.06×, 3.07× against the plain sweep). The shape is the finding rather than the ratio: the whole-mesh figure keeps growing with the pass count while the restricted one nearly FLATTENS, so a converged mesh finally costs almost nothing per extra pass. It is **bit-identical**, which needed the walk to keep its ASCENDING face scan and skip only the projection query — float addition is not associative, so a gathered face list would have needed an explicit sort to restore the order (built, measured no faster, dropped). **`RegionRemesher`** remeshes ONE face selection in place (extract → remesh with the seam pinned → reinsert), so a bore wall can get 0.2 mm triangles while the plate is untouched; two settings are decided for the caller and both follow from the seam contract — `PreserveBoundary` is forced on, and a null `ProjectionTarget` defaults to one over the region *as extracted*, because "no projection" is the wrong default rather than the neutral one (smoothing is curvature flow, so the patch would sink and leave a dent inside an unmoved rim). Rim refinement rides on the region-operator work above; `FixedVertices` are base-mesh indices, mapped for the caller. `HoleFiller.FillSmoothed` stays a *different* operation — it builds a patch that does not exist yet, so its rim must be barred from splitting. **Hole-filling upper tiers**: `HoleFiller.FillMinimal` (minimum-weight triangulation of the rim's own vertices — the Barequet–Sharir/Liepa **dynamic program**, weight = (largest dihedral, area) lexicographic; refilling two removed adjacent box faces restores the box's *exact* volume, i.e. it puts the crease back rather than chording across it) and `FillSmoothed` (a relaxed membrane: coarse fan → remesh at the hole's own rim-edge length with the rim pinned → Laplacian relaxation, built and stitched as a standalone patch so the surrounding surface is untouched and the stitch welds by index, not by tolerance); `HoleFillOptions.Fallback` picks the tier `FillAll` uses for loops the planar and simple fills decline, and defaults to **`Minimal`**. It shipped as `None` on the reasoning that reporting a hole honestly beats inventing questionable geometry — which is right, and is exactly why `Minimal` is the better default: **it invents nothing**, since every vertex of a minimum-weight patch is already a vertex of the hole. The report-what-you-cannot-fill contract survives wherever the tier genuinely cannot decide (no admissible triangulation, or a rim past `MaxMinimalFillVertices`); what changed is that `MeshRepair.AutoRepair` now *closes* a long non-planar rim instead of naming it. `Fallback = None` restores the old behaviour exactly. **HoleFiller** (simple fan + planar nested-hole earcut on `FitPlane3` frames — orientation intrinsic from boundary walk order, ring-aware chord zip repairs earcut's collinear filtering; fills the annular caps `MeshPlaneCut` refuses); **MeshExtrude** (patch extrude + open-surface thicken — wall quads are exactly the directed edges freed by moving the patch, winding correct by construction); selections (immutable Grow/Contract) + connected components (per-component area/volume/closed + extraction); viewer renders meshes
4. **B-Rep engine** 🔶 modeling operations done — curves (`Line3d`, `Circle3d`, `Ellipse3d`, **`Parabola3d`/`Hyperbola3d`** (focal / cosh-sinh parameterizations completing the analytic conic family; parabola has closed-form arc length) and **`OffsetCurve3d`** (planar offset as first-class geometry, exact O′ = (1 − dκ)·C′ — |d| vs curvature radius deliberately unvalidated, as OCCT), with **`Curve3d` exposing virtual `DerivativeAt`/`SecondDerivativeAt`** overridden exactly by every analytic curve and wrapper; `NurbsCurve` with exact rational conics, **exact analytic derivatives** (`DerivativeAt`/`SecondDerivativeAt` via The NURBS Book A2.3 + the rational quotient rule; `TangentAt` overridden — the finite-difference note below now applies only to curves without an exact override) and **`NurbsCurve.InterpolatePoints`** (chord-length parameterization, natural-end open cubic via tridiagonal Thomas solve, or C2-seam closed periodic; exact pass-through), `ReversedCurve`/`TransformedCurve` wrappers with `Underlying` for sampling rules), surfaces (`PlaneSurface`, `CylinderSurface`, `SphereSurface`, `NurbsSurface` — plus **`NurbsSurface.InterpolatePoints`/`Approximate`** (`GeomAPI_PointsToBSpline`, The NURBS Book §9.2/§9.4): the tensor-product generalization of the curve fit, with chord parameters AVERAGED per direction so both share one parameterization (the loft-crack rule for a grid) and the fit separates into per-column-then-per-row 1-D solves (natural-end cubic for interpolation; endpoint-fixed `NᵀN` normal equations by dense Cholesky for a coarser-than-data least-squares net, sized by explicit control counts or grown to a tolerance). Exact oracles: interpolation passes through every grid point and a coplanar grid stays exactly planar; approximation reproduces the grid at full control count (a determined system's zero residual) and keeps a coplanar grid planar however coarse the net (least squares is linear, so an affine coordinate relation survives) — `ExtrudedSurface`, `RevolvedSurface` with angle, `SweptSurface` with rotation-minimizing frames), topology (`BrepSolid`→…→`BrepVertex` with `Validate` + Euler–Poincaré incl. genus), `SolidFactory`: `MakeBox`/`MakeCylinder`/**`MakeCone`** (frustum or apex — the side is an exact `RevolvedSurface` of a slanted line generator, deliberately NOT a new surface type: the revolved-band machinery already provides projection/tessellation/intersection/wrap-split/STEP; rim circles built on the surface's own radial frame stay phase-aligned with u=0; a zero radius makes that end a pole like sphere poles) + **`Extrude` (incl. shear + hole profiles), `Revolve` (full or partial turn, holes on partial, pipe elbows from closed profiles), `Sweep` (RMF along open paths, holes)** over `Profile` (planar closed chain; winding auto-corrected). **Mesh engine additions**: `ConvexHull` (quickhull: extent-scaled epsilon for all visibility tests, coplanar points absorbed by consistent triangulation, degenerate inputs throw, output passes manifold-validating Build) and `HalfEdgeMesh.Transformed(Matrix4d)` (negative-determinant transforms flip winding so meshes stay outward-oriented). **`Shape.Cone`** (Native all three reps), **`Shape.Hull`** (mesh-construction: B-Rep-Impossible, implicit/mesh Bridged via quickhull over tessellated vertices — exact for polyhedral operands), **`Shape.Mirror(point, normal)`** (Householder reflection through the transform node; implicit-**exact** — the compiler decomposes improper similarities as m = (m·FlipZ)·FlipZ, lowers at identity, wraps `Sdf.Mirror`, places the proper remainder; mesh exact with winding flip; B-Rep Native for box/cylinder/extrude/sphere/torus/cone — mirrored cylinders keep exact `Circle3d` rims because FlipZ fixes the rim plane — and **mirrored revolve/sweep/rim/drill are Native too**: a reflection conjugates a rotation — F∘Rot(d,φ)∘F = Rot(−F·d,φ), the LH-thread identity — so a mirrored revolve is the same angular sweep about the NEGATED transformed axis; sweeps need no fix at all because rotation-minimizing transport is intrinsic and commutes with any isometry; rim surgery and drill tools commute with isometries, with selectors keeping their run-on-the-LOWERED-solid contract. **Draft/Shell/RoundEdges/Loft (and the pure taper, which IS a two-section loft) are Native under a mirror too, and needed no identity at all** — each is defined by LENGTHS and ANGLES, which every isometry preserves, so the operation applied to the mirrored child IS the mirrored operation; the only directional input is Draft's pull, which takes its LINEAR IMAGE un-negated (a pull direction is transported by the map, where a revolve's axis is conjugated by it, hence the negation there and not here) and works because `Draft.Apply` chooses its rotation SENSE by measurement rather than from a handedness convention. Verified against analytic volumes rather than against a mirrored twin, which a flipped pull would match: a 5° draft about a block's BASE gives 1341.42 narrowing and 1542.99 widening, two hundred units apart. `SheetMetalBody` is the one refusal left in this family and it is a real one: a flange tree is ORDERED and quoted on named edges, so a reflection would need it rebuilt the other way round rather than re-placed). `BRepTessellator` handles planar (multi-loop, earcut) /cylinder/extruded/revolved/swept faces with edge-consistent grid sampling and seam zipping. Notes: default `Curve3d.TangentAt` uses 2nd-order one-sided differences at domain ends (sweep frames are sensitive to start-tangent error); `PolygonTriangulator` is a faithful mapbox-earcut port — its collinear filtering can merge exactly-collinear boundary runs, which the tessellator's zip pass repairs — with **one deliberate departure: a sliver ear is DEFERRED, not clipped, while any other ear remains**. Earcut filters *exactly* collinear vertices, but boundary samples of a straight edge that came through a boolean are collinear only to a few ulps, so a run of them survives and one is eventually clipped as a facet whose normal direction is decided by round-off. The fix is neither clipping nor removing (the middle sample belongs to no other triangle, so dropping the facet opens a T-junction — a guarded drop in `BRepTessellator` was built and measured to do nothing): **that vertex is a corner of a FAT triangle once its neighbours are consumed, so preferring any other ear reroutes the diagonal instead of eating the run.** Sliver-ness is a HEIGHT (twice the area over the longest edge) at 1e-13 × the polygon's extent — the scale-free tier, and the same measure the tessellation audit counts degenerate facets with, so the two cannot disagree about what a sliver is. The preference cannot change a sliver-free polygon's output and cannot fail (a sweep finding nothing but slivers takes them); `TessellationCorpusQualityTests.Corpus_EmitsNoDegenerateFacets` now pins **zero** across the corpus at every density, where it used to pin "at most one, and only from a planar face". **Surface–surface intersection done** (`SurfaceIntersection.Intersect(a, b, region)`): analytic curves for plane/plane (line), plane/cylinder (circle, ellipse, or parallel lines), plane/sphere, sphere/sphere, parallel cylinders; general marching tracer (grid seeding + BVH pairing + damped Gauss–Newton refinement + tangent-predictor/Newton-corrector stepping, periodic-aware, multi-branch, closed-loop detection) for all other pairs, returning `PolylineCurve3d` (exact at vertices, chordal between). New curves: `Ellipse3d`, `PolylineCurve3d`. **Trimming groundwork done**: `Surface.TryProjectPoint` (inverse evaluation — exact for plane/cylinder/sphere, Gauss–Newton otherwise), `FaceGeometry` (curve pullback into parameter space with periodic-u unwrapping, `LoopSignedArea`, point-in-face by upward-v ray parity with periodic segment compaction), `FaceSplitter.SplitByClosedCurve` (closed interior curves → face-with-hole + disk sharing one manifold edge; `createDisk:false` hands the edge's second use to another face). End-to-end drill rehearsal in `DrillTests`: box + intersection circles + cap splitting + bore-wall assembly → valid genus-1 solid with exact volume. **Open-curve face splitting done**: `TopologyEditor.SplitEdge` (splits an edge at a parameter, patching every using loop — neighbor faces stay consistent), `FaceSplitter.SplitByCurve` (full parameter-space arrangement: polyline crossing detection + 2×2 Newton refinement, boundary-edge splitting with endpoint-vertex reuse, interior curve segments as shared two-use edges via `CurveSegment`, sub-face tracing by smallest-clockwise-turn walking, CW loops assigned as holes to their smallest containing CCW loop). **`FaceSplitter.SplitByCurves(face, curves)`** takes a face's whole curve list — `BrepBoolean.SplitAll` hands it over, so ONE place decides between the curve-at-a-time CASCADE (bit-for-bit what every existing boolean gets) and ONE SIMULTANEOUS ARRANGEMENT, the only way to place a curve that TERMINATES inside the face: nodes against the boundary, against every other curve, at coincident ENDPOINTS and at mandatory breaks, then one `TraceFaces` walk. `SplitByCurve` is the one-curve call into it. Verified through whole solids: single cut (exact volume), cross cut through a split-created vertex (4 quadrants), annular cap cut beside its hole (hole follows the correct side). **Trimmed-face tessellation + cut-through-hole booleans done**: `TrimmedFaceTessellator` (Interop) renders faces whose loops don't cover the surface's grid domain, routing in **four tiers**: a single-loop region that is a **band** — two boundary chains monotone in one parameter joined by a single rung at each end, the shape of every mitered rim-fillet band and every partial cylinder fragment — is **zipped** chain-to-chain (the chains are already paired, so the merge walk the periodic-band path uses IS the correct triangulation, minus the period closure); then band-with-holes seam unrolling — whose unrolled region is **slab-swept** (`ZipSlabs`/`SweepMonotone`): each hole is split at its extreme-u vertices into a lower and an upper u-monotone chain, cutting the band into u-monotone slabs triangulated by the textbook stack sweep, with cut halves shared verbatim between adjacent slabs so it is watertight by index with no invented vertices, and a global uv-area identity as the closing guard (any failure returns null and defers, so the new path can never be worse than the old); then periodic bands; and **only then** the exact-coordinate ear clipper (shortest-diagonal ears, ~1e-9 jitter blocking band, hole bridging) — plus surface-exact midpoint refinement (Steiner-by-refinement, no upfront classification). Routing is a two-sided 3D match of loop samples against the natural grid boundary (the welding invariant, so existing faces can't misroute). **Why the tier order is a numerical rule and not an optimization**: ear-clipping a band lets the shortest-diagonal rule eat the dense boundary chains, and three consecutive samples of a smooth boundary curve span a sliver whose normal is `T × K` — the curve's **binormal**, not the surface's. Since `T × K = k_g·N + k_n·(T × N)`, the sliver agrees with the surface only where the *geodesic* curvature dominates — and a miter ellipse meets the top of a fillet **tangent to the flat face**, where `k_g` passes through zero, so the sliver normal turns perpendicular to the surface's and **its sign is rounding noise**. Half faced inward: `Shape.Box(30, 20, 6).FilletEdges(2, topRim)` rendered a dark folded lens at every mitered corner (13 088 triangles, **808 inverted**, worst facet-vs-surface normal dot −0.22; now **280 triangles, none inverted**, worst 0.99994). Worse than the count, the ear-clipped path **did not converge at all** — refinement subdivided the clipper's long diagonals, the monotone-decrease rule cut those cascades unpredictably, and volumes wandered (3516.70 / 3517.03 / 3516.82 / 3516.84 / 3517.04 at curveSamples 24–192) around an analytic 3517.2274 they never reached, before refusing outright at 256. The strip path is **linear** in the sample count and converges **quadratically from inside** (−4.8e-5 at default quality). Its rung epsilon is the **1e-6 inverse-evaluation tier expressed RELATIVELY** (1e-6 × the loop's extent in that parameter) — u and v carry no model units, so an absolute epsilon there is meaningless; and note that anisotropic uv is a trap for any Euclidean heuristic (a mitered band is ~1.57×1.0 in uv while the surface is 3.14×30 in model units, so "shortest diagonal" in raw uv is not shortest on the surface — precisely why the clipper chose to eat the chains). **The silent grid fallback is gone**: a trimmed face the trimmed path cannot handle now THROWS, naming the surface type, where it sits, its loop shapes, both sample counts and the reason. A fallback is only legitimate when it computes the same thing more coarsely — the natural grid covers the whole parameter rectangle, so for a trimmed face it computed something else, welding into an open mesh with no complaint (the tessellation-side twin of `BrepBoolean.Verified`). **Never earcut pulled-back curved-face loops** — `PolygonTriangulator` filters exactly-collinear vertices and iso-parameter runs ARE exactly uv-collinear but not 3D-collinear: a dropped sample is an unzippable crack, and jittered earcut breeds zero-area folds that refine into non-manifold welds. `FaceSplitter` hardened for the boolean path: tolerant partial-curve pullback runs (extrapolated run-end seeds), crossing refinement by 3D curve–curve Gauss–Newton (projected-uv Newton fails near bounded domain edges), inclusive crossing seeds (cuts through split-created vertices), and **reversed-face splitting** (CW-aware tightest-turn tracing, `IsReversed` preserved — cascaded booleans on boolean output need this). `BrepBoolean`: both sides of a shared closed intersection curve take a mandatory seam break at `Domain.Start` (the wrap-split side anchors its seam vertex there), and probe points prefer the largest fragment triangle's centroid (sliver centroids sit within the SDF's sagitta of the other solid's curved surface — misclassification). End-to-end: drilled box minus a slot narrower than the bore → Validate-clean genus-3 solid, closed manifold tessellation, volume within 7e-4 of analytic. Two-ring band faces with hole loops tessellate properly (seam in the largest u-gap, unrolled + ear-clipped with hole bridging; tracer `PolylineCurve3d` edges are sampled at exact vertex parameters — uniform sampling lands on chords ~1e-4 off-surface); `Refine` terminates via a monotone-decrease enqueue rule with fail-safe fallback. Remaining trimmed-face gaps: pole-bands-with-holes and |winding|>1 fall back to grid, no Delaunay quality flips, and **tier 4 `TriangulateRegion` still ear-clips**, so it would hit the same forced fan on a non-wrapping region with an exactly uv-collinear boundary run — nothing exercises it today, so it was deliberately left alone. **Sphere-piercing booleans fixed** (was: silent wrong geometry when a sphere pierces box faces so every intersection curve is a closed circle interior to a face) — lessons: `BrepQueries.Bounds` must sample surface domains, not just edges (a hemisphere's equator-only edges hid the dome from the face-pair prefilter); tracer-clipped arcs can NEVER be refined against a boundary they stop short of — promote full-turn revolved spheres to exact analytic plane∩sphere circles (`TrySphereCarrier`, generator sampled, never `Underlying`); closed interior curves need mandatory seam breaks honored on BOTH sides (`SplitByInteriorClosedCurve` builds matching arc pairs); period-wrapping traced loops are band boundaries paired bottom-to-top by v, not area-classified holes. `Shape.Drill` validates up front (overlap/tangency per point pair; far-face coplanarity at B-Rep lowering via the `DrillShape` node) instead of failing deep in tessellation. **Cross-drill-through-bore booleans fixed** — the root cause was NOT the wrap-split but silent no-op pullback: tracer polylines sampled at uniform parameters sit step²/8r (~1e-4) off the carrier surface, past the 1e-6 inverse-evaluation tolerance, so `PullCurveRuns` returned zero runs and unsplit bands were whole-classified away. Lessons now baked in: **tracer polylines are on-surface only at their vertices — ALL pullback/parity/crossing sampling goes through one shared rule** (`FaceGeometry.ExactSampleParameters`: polylines → vertex parameters, `CurveSegment`-over-polyline → mapped base vertices, else uniform), never per-call-site patches — **and "one shared rule" only holds if every caller ASKS it**: two later sites restated the test instead and both got it wrong the same way, matching only a RAW `PolylineCurve3d`, so a `CurveSegment` wrapping one (exactly what the face splitter hands back after a cut) fell through to uniform sampling. `BRepTessellator.SampleEdge` put every interior sample a sagitta off the surface, and `TraceFaces`' tightest-turn probes read their departure direction 2% along an edge — mid-chord for a polyline — so `ProjectNear` returned a uv for a point the surface does not contain and the angle the walk sorts by was noise. Both now call the rule, which is public alongside an `IsPolylineBacked` predicate; that predicate deliberately does NOT test `Underlying`, since a segment's underlying curve is its base's and says nothing about the parameter mapping the rule turns on. Probes fall back to the FAR endpoint on a single-chord edge (an on-surface vertex, and a chord's exact direction); a **non-planar wrap cut cannot become a domain boundary** — `SplitBandByNonPlanarWrapCurve` sub-bands keep the original surface and rely on trimmed-face tessellation (the trim-the-surface rule applies only to constant-parameter cuts); multiple wrapping cuts on one band decide containment **per fragment by loop parity** (every fragment shares the full carrier, so every cut pulls back onto all of them). Equal-radius perpendicular cylinders (tangent bicylinder) are rejected loudly (overlapping v-ranges). **Automatic B-Rep booleans done** (`BrepBoolean.Union/Intersection/Difference` in Interop): per-face-pair `SurfaceIntersection`, seam-aligned splitting (each side takes the other's crossing params as mandatory breaks so tessellation welds), fragment classification by probing the other solid's `MeshSdf` (hybrid trick), reversed faces for subtracted tools (`BrepFace.IsReversed`, tessellator flips), wrap-splitting of periodic bands (`SplitBandByWrapCurve`: bore wall → two exactly-reconstructed sub-bands), and circle-extrusion→cylinder promotion for exact bore circles. **Intersection curves are CLIPPED to the pair's shared trim** (`ClipToFace`): a carrier is unbounded or bounded only by its own parameter rectangle, so the curve runs past both faces, and while each face's splitter discarded the stretches outside ITSELF nothing discarded those outside the OTHER — so a face was split along geometry the pair does not share (a pocket's four wall lines cut a host face into **9** fragments where the tool's footprint asks for **2**; a plate with a flush pocket went 18 faces → **11**, a two-bore plate with a pocket 20 → **13**, same volume). **The rule is ASYMMETRIC and that is the whole design**: each face drops only the stretches inside ITSELF and outside its partner, because the symmetric form — hand both faces the intersection of the trims — cuts the curve exactly ON this face's own boundary wherever the two faces SHARE a boundary, turning a transversal crossing into a tangential touch (`Box(20,20,10) & Box(10,30,10)`, side walls meeting along their full height: every vertical curve stopped on both rims and tracing did not close). Keeping what lies outside this face costs nothing — the splitter drops it by loop parity — and restores the crossing. Breakpoints are computed ONCE per face pair from both faces' exact crossings, so a shared stretch is cut identically on both sides; a curve surviving whole is returned as ITSELF (a closed curve must stay closed — wrap-splitting and hole-splitting key on `IsClosed`), which is what leaves every transversal boolean bit-identical. **The keep/drop test errs toward KEEPING** (a stretch wrongly kept only reproduces the un-clipped behaviour; one wrongly dropped loses a seam silently and returns two touching shells): a probe that will not project counts as inside, parity is the new **`FaceGeometry.ContainsTwoSided`** so a POLE-BOUNDED face answers instead of calling every point on itself outside (a sphere-through-a-box union otherwise lost its whole seam curve and came back at Euler 4 — the same two-sided rule `PlanarSection` already used), and a stretch running ALONG the boundary counts as inside (where two solids mate the shared rim IS a face boundary on one side). Three downstream mechanisms learned that a closed curve may now arrive clipped: the closed-curve seam anchor is conditional on BOTH sides still seeing the closed curve (a slot through a bore shares with the slot's floor only the arc inside the slot's width, so the wall is cut there and never wrap-splits while the floor still sees the full circle — the stale anchor left six unpaired edges); a closed CHAIN that WRAPS the period goes to the arrangement rather than `SplitByClosedCurveChain`, since the two sides of a wrapping cut are two bands and neither is a hole in the other (`FaceSplitter.ChainWrapsPeriod`); and that chain's pulled polyline must be **unwrapped stepwise across its junctions** — `PullCurve` unwraps a run only against itself, so on a periodic surface the concatenation jumps a whole period at every junction and the signed area deciding the winding is noise (measured: a Ø6 tool crossing a rounded box's fillet bands gave its 8-curve chain, 227° straddling u=0, a CW disk on an unreversed face, and every one of its eight edges then had the same sense as its partner). `TraceFaces`' band pairing also gained the tie-break wrapping boundaries at the same v need: **tops first**, because they are the two sides of ONE cut and a cut closes the band below before opening the band above (grouped by a scale-free v window, never a tolerant comparator, which would not be a strict weak ordering). Boolean output is **topologically sealed** (`TopologyEditor.SealSeams`: stale-use pruning, vertex unification, seam-edge merging; Difference re-winds reversed faces' loops) — results pass `Validate()` and Euler–Poincaré with correct genus. **Loft / draft / shelling** (wired into the `Shape` API — `Shape.Loft`/`LoftAlong`/`Draft`/`Shell(t, openings)`/`RoundEdges`, see the Modeling status — with `docs/examples/loft-draft-shell.md`; `Draft.Apply` also takes per-face angles in one call — corners solve once from the two FINAL planes, bit-equal to the chained spelling — and `Shelling.Shell` a per-face wall thickness, both exact because every inner corner was already a three-plane solve): `SolidFactory.Loft(sections, Smooth|Ruled)` skins a solid through profiles via `LoftedSurface`, P(u,v) = Σ αₖ(v)·Cₖ(uₖ) where α is the **cardinal basis of B-spline interpolation, inverted ONCE at construction** — that is the load-bearing decision, because a chord-length reparameterization recomputed per u gives every strip its own v mapping and cracks every shared rail; αₖ(v_j) = δⱼₖ holds by exact equality, so the v=0/v=1 grid rows reproduce the end sections bit-for-bit. Mismatched segment counts are rejected (no degree elevation yet); alignment minimizes **centroid-relative** squared corner travel — *the lesson*: leaving the sections' separation in that objective makes it a large constant plus a tiny quadratic well, which cost eight digits and left 3e-8 of residual twist, past the weld tolerance. `Draft.Apply` rotates each selected face's plane about its **neutral line** by exactly the angle (n′ = n·cosθ + p̂⊥·sinθ, corners by three-plane Cramer solves), so it is closed-form, composable (twice by θ/2 == once by θ to 1e-12) and still emits selectable `PlaneSurface` faces; curved faces, holed caps and profile-folding tapers are rejected loudly. `Shelling.Offset/Shell` is the B-Rep counterpart of the SDF-only `Shape.Shell`, exact for polyhedra (an offset plane is a plane; offset vertices are three-plane intersections), carrying topology verbatim so a plate's outline shrinks while its bore *grows*; no opening ⇒ sealed void as a second shell, two openings ⇒ genus-1 tube. **Curved faces work too, and are equally exact** — the corner machinery that blocked them is `SurfaceOffset` + `SurfaceCorner` + `ImplicitSurface` + `CarrierBody`, built ONCE for three consumers (curved shelling, curved draft, variable-radius fillet bands). `SurfaceOffset` keeps every carrier in its own family (plane→plane, cylinder→cylinder, cone→cone, torus→torus, since a revolve's offset is the revolve of its OFFSET GENERATOR) with frames, spans and sweep angles carried over verbatim so u = 0 does not move; `SurfaceCorner` solves the corner as the root of a small square system — three planes by Cramer, anything with a closed-form implicit distance by Newton, machine precision either way, residual always reported. **The step is MINIMUM-NORM, and that one choice deletes every special case**: the carriers routinely do not pin a point (a seam vertex has two incident faces because a closed rim starts and ends there; a tangent junction has three faces of which two are the SAME carrier — a pipe elbow's profile circle split into two arcs offsets to two halves of ONE circle, a sphere's hemispheres to one sphere), and confining the step to the span of the carriers' normals reproduces every rule that would otherwise be hand-written (a cylinder's seam stays in its seam half-plane, an elbow's junction keeps its angle on the offset profile circle, a sphere's equator moves radially — each of which was written down as a synthetic constraint plane first, before the general rule replaced all of them). A corner CURVE is where the exactness brand bites: analytic pairs give a conic trimmed to its corners, everything else is the chordal tracer, so `CornerPolicy.ExactOnly` is the default and refuses by name while `AllowTraced` is opt-in and LABELS the result with its measured deviation — and even then the curve's ENDS are replaced by the solved corner points, so the chord error stays strictly interior and never reaches a vertex. Two construction rules the consumers paid for: **a curved rim is CONSTRUCTED and then VERIFIED, never intersected and trusted** (a concentric circle about the original's own axis and phase lands on the offset carriers' grids by construction, while the same circle recovered from an intersection is silently chordal wherever the analytic tier does not reach — every sample is measured against BOTH carriers, which is exactly what refuses a SEALED pipe elbow, whose moved cap planes cut the offset torus in a quartic rather than a circle); and **a domain-driven carrier must be re-trimmed to its new loops** (an inward offset moves a cone's rims axially as well as radially, so the inner band kept sampling its old extent and the trimmed tessellator refused the face outright — the generator parameter for that trim is solved against the loop points' own axial coordinate on the exact carrier, never by projection, the vCut lesson). **Two fits broke on CLOSED generators, the commonest input there is**: a circle extruded into a cylinder returns to its start, so a first-to-last chord is zero and a (first, middle, last) circumcircle is degenerate — both now take well-separated samples, and `CircularArc` fits over the EDGE's domain rather than the curve's, because a partial revolve's rail is a whole `Circle3d` used over a quarter turn. Still refused: carriers with no same-family offset (swept, NURBS), non-circular curved edges, and >3-valent vertices (over-determined — the offset surfaces do not meet in a point and it needs corner PATCHES, not a better solve). **Direct editing** (`DirectEdit.OffsetFaces`/`MoveFaces`/`DeleteFaces`; `Shape.OffsetFaces`/`MoveFaces`/`DeleteFaces` over `FaceSetRef`; docs `examples/direct-editing.md`; design.md §5): what makes an IMPORTED body editable — a solid with no construction history has no parameter to change, so the only handle on it is its faces. **The backlog's "the machinery already exists" was right and the gap was smaller than it supposed**: `Shelling.Shell` already took a per-face wall thickness and already held its openings still at ZERO (a non-uniform offset array, in production, with exact corners), while `Shelling.Offset` took only a scalar — so the whole of face offsetting is the per-face `Offset` overload that was missing beside it, with `OffsetFaces` supplying a law returning zero off the selection. Both tiers inherit unchanged (the uniform overload delegates with a constant law, producing the identical array, so polyhedral output is untouched; a curved face whose law returns zero keeps its carrier object VERBATIM) and no refusal is restated. **A MOVE of a planar face is an offset BY DERIVATION** — a plane is invariant under translation within itself, so displacing a face by v reaches exactly the plane an offset of v·n̂ reaches — and implementing it as that reduction makes two behaviours facts rather than arrangements (a face moved parallel to itself does not move at all; several faces moved by one vector each take their own projection) and makes Native-under-MIRROR a theorem, since the operation is a dot product and an orthogonal map preserves dot products. A curved face is refused for a SPECIFIC reason: `CarrierBody.ConcentricRim` rebuilds each rim concentric with the ORIGINAL, right for an offset (which leaves the axis alone) and false for a translation (which moves it), and unchecked the hypothesis fails three stages downstream in `OnBothCarriers`. **Delete-and-heal is a CONDITION whose gate is the entry's own verification clause**: an edge is *wound* when one of its two faces goes and the other stays, and the deletion heals by DROPPING loops exactly when every wound edge lies on a complete interior loop of a kept **planar** face (a boss, a pad, a pocket liner). The planar clause is the correctness condition, not a convenience — a plane is bounded by its outer loop alone so an interior loop really is a hole, whereas a cylinder's or an extruded band's second loop is routinely the far END of the band, and dropping one there opens the solid into an infinite tube satisfying **both** `Validate()` and Euler–Poincaré (measured: V=1, E=1, F=2, L=2 gives V−E+2F−L = 2), so nothing downstream could catch it and the first gate written — "any loop that is not `Loops[0]`" — would have shipped it silently. Because the operation shares geometry and rebuilds only topology, a deletion REPRODUCES the body a feature was added to rather than resembling it: asserted bit for bit against a plate that never had the hole, and against the closed-form volume on a plate whose boss came from a real boolean. Verified: a box face pushed by d changes the volume by exactly A·d (both sides closed forms), while an OBLIQUE face is the complement that makes that a statement about geometry rather than code — the change is the frustum integral, measurably 140.78 against the naive 127.28 (10.6%, not round-off); a cylinder's wall grows by the analytic annulus; edited output survives a further boolean. Refused by name: a wound only PARTLY bounding a neighbour's loop (healing needs the neighbours EXTENDED until they meet, a different operation that can have no answer at all — a box's sides extended past its deleted top never meet), a wound consuming an outer loop, a non-planar neighbour, an empty selection, a whole-solid selection, and a curved face MOVE. A SCOPE limit found by a DOCS RENDER rather than a unit test (the argument for executable examples) has since ✅ landed for OFFSET: `CarrierBody` refused a REVERSED face, and a difference marks the subtracted tool's walls reversed, so a CURVED offset reached a primitive and an imported body but not a bore this kernel cut. The message read as though the offset DIRECTION were ambiguous and it is not — an offset moves a face along its OUTWARD normal, which for a reversed face is the negative of its surface's — so `CarrierBody.Lift` offsets a reversed carrier by `−distance` (the sign lives there once, since every consumer states the offset outward), gated behind `CarrierBody.Recognize(solid, allowReversedFaces)`: the offset path passes `true` while SHELL and DRAFT keep the refusal (their cavity `Flipped` twin and `Draft.Taper`'s surface-normal lean are not yet sense-aware), so forward-face offsets and all shell/draft output are bit-identical. `DirectEditVolumeTests` pushes a `Cylinder(20,30) − Cylinder(9,40)` housing's bore wall +2 (bore r9→r7) and −2 (r9→r11), each changing the volume by the exact annulus `π(9² − r'²)·30`. The MOVE and SHELL of a reversed curved face stay filed. **One measured property, of the signed-zero family**: a re-solved corner reproduces every nonzero coordinate BIT FOR BIT and can return −0.0 where the original held +0.0, because the Cramer solve divides by a determinant of −1 — equal to 0.0 under every value test, differing only in the sign bit, so it is invisible to everything except the bit-level "did this face really not move?" fixture that reaches for it; the fixture moves off the origin and the property is pinned by its own test. Two TEST-side traps re-learned: `BrepMassProperties` EXTRAPOLATES by default, so a discrete n-gon oracle disagrees with a correct answer (by 0.39 on a cylinder annulus), and a face must be located by `Bounds()` — a lowered boss's cap carries its SEAM VERTEX as its `IsPlanar` origin and its wall arrives as an `ExtrudedSurface`, not a `CylinderSurface`. **`Draft` rides the same rebuild**: a face of revolution about the pull direction tapers by rotating its GENERATOR in its own axial half-plane about the point where that generator crosses the neutral plane — the same "rotate about the neutral line" rule one dimension down — so a drafted cylinder is EXACTLY a cone and a drafted torus band another torus band; the rotation SENSE is chosen by measurement (build both candidates, keep the one leaning further toward the pull direction) rather than derived, because deriving it means re-proving three sign conventions that have each cost this kernel real geometry for an answer one dot product settles. **Filleting**: `Filleting.FilletEdge` for closed circular rims (planar cap ↔ coaxial cylinder band) via exact quarter-torus `RevolvedSurface`; general fillet chains with corner patches are future work. **Biarc fitting + a 2D curve family** (`BiArcFit`/`BiArc2d`/`BiArcChain2d/3d`, `Curve2d` with `Line2d`/`Arc2d`/`BezierCurve2d`/`NurbsCurve2d`, public `BSplineBasis`, `ArcLengthTable2d`): two tangent-continuous arcs through a point+tangent pair, tolerance-driven chains through a polyline, and a 3D wrapper turning a **planar** traced polyline into exact `Line3d` + rational-arc `NurbsCurve` pieces. **Adoption is opt-in and the deviation is always reported** — nothing in the kernel fits biarcs implicitly, `MaxDeviation` is the max distance from an *input sample* to the fit (3D combines in-plane error and out-of-plane flattening in quadrature), and non-planar input is refused rather than silently flattened. Two lessons: the free parameter must be computed as **`d = |v|²/(√disc + v·t)`**, algebraically identical to `(√disc − v·t)/denom` but accurate as the tangents become parallel *and reducing exactly to the equal-tangent case at denom = 0* — which deletes the reference implementation's epsilon branch on the SQUARED quantity `|t1+t2|² ≈ 4` and the broken semicircle case behind it (g3's assigns its first arc twice and never sets the second); and the second arc is built **backwards from the end point and reversed**, so both data points and both end tangents are exact and all round-off lands on the interior joint, where nothing has to weld. The 2D family diverges from `Curve3d` deliberately: derivatives are **abstract** (every 2D curve here is analytic — no finite-difference fallback for a new type to inherit by accident) and `Arc2d`'s sweep is **signed**, so orientation is intrinsic instead of a flag plus a reverse-and-hope repair. `BSplineBasis` (A2.1/A2.2/A2.3) is now one public copy shared by `NurbsCurve`, `NurbsCurve2d`, `NurbsSurface` and `LoftedSurface`. **`SweptSurface.TryProjectPoint` completes the 1D-projection family — by a different structural fact.** The RMF frame varies along the path so no parameter has a closed form, but every surface point at path parameter v lies in the frame's own plane there, so **`f(v) = (p − Path(v))·Tangent(v) = 0` is a scalar equation in v alone** (the foot-of-perpendicular condition), and u then follows from a 2D offset match: two decoupled 1D solves. f is not monotone on a curving path, so roots are **bracketed** by a 16-sample scan and refined by safeguarded bisection — the bracket guarantees convergence, not the seed. Measured **3.9×** (curved profile) and **5.2×** (full-circle tube) per projection. **The correctness win was larger than the speed win, and is the lesson worth keeping**: a sweep's *effective* profile is the generator **projected into the start-frame plane**, which can be a sliver whose two branches sit closer together than one seed interval; the sampled distance then shows ONE broad minimum straddling both, single-seed 1D Newton descends to whichever branch is nearer, and the projection silently returns the **mirrored** parameter — a point tens of millimetres away that still passes every structural check. Refining from every local minimum **and its two neighbours** (purely combinatorial, no epsilon) took round-trip acceptance 266/400 → 400/400 on a swept tube. `ExtrudedSurface`/`RevolvedSurface` since gained the same multi-seed rule on their 1D scans, and now also DEFER to the (multi-seeded) base grid on failure, so the overrides are never worse than the base by construction. **B-Rep booleans consume their inputs, so `Shape.From(brepSolid)` clones at the source boundary** (`BrepSolid.Clone()`: topology deep-copied, geometry shared — curves and surfaces are immutable, only topology mutates). **`BrepSolid.Transformed(Matrix4d)`** is that same walk with the geometry moved through the new **`GeometryTransform`** (per-type surface and curve mapping), each surface answered in its OWN family so the tessellator keeps its sampling rules and STEP its analytic entities. **It is a POSE, not a re-derivation, and PROPER RIGIDITY is exactly what buys that**: every parameterization in this kernel is built from lengths and angles, both of which an isometry preserves, so edge trim domains, seam phases and revolve angles are carried VERBATIM (asserted bitwise) rather than recomputed — and curve OBJECTS are mapped once and reused, since a seam curve backs two edges and a carrier backs many, and splitting one carrier into several numerically-equal copies is how a solid stops welding with no count changing. Curves never refuse (a `TransformedCurve` fallback is exact in position AND parameterization, and its `Underlying` still reports the base type); a surface has no such wrapper, so an unknown surface type refuses by name. Verified through the two consumers that can actually see a bad re-placement — a posed drilled plate re-tessellates closed at the same DISCRETE volume (compared against the un-posed mesh, not against πr²h, since an isometry preserves the inscribed n-gon exactly while the analytic value would only agree to the chord error) and then survives a SECOND boolean. **The filed assessment's "+uniform scale where the type allows" is wrong, and the reason is the finding**: the type is not the only party that has to allow it — `PolylineCurve3d` is parameterized by cumulative chord length, so scaling its points scales its DOMAIN, while a `BrepEdge` stores its trim domain separately and a `CurveSegment` stores base parameters in the base's units. Scaling the curve alone desynchronizes them silently, and every tracer-produced edge is polyline-backed, so uniform scale is refused for a BOOKKEEPING reason rather than a geometric one and the message says which. Shear/non-uniform scale change the surface FAMILY (a sheared cylinder is an elliptic cylinder, a sheared sphere an ellipsoid); a reflection reverses orientation, so every loop would need re-winding and the handedness-carrying types their own rule — `Shape.Mirror` already does all of that one layer up, so a second route here would be a riskier way to reach an answer the kernel already has. Nothing internal needs any of it (lowering bakes transforms into construction inputs), which is the point: it exists for geometry with no construction history to bake into — an imported STEP or IGES body being posed. **The failure is conditional, which is why it hid**: a tool whose curves stay interior to the body's faces (a plain through bore) only adds hole loops to freshly-built cap faces and survives two lowerings even unfixed, while a tool that crosses the body's edges (a notch, a corner bite) makes the second lowering throw `Hole loop is not contained in any sub-face` or produce an unclosed solid — and counts survive either way, so the damaged solid still *looks* intact. A `DrillShape` also lowers its body **once** now (the expansion's difference chain is peeled so the coplanarity validation and the subtraction share one lowering, with a fallback if the chain shape ever changes): measured **1.77×** on a 6+2-hole plate, the saving equal to exactly one body lowering. Holes are cross-validated against earlier `Drill` calls **on the same placement plane**; different planes are deliberately not compared, since opposing bores need a tool-vs-tool solid intersection rather than a 2D centre-distance test. **Mass properties** (OCCT `BRepGProp`): `MeshMassProperties.Compute(mesh, density)` in EngrCAD.Mesh is **exact for any polyhedron** (closed-form tetrahedral moments per facet, Newell area so concave n-gons are right where a fan-area sum is not), returning a `MassProperties` readonly struct that carries the *volume-weighted* second moment about the centroid S = ∫(r−c)(r−c)ᵀ dV and derives everything else (`Inertia` = ρ(tr S·Id − S), `InertiaAbout` by parallel axis, `Principal()` = ascending moments + a right-handed `Frame3d` at the centre of mass, `WithDensity`, `Transformed` — similarity only, since shear and non-uniform scale change the surface area in a way the properties alone cannot express, so it refuses rather than returning a wrong area — and `Combine`, which handles mixed densities and reports the bulk density). Storing S rather than I is the load-bearing choice: S transforms as a clean congruence and adds under the parallel-axis theorem, so every derived operation is two lines instead of four special cases. `BrepMassProperties.Compute` in Interop takes the B-Rep route by **tessellate-then-sum, deliberately**: exact-as-an-identity for planar-faced solids and O(h²) inscribed convergence for curved ones, with **Richardson extrapolation on by default** — measured relative volume error at the default 64 segments/circle drops 1.6e-3 → **1.9e-7** (cylinder), 2.2e-3 → 4.8e-7 (sphere), 2.0e-3 → 3.7e-7 (torus) and 1.1e-4 → **1.4e-8** for a drilled plate, i.e. through a boolean whose bore wall is a trimmed face.
