Exact-surface quadrature was rejected on purpose: it needs the trimmed parameter domain scanned against trimming curves that are themselves only approximate for tracer edges, so it would hide the same error behind a bigger integral. `Part.MassProperties(density)` / `PartInstance.MassProperties` / `instances.MassProperties(densityPerPart)` ride the cached `Part.TryGetSolid()` lowering rather than lowering again. **Shape healing** (OCCT `ShapeFix`): `ShapeHealing.Heal(solid, options)` repairs imported topology — the case being third-party STEP, since `StepReader` shares topology by entity identity (right for files this kernel wrote) while foreign writers emit each face's wire separately, so a solid arrives as a face soup with duplicated edges and merely-coincident vertices. Six switchable passes: vertex merging, small-edge collapse, degenerate-face removal, edge sewing, opt-in straight-edge refitting, wire re-ordering/re-sensing. **Every repair is a return value, not a log line** (`ShapeHealingReport`: per-pass counts, `NonManifoldEdgesBefore/After`, `OpenLoopsBefore/After`, `IsManifold`, `Notes`), matching the `MeshRepair`/`StepReadResult.Diagnostics` convention; `Analyze` is a dry run of the identical pipeline, and healing never modifies its input. `RefitStraightEdges` is OFF by default because it is the one pass that moves geometry, and boolean output whose vertices were already unified at the 1e-7 seam tier must not have its exactly-constructed lines dragged off their surfaces; curved gaps are *reported* rather than invented. **STEP export + import**: `StepWriter.Write/WriteFile` (AP214 `MANIFOLD_SOLID_BREP`, analytic surfaces/curves incl. rational NURBS, wrapper-curve simplification; swept surfaces not exportable) and `StepReader.Read/ReadFile` (full Part 21 parser + AP214 entity mapping back to `BrepSolid`; round-trips everything the writer emits — topology shared by entity identity, edge domains reconstructed exactly from vertex positions (closed-form conic phases, Newton with exact derivatives for B-splines), `SURFACE_OF_REVOLUTION` angle/generator-trims recovered from rails/rims by **bisection on the exact (radius, axial) profile residuals** (root solves — distance minimization stalls at √ε ≈ 1e-8, past weld tolerance); mm assumed, unknown entities skipped with diagnostics; verified on box/cylinder/drilled-plate genus-2/revolves/booleans/fillets at ~1e-12 volume agreement; known-lossy: `TransformedCurve(NurbsCurve)` exports as sampled degree-1 polylines). **Native B-Rep archive** (`BrepArchive` in BRep; `.ecb`, wired into `--export`): the LOSSLESS round-trip STEP cannot give — every curve and surface type round-trips as ITSELF, including the ones with no AP214 entity at all (`HelicalSurface`, i.e. every modelled thread; `LoftedSurface`; `SweptSurface`; `OffsetCurve3d`; `SpiralArc3d`; `PhaseShiftedCurve`) plus trimmed edge domains and `CurveSegment` mappings. **Text, and the reason is the testing culture rather than the geometry** (design.md §5): a numbered entity table with `#n` references and one entity per line, so a committed corpus archive can be DIFFED the way the golden fingerprints and byte-compared docs PNGs already are — a binary would win a few hundred KB on files nobody has complained about and would need a decoder written before anyone would look at it. Exactness is not the trade-off it sounds like: `"R"` formatting is a bijection on finite doubles, and the tests assert the strong form — **save→load→save is byte-for-byte a fixed point** across a 14-solid factory corpus plus 5 boolean-derived solids (volume held to 1e-12 relative, and a restored solid is re-booleaned and re-tessellated closed to prove it is a complete independently-owned graph). **The entity table keys on REFERENCE identity, never on structural equality, and that is load-bearing**: `BrepEdge.IsClosedEdge` IS `ReferenceEquals(StartVertex, EndVertex)`, so two coincident vertices and one shared vertex are DIFFERENT solids and a position-deduplicating format would silently change topology; the same rule gives sharing for free (an edge used by two faces is written once, a seam curve shared by two edges comes back as one object). Frames rebuild through `Frame3d.FromOrthonormal`, the only factory storing its axes verbatim — the `AxisRef` never-re-normalize-a-unit-vector lesson, since the re-deriving factories would move them by ulps and the fixed point would break. Unknown versions, foreign units, dangling and forward references are all refused BY NAME. Measured finding worth keeping: the two constructors that re-normalize a stored direction (`RevolvedSurface.AxisDirection`, `OffsetCurve3d.PlaneNormal`) turn out to be **idempotent on their own output** for every corpus member, so the fixed point holds with no exact-construction back door — which was not obvious in advance, and is exactly why the assertion is a byte comparison rather than a tolerance (a tolerance would have hidden the question). Landing it needed two accessor gaps closed: `SweptSurface.StartX`/`FrameCount` (the frame count is part of a sweep's IDENTITY, not a hint — every interior frame is interpolated between the computed ones) and `SweptRailCurve.Surface`/`LocalOffset`. Scope is deliberately GEOMETRY, not document: solids, not scenes, poses, features or materials. **IGES 5.3 import** (`IgesReader` + the internal `IgesParser` in BRep; docs `examples/import.md`) — **import only, deliberately**, per the standing assessment: writing it would mean maintaining a second lossier encoding of geometry STEP already carries better. The useful subset turned out to be decided by **which entities map onto surfaces the kernel already has**, not by which are common — 118 ruled is a two-section `LoftedSurface`, 122 tabulated cylinder an `ExtrudedSurface`, 120 a `RevolvedSurface`, each a few lines because the target type exists; alongside 110/100/104/126/128/102/116/108/124 and 142/144 trimmed surfaces. Entity **186 MSBO (+502/504/508/510/514) is filed rather than built**: it is a second parallel topology encoding inside the same format, the same size of job as the whole rest of the reader, and it buys correctness `ShapeHealing` already supplies. **The result is a FACE SOUP and the design says so** (`IsFaceSoup`): IGES carries no shared topology, so two faces meeting along an edge reference two coincident-but-distinct curves, the shell's edges are used once, `Validate()` refuses it, and the honest pipeline is three explicit steps — read, `ShapeHealing.Heal`, `Shape.From` — with `Shape.From(path)` deliberately NOT learning `.igs` since it would hand back geometry that fails at lowering. Three lessons: **record structure is validated up front and refused by name** (column 73 is the section letter and 74–80 the sequence number on EVERY card, so a file without that shape is rejected before a parameter is read — `StlReader`'s size-test-before-sniffing rule, applied to the first card-image reader in the codebase); **Hollerith counts must be honoured when splitting fields**, which is not a nicety — an author field containing the parameter delimiter shifts every later Global parameter by one, and Global parameter 14 is the UNIT FLAG, so the failure mode is a file that imports cleanly at the wrong scale (it has a test); and **entity 104's conic type is classified from its own coefficients, not from its form number**, which is routinely wrong in real files — the discriminant is exact arithmetic on data the file already gives, and a contradiction is reported with the coefficients believed. The general rule that separates the two: read the DECLARATION when it is the only source (144's outer-vs-inner boundary), read the GEOMETRY when the geometry is independently checkable. **Viewer picking**: click-select via unprojected ray + per-object triangle BVH + Möller–Trumbore, selection highlighted and named in the title bar (note: Avalonia's pointer stack ignores legacy synthetic `mouse_event` clicks — BUT **`SendInput` works**: synthetic clicks and drags injected via `SendInput` (absolute normalized coordinates, window activated first) DO reach the pointer stack, so interactive viewer features can be driven and screenshotted end-to-end rather than only exercised by calling `Pick`/`Select` directly). **Band-crossing booleans on whole-solid fillets landed, and the fix was one sentence longer than the filed diagnosis.** The tracer's "stops one march step short of a bounded domain" property is not merely an accuracy loss: where that domain edge also bounds the face being split, the curve crosses NOTHING, face splitting finds zero crossings, the face is whole-classified and the result cracks along the entire boundary (measured on `FilletAllEdges(20×14×8, r2) − Ø6 cylinder`: the four band curves ended 5.5e-5 and 1.1e-2 from the two tangency lines, zero crossings). `BrepBoolean.SnapTracerEnds` extends each traced polyline onto the EXACT solution of E(t) = S(u,v) — the boundary edge against the other solid's carrier, a 3×3 Newton seeded from the polyline's own last vertex (already on S, so only t moves) — **once, on the single curve object both faces share**, which is what makes the two solids agree; snapping per face during splitting would leave endpoints a sagitta apart and open a pinhole at every crossing. **That alone was not enough, and the second half is the third occurrence of a lesson already recorded twice**: `FaceSplitter.SplitByCurve`'s "is this stretch inside the face?" probe took the ARITHMETIC midpoint of the interior stretch, which on a polyline is a mid-chord point — 5e-3 off the surface on a fillet band, five thousand times the inverse-evaluation tolerance — so `TryProjectPoint` failed, the stretch was silently discarded as "leaves the surface entirely", and the face came back unsplit. Every site that samples a curve must ASK `FaceGeometry.ExactSampleParameters`; restating the test is how all three got it wrong. Verified: a Ø6 bore through two fillet bands and Ø4 cross-drills along Y and X are Validate-clean, closed, satisfy |A|−|A−B| = |A∩B| to 0.02–0.06% of the removed volume, and converge with density; **and a family nobody was aiming at came with it** — a bore swallowing a rounded rectangle's corner and breaking out through both adjacent walls now converges QUADRATICALLY onto its analytic volume (1.0e-4/2.7e-5/6.8e-6/1.7e-6 at 32/64/128/256). `BooleanFailureTests`' unclosable fixture had to move a SECOND time (to a shaft coincident with its own bore), and `Verified`'s unclosed-solid backstop is now unreachable from every `Shape`-level construction tried — the `TrimmedFaceRefusalTests` pattern again. Residual, filed: a tool drilled ALONG a band's own axis still refuses — and the recorded symptom had gone stale: it now fails at CLASSIFICATION (`Could not find a probe point on a face fragment`, which names the fragment since this pass — a lengthwise sliver band strip whose 12×12 probe grid contains no sample), not at splitting. **The baked-tracer-polyline half LANDED**: `PolylineCurve3d.Carriers` carries the two exact surfaces the tracer marched on (attached in `March`, preserved through end-snapping/`Simplified`/`GeometryTransform`, serialized as two optional trailing refs in `BrepArchive` — the boolean corpus holds reload volumes to 1e-12 through a deterministic tessellation, so a dropped pair would be a reload that tessellates coarser than its save), and `BRepTessellator.SampleEdge` refines each chord onto the exact intersection (`SurfaceCorner.TrySolvePoint`, minimum-norm Newton, weld-tier acceptance, INSERTS only so baked vertices pass through bit-for-bit) until it subtends one natural angular step: the band-crossing bore went 0.9988/0.9460/0.3229 → 0.9988/**0.9999/1.0000** at 32/96/192. The scope boundary is a measurement, not a guess: only OPEN branches in OUTER loops refine, because the paired strip/slab tiers absorb the density while `TriangulateBandWithHoles` folds on a denser hole rim (plane-cut torus bore: 0 → 3 base folds at 48/24, refusing at 192/96) — hole rims keep their baked density until that tier grows a dense-rim row path (filed). **Coincident (flush) PLANAR booleans landed** (`CoplanarFaces.cs` in Interop): flush embossing, stacked plates, butted blocks and a pocket floor flush with the host's own face all fuse into ONE solid with exact volumes, following the mesh boolean's model verbatim so the two engines cannot disagree — the shared region's rim is imprinted by the neighbours' ordinary transversal curves and the coincident fragments are classified by **normal agreement**, since a fragment lying on the other solid's boundary reads distance ~0 from its SDF (the B-Rep twin of the winding number being ½ there). Agreeing normals ⇒ the surface bounds union and intersection and vanishes from the difference; opposing ⇒ union and intersection bury it and only the difference keeps it; **exactly one copy survives and it is always the FIRST solid's**, the same deliberate asymmetry (see design.md §5). Coincident CURVED surface stays refused BY NAME before any splitting (`SurveyCoincidence` recognizes coaxial equal-radius cylinders and names the axis and radius). **Three rules the cases taught, all gated on a shared plane existing so transversal booleans are bit-for-bit unaffected**: a curve that never reaches a face's INTERIOR must not split it (when solids mate, each neighbour face's own boundary IS an intersection curve, and splitting a face along its own boundary is what the arrangement tracer cannot close); a face pair whose bounds meet in a single POINT is dropped (butting a boss against a plate puts two side walls corner to corner, and their carriers still cross in a full line — the line is real, the contact is not); and **a shared plane disqualifies the disjoint fast path** (two stacked plates of one footprint intersect only along their own boundary edges, so after the first rule they look disjoint and came back as two touching shells, which is the exact fusion failure the tier exists to fix). Two more findings worth keeping: **coplanar overlap must be decided by sampling the shared AREA, not by probing centroids** — two plates overlapping in a strip have neither centroid inside the other, so the first version missed the case that most needs it; and **the rim needs a second source**, because a sketch extrusion's wall is a bounded patch and `TryPlaneExtrudedSection` deliberately reports no section when the plane is flush with the generator's rim, which is exactly embossed text — so a coplanar face also takes its partner's OWN boundary curves as rim curves (skipped where an existing curve covers them), which is also the best weld available since the new edges ride geometry the other solid already references. `BooleanFailureTests`' unclosable fixture had to MOVE (the coplanar cross now fuses exactly); it is a bore swallowing a rounded corner and breaking out through both adjacent walls. Remaining future work: **tangent/coincident CURVED boolean cases** — two named configurations, both refused BY NAME and both pinned by test in every operation (`BooleanFailureTests`): coaxial equal-radius cylinders (a shaft in a bore of its own diameter) and the tangent BICYLINDER (equal-radius perpendicular cylinders through one axis point, whose degenerate-Steinmetz cut wraps its partner's band with its v-range overlapping the band's own rim, so there is no side to send each boundary loop to). The tracer finds both correctly; what is missing is clipping the two trims against each other ON a curved carrier, which is a 2D arrangement in the shared surface's parameter space rather than the corner SOLVE `SurfaceCorner` already provides. The modelling answers — a working clearance, or the implicit route — are exercised beside the refusals rather than merely named. Also: fillet gaps that remain after the corner work (variable-RADIUS fillets, cliff/vertex-blend run terminations, arc-terminal runs; arc-rim sharp corners are a documented POLICY refusal — see design.md — and band-crossing booleans on whole-solid fillets carry a precise diagnosis in todo.md), NURBS surface export. **STEP follow-ups landed**: length units are resolved and SCALED on import (the authoritative unit is the one the GLOBAL_UNIT_ASSIGNED_CONTEXT references — an imperial file's CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT('INCH', 25.4 mm) chain necessarily *contains* a metric base-unit entity that is not the file's unit, so a bare scan of length-unit entities is wrong by construction); CONICAL_SURFACE/TOROIDAL_SURFACE synthesize onto `RevolvedSurface` (cone = slanted-line generator over the face's boundary-vertex axial range, apex snapped EXACTLY onto the axis so the pole machinery sees a pole and not a hair-thin rim; torus = the minor circle in the frame's x–z plane), which surfaced the missing **closed-generator two-rim disambiguation**: a split torus's two bands see the SAME two junction circles and min/max hands both faces the same half — the rim coedge senses (with the rim circle's own axis alignment folded in) say which rim is the generator's start, the same outward-band convention the single-rim pole case reads. **A partial revolve of a SINGLE closed profile curve now recovers its angle too** (`TryAngleFromRotatedCopy`): a one-curve profile has no segment junctions, so the sweep traces no axis-centered rail arc anywhere and the angle recovery used to find NOTHING — a 1.2 rad elbow came back SILENTLY as a full turn with zero diagnostics, refused by the tessellator's full-domain gate three stages later; the angle is now read in closed form as the azimuthal rotation between corresponding samples of the generator and its rotated boundary copy (congruence checked in (radius, axial) profile coordinates), and the closed-generator diagnostic is exempted there since the face genuinely covers the whole generator; `TransformedCurve(NurbsCurve)` exports EXACTLY by transforming control points (a rational curve is an affine combination of its control points at every parameter, so weights and knots are untouched for any affine map) — NURBS-profile extrusions now round-trip at reconstruction accuracy; PARABOLA/HYPERBOLA/OFFSET_CURVE_3D map both ways with conic edge trims in CLOSED FORM (the parabola's parameter IS the local y coordinate; asinh of the B-component for the hyperbola), plus `Parabola3d.ToNurbs()` (the one polynomial conic: exact quadratic Bézier). **Healing completion**: `RetrimCurvedEdges` (FixGaps' parametric mode — LOCAL Gauss–Newton foot parameters, break-before-negligible-step for idempotence; the perpendicular residual is reported, never repaired) and `RepairShells` (ShapeFix_Shell: sense-consistency flood — flipping a face re-winds loops AND toggles IsReversed together — plus a global outward vote from boundary-loop fan volumes against containment parity, honestly AMBIGUOUS for pole-bounded/closed-band components like spheres, and shell repartition to one shell per connected component, all bit-stable no-ops on well-formed input). **Inverse-evaluation seed selection**: the base 17×17 grid now refines from every LOCAL minimum and its neighbours (global-best seed FIRST, so previously-succeeding calls are bit-identical; a folded NURBS hairpin measured 80/205 round-trip failures single-seeded, 0 multi-seeded), and the Extruded/Revolved 1D overrides defer to the base grid on failure so "the override is never worse than the base" holds by construction — the fixture lesson worth keeping: a MIRROR-SYMMETRIC hostile fixture can be secretly benign, because every wrong-branch seed then has a same-branch mirror at the identical tangential offset and the argmin can never land on the wrong branch; the hairpin only bites with asymmetric branch parameterizations. **Inverse evaluation on a swept surface is a ONE-dimensional solve — this was ~100% of the B-Rep boolean's cost.** `ExtrudedSurface`/`RevolvedSurface` override `TryProjectPoint`: an extrusion's `v` is fixed by the direction component, so only `Q(C(u) − p)` constrains `u`; a revolve's `u` is the azimuth in closed form once `v` matches the generator's (radius, axial) profile. Both scan the *generator alone* at the base class's u resolution and refine with 1D Gauss–Newton on the exact `DerivativeAt`. The generic base scans a **17×17 (u,v) grid that re-evaluates the same generator point once per column — 289 curve evaluations where 17 carry all the information** — and since inverse evaluation is the inner loop of every face pullback, it dominated everything: 7-glyph engraving **65.3 s → 4.0 s**, a 5-hole drilled plate **14.4 s → 1.7 s**, the Interop test suite **4m16s → 22 s**, DocsGen **106 s → 41 s**, with volumes bit-identical and not one of the 37 rendered docs PNGs changing a byte. Profiling first was essential: the two *plausible* culprits (an over-conservative face-pair prefilter, and face-splitting/`SealSeams`) were both innocent — only 113 of 894 pairs survive the prefilter and all 113 intersections resolve in ~1 ms. **When a surface is a sweep, never inverse-evaluate it in 2D.** (`SweptSurface`/`NurbsSurface` still use the base grid, legitimately — an RMF frame varies along the path.) `Scene.PreMesh` and `AmbientOcclusion.Prime` also prime parts **in parallel** now (`ParallelFor.Blocks`; parts are independent by construction and the first failure *in scene order* is rethrown with its original stack, never a scheduling-dependent `AggregateException`). **Sketch-extrusion booleans (pockets, slots, engraving) are exact** — the fix for a silent open-mesh bug, and its lessons are load-bearing: an extrusion of a straight generator IS a plane, but a **bounded** one, so the analytic line must be clipped to the generator's parallelogram, not merely to the query region (`TryPlanarPatch`/`PlanarPatches`, straightness decided by sampling the actual generator at the weld tier — `Underlying` is only a type hint). Better still, when the generator's plane is **parallel to the cutting plane** the section is exactly the generator *translated* by `direction·v` (`TryPlaneExtrudedSection`): exact for ANY generator shape (lines, slot arcs, glyph Béziers), and — the load-bearing property — its endpoints come from the generator's own points, so consecutive profile segments hand over their shared corner **bit-for-bit** and the outline closes. Note the precondition is NOT "plane ⊥ direction", which is why sheared tools are exact too. **The marching tracer breaks its step AFTER `Correct` leaves the domain, so it always stops up to one march step short of a bounded generator's ends — never use it where a curve must terminate exactly on a boundary** (measured: cuts missing corners by 0.015–0.284 mm, so the outline never closed). Worse, when truncation left no usable curves at all, the disjoint fast path buried the tool as an internal cavity: a closed, `Validate`-clean solid with the *wrong* volume. `BrepBoolean.Verified` now enforces two-manifold output and throws `BrepBooleanException` naming the operation, counting unpaired edges, locating a crack, and suggesting the implicit route — but it catches **unclosed, not wrong-but-closed**, so end-to-end boolean tests must assert analytic volumes. Numerical notes from the Modeling work: the generic `Surface.TryProjectPoint` wraps (not clamps) periodic u so Newton can cross the seam; plane⊥cylinder intersection circles are **phase-aligned to the cylinder's frame** (arbitrary-perpendicular frames caused tessellation cracks on rotated bores — and `DrillTests` winding depends on this); `BrepBoolean.ProbePoint` routes full-period-wrapping loops to the band path (projection jitter gives degenerate sliver loops nonzero area, and the planar path would probe on the fragment boundary where the SDF is 0); `SurfaceIntersection.Promote` sanity-checks that the generator lies on the candidate cylinder (a `TransformedCurve`-wrapped circle's `Underlying` is the untransformed circle). **"Lies on the carrier" is not the same claim as "IS the carrier", and the gap fabricates surface.** Promotion also has to check the generator sweeps a WHOLE turn: a rounded rectangle's corner is a QUARTER arc extruded, every point of which lies on the full cylinder, so a start-point-only guard promoted it and the kernel then reported intersections around 270° of surface the face does not carry. The tracer duly traced those, and the resulting open curves ended strictly inside a *tool* face — so the failure surfaced three faces away, as `Open splitting curves must start and end outside the face` on the tool, for a boolean that is a plain hole. The filed diagnosis blamed the parallel-cylinder analytic path and the face-bounds prefilter; both were innocent, and the prefilter could not have helped anyway (the two AABBs touch at a corner, and it is deliberately conservative-over). The blast radius was also wider than the report: a THROUGH counterbore anywhere in a rounded-rect plate failed, dead centre included. The guard is now geometric — every sample on the cylinder AND the accumulated swept angle a full turn — which as a bonus lets a quarter-arc corner section against a parallel plane as the exact translated ARC instead of a whole invented circle. **A sketch extrusion's SIDE WALL is a plane, and until it was treated as one a bore drilled into it could not converge.** `TryPlanarPatch` already recognized the bounded parallelogram, but only the plane-vs-plane pair used it, so a wall meeting a quadric fell to the marching tracer and the rim came back as a fixed ~57-sample polyline where the identical bore on a flat cap got a `Circle3d`. A fixed sample count is a FLOOR no tessellation density can lower, which is the tell: the error did not merely stay large, it stopped converging and changed sign as the analytic n-gon reference crossed it — −7.4e-4 / −5.3e-5 / +4.7e-5 / +6.5e-5 at 32/64/128/256, now 7.1e-14 / 6.8e-14 / 4.3e-14 / −5.3e-14. `TryPatchQuadric` mirrors the main switch's analytic cases for a BOUNDED patch (the case that actually fires is revolve-⊥-axis, not cylinder: drill tools are axis-touching revolves, and `PlaneRevolved` keeps the generator's bounded axial extent so no circle is invented above a blind bore's end), and containment is decided **closed-form, not by sampling** — the patch's (s, t) are affine in the point, so on a conic each runs centre ± hypot(a, b). **A conic poking out of the wall is now CLIPPED to the runs the patch carries** (`ClipConicToPatch`), which took a bore breaking out of a sketch extrusion's top face from REFUSED at every density ("unclosed solid: 6 of 20 edges used by 1 face") to +1.2252 / +0.3065 / +0.0766 / +0.0192 at 32/64/128/256, ratio exactly 4.00 and one-signed — and to within 1e-11 of the SAME plate built as a `Shape.Box`, whose walls are unbounded planes, at every density. **The clip is one harmonic per patch edge**: both patch coordinates are affine in the point and a conic is affine in (cos θ, sin θ), so each is exactly `c + a·cos θ + b·sin θ` and each edge is one `R·cos(θ − φ) = level − c` with roots `φ ± acos(…)`; membership is decided at the MIDPOINT of each interval between consecutive roots, because the four constraints are independent (two crossings of s = 0 can bracket a stretch that leaves through t = 1) and a midpoint is strictly in or out of every one, so the test is exact and no epsilon enters. A conic wholly inside is returned as ITSELF by reference — bit-for-bit what it always was, and a closed curve stays closed for the wrap-splitting and hole-splitting paths — and a run straddling the seam comes back as ONE `CurveSegment` past the domain end. **Closed form is not an accuracy preference but what makes the ends WELD**: a clipped end becomes a VERTEX shared with the neighbouring face (a bore off a wall's top edge ends exactly where the top face's own curve starts), measured **exactly** on the patch boundary and within **0 and 4.4e-16** — 0–2 ulps — of the top plane's own ±√(r²−d²), where the tracer stops up to one march step short and never reaches it; and the `Promote` guard is geometric rather than a type test, every sample staying inside the extrusion's own [0,1]² with escape **0.000**. **The one place a tolerance is unavoidable is a TANGENCY, and the rule is derived from which mistake is cheaper**: near a tangency the `acos` argument is within round-off of ±1 where acos has a square-root singularity, so the two roots come back ~1e-7 rad apart however exact the geometry is and the midpoint between them reads in or out by round-off — but the two errors are not symmetric, since DROPPING a run of span δ removes a chord of `scale·δ` of genuine curve (an outright gap) while KEEPING it leaves the curve only `scale·(1 − cos(δ/2))` outside the patch, second order in δ (2.7e-15 against a lost 8e-8 at a real tangency, six orders apart). So a dropped run is flipped whenever that excursion is inside the weld tolerance, kept runs are never dropped, and a touching conic comes back CLOSED — the clip's own derivation of the "err toward KEEPING" rule `ClipToFace` states one layer up. **Whether the round-off falls the safe way without it is ALIGNMENT rather than tolerance**, so the test is a FAMILY: two hand-picked tangencies both passed with the rule disabled, and a sweep shows 62 of 480 configurations pinholing without it against 0 of 480 with it. **The filed entry predicted the wrong failure mode**, which is the finding worth keeping: it expected the recorded fixed-sampling-floor signature (a non-converging, sign-flipping error), but the case the tracer CAN reach — a blind bore off one wall — converges quadratically through the tracer too (+0.4050 / +0.0997 / +0.0250 / +0.0063, ratios 4.06/3.98/4.00), because `SnapTracerEnds` and `SampleEdge`'s baked-carrier refinement already remove that floor once a usable branch exists; what the tracer could not do was produce a usable branch AT ALL when both walls are crossed, so this fixes a REFUSAL rather than a floor. Still deferred: the axis-parallel LINE pair (not a conic, and measurably needs no help). **A drill tool's flat POLE CAP breaking out of a face now works, and the entry filed against it named the wrong stage** — the fix is in `BrepBoolean.ProbePoint`, not in a planar-carrier recognizer. `Drill`'s tool is ONE axis-touching revolve, so its flat end is a `RevolvedSurface` pole cap where a `Shape.Cylinder`'s is a `PlaneSurface`, and a BLIND bore puts that cap inside the body where the breakout face CUTS it. **The pole path is right to skip the parity check and was wrong about which v the loop is at**: a single loop that WRAPS the periodic direction separates the pole from everything else, so the face is the pole's side and every v strictly between the pole and the loop is inside AT EVERY u — which is exactly why the loop must be measured by its CLOSEST APPROACH to the pole rather than by its average. The two agree on an uncut cap (whose rim sits at one v) and part company as soon as a cut crosses it; measured on a Ø6 blind hole in a 40×30×10 plate with its axis 1 below the top face, the average put the probe **0.106 ABOVE that top face**, so the fragment that should have been kept was classified away and the boolean refused with "3 of 19 edges are used by 1 face(s)". **`FaceGeometry.ContainsTwoSided` is NOT the fix** and it is the obvious reach: it errs toward inside by design and duly accepts that very point. Sweeping the breakout depth fixes three rows and regresses none; the headline case converges quadratically (+6.74e-1 / +1.59e-1 / +3.80e-2 / +9.71e-3 at 32/64/128/256, ratios 4.24/4.18/3.92) and the same cut made by a `Shape.Cylinder` converges together with it. **The annulus recognizer the entry called for was built, measured and REVERTED once**, which is the second finding: on its own it buys nothing, because the intersection there is a straight CHORD and a traced polyline along a straight curve lies on that curve at every point rather than only at its vertices — so the tracer's only defect is end truncation, which `SnapTracerEnds` already removes, and the whole boolean's output on this family is bit-identical with and without it. Worse, it TRADES refusals (fixing the exactly-diametral cut, breaking two shallower ones in trimmed-face tessellation), and the standing rule is that such an algorithm should not be reached at all. Its blocker was then established by subtraction rather than theorised: the SAME exact chord resampled to 25 points — identical geometry, identical endpoints, different density — passes all three rows, so a straight `Line3d` boundary takes 2 samples from `SampleEdge` while the disc's parameterization is ANGULAR and the chord crosses many u columns. **Both halves have since landed TOGETHER, and neither is worth landing alone** — `SurfaceIntersection.TryRevolvedDisk`/`TryPlanarDisk` recognize a coaxial disk as the annulus-restricted PLANE it is (the two planes' analytic line clipped to the region, to a bounded patch's parallelogram and finally to the disk's radial extent, one square root per rim; PARALLEL planes refused BY NAME so the axis-perpendicular arm and the coplanar-fusion tier keep every case they owned), and `BRepTessellator.SampleEdge` gives a straight edge the ANGULAR density of any face whose azimuth it crosses. **The defect the density rule fixes is that a straight curve is described exactly by its endpoints while the FACE it bounds may not be**: a chord's two ends both sit on the disc's rim, at the same v the arc closing the loop already occupies, so the pulled-back loop is a zero-area sliver out along v = 1 and back — a winding structure the trimmed tessellator refuses *however fine the grid around it becomes*. **The gate IS the correctness condition rather than a proxy for it**: the count is the azimuth swept about the face's own axis, so an iso-parameter straight edge (a cylinder's or a cone's ruling, a revolve's seam, a helical band's generator — every straight edge on an angular face that existed before the rule) measures zero and stays at exactly two samples with no separate test to keep in step, and extra samples cost no fidelity since every one is exactly on the curve. Measured across the eleven-row breakout sweep: baseline refuses z0 = 10; the recognizer alone fixes 10 and breaks 11.5 and 10.5; **the density rule alone is bit-identical to baseline on all eleven rows** (with the tracer route the chord is polyline-backed, so the straight-edge branch is never reached for it); both together pass every row, the diametral cut converging at ratios 3.94 / 6.12 / 3.63. The wide verification pass expected of a shared sampling rule came to ONE moved docs PNG (`holes-breakout`, **14 px of 1 792 000**, thirteen of them ±1 on the bore's dark interior) and no corpus movement. Two riders recorded honestly: the drilled-breakout corpus residual was UNCHANGED (0.107 / 0.694 / 0.840), so the entry's guess that this was the same work was falsified and the residual isolated to the drill's bore WALL, which the density rule structurally cannot reach (the wall's boundary is two traced polylines and two rim arcs, nothing straight) — the CYLINDRICAL-BAND carrier below is what closed it; and z0 = 11.5, whose wall had been taking an anomalously coarse route with its traced chord (222 facets at 64 segments where its neighbours take ~2 000), joined the family's known residual — volume error 2.5e-2 → 8.0e-2 at 64 segments and agreement 0.99997 → 0.98054 at 96/48, beside 10.5's own 0.97901, with slivers 2 → 0 and the disc fragment strictly better (141 → 102 facets, same area) — and RECLAIMED both exactly (2.5e-2 and 0.99997) once the wall was fixed, its wall then measuring 94.2403 against an exact 94.2478, i.e. inscribed where the family's walls used to bulge past their analytic area. **The bore WALL is a cylindrical band, and recognizing it is what removed that whole family of residuals** (`SurfaceIntersection.TryCylinderCarrier`/`TryCylindricalBand`): a full-turn revolve of an axis-PARALLEL straight generator IS a cylinder, so the two straight lines a bore makes where it breaks out through a face parallel to its axis are exact `Line3d`s clipped to the band's own axial extent, where before there was no analytic arm at all and they arrived as a 49-sample tracer polyline **at every density** — a fixed floor. The recognizer tests the radial VECTOR rather than the radius, which carries two conditions at once: a constant radius makes the swept set a cylinder and a constant radial DIRECTION makes the generator straight and axis-parallel, so the band's own u IS the promoted cylinder's azimuth and nothing is re-phased — a generator merely at constant radius (a helix) sweeps the same points under a different parameterization, which is the "lies on the carrier is not IS the carrier" trap, refused with no separate guard; an oblique plane's conic is accepted only when it lies wholly inside the axial extent (one comparison, since the axial coordinate along a conic ranges over centre ± hypot of the two semi-axis components) and otherwise falls to the tracer. **The arm sits AFTER the axis-perpendicular one, so every circle a drilled cap already produced keeps `PlaneRevolved`'s arithmetic bit for bit** and only cases that previously marched can move — the append-never-replace ordering that is the whole safety argument for touching that switch. Measured: `drilled breakout` went 0.107 / 0.694 / 0.840 → **0.994 / 0.957 / 0.989** on 92 / 220 / 424 facets against 374 / 1 008 / 3 682 and is now an ordinary Corpus member, while the drill and `Shape.Cylinder` routes for one blind bore stopped differing in GRADE as well as in answer (+0.4566 / +0.1151 / +0.0289 / +0.0072 against +0.4573 / +0.1146 / +0.0288 / +0.0072 at 32/64/128/256, i.e. a gap of 0.16–0.39% of the shared error where it had been 47%, and a gap that now CHANGES SIGN — so the test that used to assert the drill route was the coarser one lost its sign assertion rather than relaxing it). **A left-hand thread is not a right-hand thread on some other frame** — every right-handed frame is a rotation of every other, so handedness cannot enter through the pose and has to enter the arithmetic; the near-miss that looks like it works is flipping two axes, which is a half-turn, not a reflection. **A grid quad's diagonal is chosen by GEOMETRY** (`PolygonFan` — shorter 3D diagonal, shared by `Triangulated`/`SignedVolume`/mass properties/`RenderMesh`/the writers). It used to be chosen by CORNER ORDER, which made a mirrored sheared band carry a systematically 3× larger volume deficit than its twin off the IDENTICAL vertex set (measured 0 of 131 200 differ at 1e-9); the two now measure identically. The tie guard is relative (1e-12 on squared lengths), because a UV-sphere quad's diagonals are mathematically equal and an exact comparison hands 408 of 960 splits to round-off. **A `CylinderSurface` band tessellates from its RING LOOPS, not from a parameter grid**, which inverts the trim-the-surface rule: wrap-splitting one keeps the whole cylinder on both sub-bands (shortening the loops IS the edit) where an extruded or revolved band must have its surface shortened too, its vCut is one exact dot product with no projection error to refine away, and — being unbounded in v — its "the cut coincides with a boundary ring" test must read the rings, since against an infinite domain it could never fire. **A wrapping cut whose v VARIES now works on a plain cylinder too — and the refusal that stood in its way named the wrong stage.** It read "the sub-bands would need trimmed cylindrical tessellation with wrapping loops", backed by the observation that lifting it made the split succeed and moved the failure three stages downstream to `Directed edge appears twice` from the mesh builder. The split was right and the trimmed path already pairs wrapping loops by pulled-back u; the defect was `BRepTessellator`'s ROUTING. A ring-driven band is tessellated by pairing ring sample j to ring sample j, and the gate asked only how many loops the face had — **but the path's real precondition is that the two loop polylines sample the same AZIMUTHS in the same order**, true of two natural rings (circles on the cylinder's own frame at identical parameters, radial parts equal to a few ulps) and false of two independently traced cuts, whose phases have no relation. Measured, index-pairing those folded 18 of the tool band's 40 quads inward at a worst facet-vs-surface agreement of −0.0000, and the duplicated directed edge was that fold reaching the welder. `IsRingPairedBand` checks the condition and hands everything else to the trimmed path. Two lessons: **a tier's gate should BE its correctness condition, checked, not a proxy for it**; and **"the failure moved downstream when I lifted the guard" shows the guard is load-bearing, not what it is guarding against** — the new failure was a different bug the refusal had been hiding. Residual, pre-existing and now measured: a traced rim's sample count comes from the tracer's ARC-LENGTH step, so a small band gets few samples per turn — a Ø3 drill through a Ø10 cylinder reads 0.974/0.949/0.565 facet agreement at 32/96/192 (fold-free, volume converging quadratically) against 0.858/0.9995/0.9998 for a Ø10 drill through a Ø26 one, which is why the corpus member is the latter.
5. **Interop completion** 🔶 the fourth edge landed — **mesh→B-Rep reconstruction** (`MeshToBrep`/`SolidAssembler`; see the status paragraph and design.md §5): tessellated CAD geometry re-recognised as a parametric solid of analytic faces (plane/cylinder/sphere), verified by the exact-radius-across-densities and face-count tests. Remaining: cone/torus/NURBS surface fitting, spherical faces WITH boundaries (dome caps — the `SurfaceIntersection` plane∩sphere path exists but is untested), a seamed single-face solid (whole sphere), and scan reverse-engineering (noise/outliers/holes). Plus the older residuals: remaining conversions, mesh↔SDF, robustness passes
6. **Simulation** 🔶 meshing + linear-static structural + thermal + modal + buckling/harmonic + transient dynamics + fatigue + topology optimisation done — **`EngrCAD.Fea`**: `TetMesher.Mesh(closedSurface)` fills any representation's surface mesh with tetrahedra. Pipeline: exact-predicate Bowyer–Watson Delaunay of the surface vertices → classification by winding number at each element's centroid → **boundary recovery** (refine until every inside/outside separating face lies on the input surface, verified and named when it does not) → optional Delaunay refinement to a radius-edge target and/or a `Func<Vector3d, double>` sizing field (an `Sdf` composes directly). Outputs `TetMesh` (SoA, every element positively oriented — checked EXACTLY, so `Volume` is a sum of positive terms with no cancellation), per-element region ids from multi-body input, and boundary facets each naming the input triangle they subdivide (`TetFacet.SourceTriangle` — the seam for boundary conditions and B-Rep face identity). `QuadraticTetMesh.From` is the 10-node layer as a PURE function of the linear mesh (mid-edge nodes keyed on the canonical `(min, max)` corner pair so elements sharing an edge share the node; corner indices unchanged so corner data transfers with no mapping; Abaqus C3D10 / VTK ordering). `TetQuality` reports minimum dihedral BESIDE radius-edge because bounding radius-edge provably cannot exclude slivers, plus histograms and `SliverCount`. **Three design decisions, each reached by a failure** (design.md §3b): recovery works per planar PATCH not per input triangle (a Delaunay triangulation picks its own diagonal across a coplanar quad, so demanding the input triangle never converges — a unit cube exhausted a 500 000-point budget); **classification comes BEFORE the boundary** (an exactly-coplanar quad makes the tetrahedralization contain a FLAT tetrahedron whose four faces are both diagonals at once, so area coverage reads exactly 2.0000× and refines forever — 40 of 72 patches on a 12×6 UV sphere; deriving the boundary from an independently-decided classification has no tie to break, and it took the suite from 95 s to 3 s); and refinement follows **Ruppert's rule in Ruppert's ORDER** (an encroaching circumcentre is never inserted, the sub-triangle is split instead — inserting first and repairing after cascades). Measured (win-x64, Release): `box 20³` at size 2.0/1.2/0.8 → 40 593/170 921/234 335 elements at 80 522/59 346/31 539 tets/s, mean-min dihedral 44.4/46.2/40.8°, radius-edge ≤ 1.82/1.88/2.33, slivers 1.0/0.7/1.6%; volume residuals 1.7e-15 … 1.1e-13 throughout and **zero recovery rounds on every well-formed surface**. **Refinement is not a quality option on curved bodies, it is what makes the mesh exist**: a sphere's tessellation vertices are ALL exactly cospherical, so a tetrahedralization with no interior vertices is slivers by construction (48×24 sphere: mean-min dihedral 5.5° and 85.9% slivers conforming-only, 39.8° and 4.8% with refinement). **What recovery wants of its input was MEASURED, and the filed limitation was wrong in two directions.** It read "recovery takes CAD tessellations but NOT irregular remeshed surfaces — near-uniform vertex spacing with no structure leaves enough triangles failing to be Delaunay faces that red subdivision cannot clear them". Neither half holds. **A remesh is not the obstacle**: a remeshed sphere meshes in **zero** recovery rounds at three target edge lengths once the remesh is Delaunay-clean (`RemeshOptions.PreventLongEdgeFlips`), *with one patch per triangle* — the exact configuration the explanation blamed. **And triangle quality is not the criterion either**: a remeshed box at a **0.145°** worst angle and radius-edge **198** meshes, while a remeshed sphere at **27.9°** and **1.07** is refused, and the structured cylinder that recovers in zero rounds has WORSE triangles (3.74°, 7.66) than the remeshed sphere that does not. The real condition is that the surface triangulation must **already be the boundary of the Delaunay tetrahedralization of its own vertices** — where the surface is FLAT a patch absorbs any diagonal so nothing has to be recovered (a box is 6–7 patches however badly triangulated), and where it is CURVED every triangle is its own patch and must appear VERBATIM, which is the requirement the patch abstraction exists to avoid, arriving through the back door because curvature leaves it nothing to group. The practical answer is one flag: the remesher's flip stage is valence-driven with **no length term**, so it can replace a Delaunay diagonal with a longer one, and `PreventLongEdgeFlips` is the difference between every refused sphere row and every zero-round one. **Recovery also does not merely fail to clear the rest — it does not converge, in two distinct ways, and both are now detected instead of spent on**: a plainly remeshed sphere runs 6, 10, 11, 7, 5 offending faces and then sits at 5 forever (5 at round 12 and still 5 at round 40 while 44 Steiner points a round are spent) — and note WHY the obvious identical-set stall test does not fire, because it is the interesting half: they are five DIFFERENT faces each round, each smaller than the last, until their three vertices agree to **1e-11** on a radius-10 sphere, refinement chasing its own tail into degeneracy, which a set comparison reads as progress; while a remeshed cylinder DIVERGES outright (162, 405, 869, 1810, 3570, with every offending face from round 1 touching a Steiner point the round before created — refinement manufacturing the defect it is refining for). So recovery stops once the offending count has failed to improve on its best for five rounds (`NonConvergenceRounds`, the trimmed-face monotone-decrease rule) and says a bigger budget will not help, and **the refusal measures the INPUT** (worst minimum angle, worst radius-edge ratio, the fraction of triangles with no coplanar neighbour) instead of advising "remesh the surface" — which was backwards for the input that most often reached it, since `MeshPrimitives.Cylinder`'s n-gon caps triangulate as a one-corner fan at 3.74° and every remesh of it tried lands between **0.013° and 7.7°** with radius-edge up to **2124**: remeshing was CREATING the slivers it was being recommended as the cure for. What genuinely remains is the curved non-Delaunay case, which needs protecting-ball segment and subfacet encroachment (Shewchuk's CDT construction) — red subdivision is not a weak version of that but a different thing that provably cannot reach it. **Sliver removal landed as `TetSmoothing.Smooth`** (design.md §3b), the post-pass for the one defect radius-edge refinement PROVABLY cannot bound: interior vertices are moved against a worst-incident-dihedral objective by a deterministic pattern search, and measured on a 20³ box **every sliver goes** at three sizes (190 → 0, 399 → 0, 1 149 → 0) with the worst dihedral 0.00° → 10–17° and volume drift 7.8e-15 … 2.1e-14 — **but the residual is INPUT-DEPENDENT and that is the caveat to keep**: the SAME 20³ box with its faces triangulated by the B-Rep tessellator instead of by `MeshPrimitives.Box` starts from the identical 190 slivers and finishes with 2 rather than 0, because a pattern search is a heuristic local optimizer and a small input difference changes which candidate wins a near-tie, hence the path. **Two causes were guessed before measuring and BOTH are wrong, so both are pinned by test**: it is not TRANSLATION (the same primitive anchored at a corner and centred on the origin both reach 0) and not the BUILD (Release and Debug agree bit for bit) — the lesson being that a heuristic's fixture-specific number invites a causal story, and the story is worth measuring before it is written down. So what is promised is DETERMINISM for a given input on a given build, not sliver-freeness, and the tests assert a strict decrease rather than zero because a fixture-specific zero is exactly the kind of claim that rots. **The technique choice IS the design decision**: exudation (weighted-Delaunay perturbation) is stronger and changes TOPOLOGY, so it would need the boundary contract, the classification and the region ids re-established afterwards, whereas moving points only keeps all four by construction — the boundary never moves so the volume identity is exact rather than measured, and every candidate position is accepted only if it leaves all incident elements strictly positively oriented BY THE EXACT PREDICATE, so `TetMesh`'s invariant is preserved rather than re-checked and hoped for. Two things reported rather than buried: **the MEAN minimum dihedral falls 2–4° while the worst rises** (the objective is the worst incident angle, so lifting it moves a vertex away from what its other elements would have preferred — the right trade when the worst element is what conditions the matrix, but a trade), and it costs **~10× the meshing it follows** (4.9 s against 504 ms at 40 593 elements), which is why it is a post-pass a caller asks for. **A deliberate boundary layer is frozen, not repaired** — every vertex touching an element stretched past `TetQualityOptions.AnisotropyThreshold` is left alone, since smoothing a layer back toward isotropy would destroy exactly the resolution it exists to provide; that inherits the partition's honest limit unchanged (a layer element and an accidental sliver are AFFINELY EQUIVALENT, so freezing by measured stretch necessarily freezes accidental stretched slivers too, and the count is reported instead of the ambiguity being wished away). Docs: `docs/examples/fea-meshing.md`. **Anisotropic boundary layers landed** (`BoundaryLayerSpec` on `TetMeshOptions`; design.md §3b; docs `examples/fea-meshing.md`): a graded stack — first-layer thickness, growth ratio, layer count, the standard controls — marched INWARD from a wall selected by a **`Facets` predicate, the same selector a boundary condition uses**, so a wall is named once. **It adds no volume algorithm**: the nodes are marched first, and what is left over is bounded by an ordinary closed triangle mesh (the offset wall plus the non-wall faces trimmed back to the stack's rim) that `TetMesher` already fills, so the volume identity, exact orientation, determinism and the refusal culture are inherited rather than restated; the stack's elements are emitted FIRST, so `[0, BoundaryLayerReport.ElementCount)` names them. Nodes march along an ANGLE-WEIGHTED per-node average (the `MeshSdf` pseudonormal convention) — a facet normal would move a shared vertex to several places and tear the layer along every edge where two facets disagree, which on a curved wall is every edge — and a rim node's direction is PROJECTED out of every non-wall plane it touches, so the stack's side wall is genuinely part of the part's surface rather than approximately part of it (how far the projection had to turn is then checked, 60° by default; a rim landing on a CURVED neighbour is refused by name). **Three decisions carry it.** (a) **The prism-to-tet diagonal rule is COMBINATORIAL, and `PolygonFan`'s shorter-3D-diagonal rule would be wrong here** — a layer quad on a flat wall is an exact rectangle whose two diagonals are mathematically equal, so the choice would fall to round-off on essentially every element of the stack (the 408-of-960 UV-sphere trap, except that a boundary layer is made ENTIRELY of that degenerate case). Dompierre's rule — a quad's diagonal contains whichever of its two base vertices has the smaller INPUT-SURFACE index — is symmetric in the two, so neighbours agree without communicating. (b) **The stage runs in TWO passes because the party that decides the interface triangulation is the FILL, not the layer**: boundary recovery works per planar PATCH precisely because Delaunay picks its own diagonal across a coplanar quad, so handing over an offset wall and assuming it comes back triangulated the same way is wrong on a box and wrong SILENTLY. March the columns, hand over the surface, read the interface triangulation back off the finished fill, build the prisms on that. The weld is by exact position, which makes the conformity check free: each interface triangle is then used by two elements and vanishes from the combined boundary, so **"every boundary face has a known tag" IS the statement that the two meshes conform**. (c) **`TetQuality` PARTITIONS rather than judging** — its sliver rule would call every legitimate layer element degenerate, and a report that cries wolf on correct output is worse than none — so `SliverCount` and the radius-edge figures cover the ISOTROPIC elements only while the stretched ones are counted (`AnisotropicCount`/`MaxStretch`) and measured in their own metric (`MinStretchedDihedralDegrees`: the min dihedral after un-stretching along the element's thinnest principal axis — only that axis, because a FULL whitening maps every non-degenerate tetrahedron to a well-shaped one and so carries no signal, whereas scaling one axis restores a layer element and leaves a needle exactly as bad). A mesh with nothing stretched reports what it always did, number for number. **The honest limit is stated rather than implied**: a layer element and an accidental sliver are AFFINELY EQUIVALENT (the stack element is four nearly-coplanar points too), so no purely local geometric measure separates them — what distinguishes them is whether the thin direction is shared with the neighbours and with the physics, which is intent, not geometry. **The frozen interface is the consequence to plan around**: once the stack has elements against its inner face, optional refinement must not insert a vertex there, so Ruppert's encroachment blocks interior points inside those triangles' diametral balls — on a plain two-triangles-per-face box that is the whole interior and NOTHING refines (measured: 72 declined points, a 6-element core). That is the standing rule of boundary-layer meshing showing through — **the surface mesh sets the layer's in-plane element size** — and it is REPORTED (`RefinementBlockedByFrozenBoundary`) rather than discovered. Note which paths are frozen: the two OPTIONAL ones (both merely decline, and the loop is still bounded without them), while boundary RECOVERY is deliberately left free, since a frozen patch that must be split for the mesh to conform at all is a real failure the layer's own interface check names better. Refusals, in the order they fire: a per-facet FOLD (the wall turning faster than the stack is tall), a trimmed face TURNING INSIDE OUT (two flat walls swapping places across a thin part), a global SELF-INTERSECTION, and non-positive leftover volume. **Nothing reachable from a real body exercises the self-intersection net**, and the reason is structural rather than lucky — two flat walls closing never CROSS, they swap places, and two parallel sheets that have swapped places are still parallel, so the face between them inverts first; and where a wall is curved enough to make the offsets genuinely cross, its facets fold before they get there (the `TrimmedFaceRefusalTests` pattern: the verdict is locked by test so the finding cannot rot). Verified: volume identity **0 … 3.8e-14 relative** over a 20³ box, a Ø10×20 duct, an L-prism with a concave edge and six decades of scale; first layer measured `0.15000000000000036` against a requested 0.15 and worst ratio `1.3000000000000074` against 1.3; the stack's outer skin has exactly the wall's area to 1e-9; every face used once or twice (the direct statement of conformity) with the once-used set exactly the reported boundary facets; two runs bit-identical. **And the existing solver fixtures still hold through a layered mesh** (`BoundaryLayerSolverTests`, linear/quadratic): structural displacement patch test **9.8e-16 / 9.6e-15** relative (strain 3.4e-14 / 1.4e-13), thermal patch test **2.4e-15 / 9.9e-15** (flux 9.1e-14 / 3.0e-13, energy balance 1.2e-14 / 2.6e-14), 1D slab **1.1e-14 / 4.0e-14** on a 100 K drop, equilibrium (pressure + gravity vs reactions) **1.2e-12**, and the cantilever converging on Euler–Bernoulli monotonically from the stiff side at **−1.27% → −0.32% → −0.16%** (420 / 1 688 / 3 796 quadratic elements). The patch tests are the ones that earn their keep, because a wrongly split prism is invisible to everything else — the solve converges and returns a plausible answer, but a linear field can only be reproduced EXACTLY if the elements genuinely tile the body. Not in v1 (filed): uniform thickness only with no `cos` correction at a convex corner (a box corner's perpendicular stand-off is 0.577 of the request; `MinMarchClearance` reports it), no auto-refinement of the wall triangulation, rims may only stop on FLAT faces, and concave corners stretch into a wedge rather than fanning — a peanut and a dumbbell both fail recovery on the resulting slivers. **Structural (linear static) landed** (design.md §3c; docs `docs/examples/fea-structural.md`): `AnalysisMesh` is ONE view over 4- and 10-node elements (the linear/quadratic difference reduced to two integers, so assembly/BCs/load integration/stress recovery/publishing are written once), `Material`/`Materials` (isotropic E, nu, density + the thermal triple; a nominal verify-against-datasheet catalogue — **now in `EngrCAD.Core` beside `ModelUnits`, shared with the document model's `Part.Material`, with the analysis properties OPTIONAL and the refusals moved to the point of use**; mm/N/MPa/tonne throughout), `StructuralModel` (materials per region + supports and loads over **facet selectors** — `Facets.Tag`/`OnPlane`/`FacingAlong`/`InBox`, where the tag is `TetFacet.SourceTriangle` so `TetMeshOptions.FacetTags` makes a condition name a B-Rep face; `Fix`/`Prescribe`/`Pressure`/`Traction`/`Force`/`Gravity`/`BodyForce`/`NodalForce`), `StructuralSolver` (AMD-ordered `SparseCholesky` or CG) and `StructuralResults` (displacement, strain, stress, von Mises, principal, reactions, `MeshField` publishing, `.vtu`). **Supports are ELIMINATED, not penalised** — constrained DOFs leave the system rather than getting a large diagonal, so the reduced matrix is genuinely SPD, its conditioning is the model's own, and a prescribed displacement moves cleanly to the RHS. **An unrestrained body is refused BEFORE the factorization, per connected body, with the surviving motion DESCRIBED**: the six rigid modes restricted to the constrained DOFs, null space by Jacobi eigen-decomposition of the 6×6 Gram (floor 1e-12 on eigenvalues = a 1e-6 relative singular value, the sketch-constraint rule since a Gram's eigenvalues are squared), each null vector unpacked into a translation and a located axis. Per BODY because a fixed part beside a floating one is singular in a way no whole-model mode describes; and the NULL SPACE rather than the rank, because a first version reporting "which candidates a pivoted Cholesky did not eliminate" named three *translations* for a model pinned at one node whose surviving motions were three *rotations*. **Verification is the deliverable** (all in `EngrCAD.Fea.Tests`, on STRUCTURED Kuhn-subdivision meshes so a measured order means something): patch tests exact to 1e-13 relative for both element types with the two orders agreeing on strain energy to twelve digits; manufactured-solution convergence **2.00/1.00 (linear) and 3.03/2.02 (quadratic)** in L2/energy against theory 2/1 and 3/2; a 100×10×10 cantilever extrapolating to **1.90494 mm, +0.01% from Euler–Bernoulli's PL³/3EI = 1.90476 and −0.76% from Timoshenko's 1.91962** (two effects cancelling — a 3D solve includes shear deformation, while a genuine three-dimensional clamp is stiffer than a beam's built-in end — and linear tets are 14% low at 12 288 elements against quadratic's 0.4% at 1 536); **Kirsch/Howland at K_tn = 2.4216 against 2.4324, −0.44%** (the finite-width correction being Peterson's fit to Howland's strip solution, `2 + 0.284L − 0.600L² + 1.32L³`, which at d/W = 0.25 is nearly 8% above the textbook infinite-plate 3); plus per-element rigid-mode annihilation, frame-indifference to 1e-12. **Directional materials landed** (design.md §3h; `ElasticLaw` — `FromMaterial`/`TransverselyIsotropic`/`Orthotropic`/`Anisotropic` + `WithThermalExpansion`, `StructuralModel.SetElasticity(region, law)`): a 6x6 stiffness rotated into global coordinates ONCE at construction and cached per REGION, so an anisotropic element costs one `B'DB` more than an isotropic one and nothing else. **It sits beside `Material` rather than widening it**, and the deciding argument is the FRAME rather than the field count — which way the fibres run is a property of how the stuff was laid into this part, not of the stuff, so it is per-region analysis data while density and name stay on the `Material` a BOM reads. Isotropic models are untouched **bit for bit** (the branch is on `IsIsotropic`, asserted bitwise; the general path is separately asserted to agree with the index form to round-off, and the thermal-load split measured 0.0 difference). Verified EXACTLY, since a uniform stress state in a prismatic bar is a constant strain and so lies in the linear-tet space: an off-axis lamina reads **135 000.00 / 20 267.42 / 12 406.66 / 9 000.00 MPa** at 0/30/45/90° against the classical `1/Ex = c⁴/E1 + s⁴/E2 + (1/G12 − 2ν12/E1)s²c²` to 1e-12 relative, with the whole strain tensor at 4.4e-17 of a 2.0e-3 scale; a free bar under directional expansion strains by exactly `alpha_i·dT` (1.1e-17 of 1.7e-3) with residual stress 1.2e-13 of the 15.12 MPa a restrained bar would carry, and a restrained bar carries σ = (−12.72, −27.95, −27.95) MPa to 3.6e-15 — three genuinely different numbers, so a law quietly collapsing to one scalar fails at the right magnitude. What it deliberately does NOT do is named: no composite catalogue entries, no laminate theory, and no directional failure criteria (so `MaxVonMises` on a composite part is a number with no engineering meaning), all filed. **Superconvergent stress recovery landed too** (design.md §3i; `StressRecovery.Direct|Superconvergent` on `StructuralResults`, plus `ErrorEstimate`): Zienkiewicz–Zhu patch recovery samples the stress where it is superconvergent (the element's own Gauss points) instead of at the nodes where it is worst, and **the error estimator is the deliverable while the accuracy is the by-product** — a solve returns a number whatever the mesh, and `ErrorEstimate` is the per-element and global answer to whether it is worth anything. Measured against the manufactured solution, as an L2 norm of the nodal stress field taken INSIDE the elements: linear elements go 2.302e-2 → **1.599e-3** (14.4×) at rates 1.418 → **2.300** against theory 1 and 2, quadratic 2.669e-3 → **2.348e-4** (11.4×) at 2.000 → **2.761** against 2 and 3; the effectivity index (estimated over true error, energy norm) runs 0.9544/0.9848/**0.9955** and 1.0187/1.0150/**1.0128**, monotone toward 1 from below and above; and a linear stress field is recovered EXACTLY (1.1e-12 of 3.2e2) with the estimate reading 0.00%. The 2.76 rather than 3.00 is reported as measured rather than rounded up, with the two candidate causes filed and a measurement that would separate them, self-equilibrated loads giving identical strains under two different 3-2-1 restraints, and exact gravity/pressure/total-force resultants. **Patches are per MATERIAL REGION** — a patch never spans an interface, contributions accumulate per (node, region) slot, the boundary fill walks within a region, and `NodalStressIn(region, node)` gives the per-material value the node-indexed `NodalStress` can only blend (`AnalysisMesh.RegionsAt`/`InterfaceNodeCount`); see the status bullet for the reach argument, the 92.9% measurement, the parallel-vs-series fixture finding and the reachability boundary. **Thermal (steady + transient + coupling) landed** (design.md §3d; docs `docs/examples/fea-thermal.md`): Fourier conduction `q = −k·grad T` with **one temperature DOF per node**, built as the same triple with the physics swapped — `ThermalModel` → `ThermalSolver.Solve`/`SolveTransient` → `ThermalResults`/`ThermalTransientResults` — over the same `AnalysisMesh` and the same `Facets` selectors, so `Fix`→`Temperature`, `Pressure`→`HeatFlux`, `Force`→`HeatLoad`, `Gravity`/`BodyForce`→`Generation`. `Material` gained conductivity/specific heat/expansion with the mm/N/tonne/s units documented (**conductivity is numerically the SI W/(m·K)**, since the milli- in the power cancels the milli- in the length; **specific heat is the SI J/(kg·K) times 1e6**). **Three things are genuinely different and only three.** (a) **Convection contributes to the MATRIX as well as the load vector**, so it is the one condition NOT pre-reduced to nodal heat — its halves are meaningless apart — and it is what lets a model with no held temperature be solvable at all. (b) **The capacity matrix needs a rule two degrees ABOVE the conductivity's** (`N·N` is degree 2p where `grad N · grad N` is 2(p−1)), and getting it wrong is silent: the conductivity's one-point rule gives a 4-node capacity whose entries are ALL `ρcV/16` — rank one, singular — **whose total is still exactly ρcV**, so a "does the capacity sum right" check passes it (measured spread exactly 0). (c) **The undriven refusal is a BOOLEAN per connected body**, not an eigen-solve: conduction's null space on a connected body is exactly the constants, so a held temperature or a convective facet anywhere kills it and a pure flux (Neumann) does not — where the structural check needs Jacobi to find which of six rigid modes partly survive. A transient of an insulated body is legal (the capacity term is positive definite on its own). Time integration is a theta scheme at a **constant** step, which is what makes **ONE factorization serve the whole run** — the "factor once, solve many right-hand sides" case `FeaSolveMethod.Direct` records as not yet arising structurally. **Backward Euler is the default for L-stability, and the honest counterweight is recorded beside it**: on a quenched 40 mm bar with 1 mm elements (h²/α = 0.1 s), at dt = 2.0/1.0/0.5 s backward Euler makes **zero backward moves** while Crank–Nicolson swings back by **105.9/81.5/56.3% of the temperature step** (64.8/42.6/24.0% still after five steps) — but at dt = 0.005 s BOTH undershoot (5.8% and 7.1%), because that is the **consistent capacity matrix**, not the scheme. The capacity is consistent only, and **row-sum lumping is unavailable for 10-node elements**: the row sums are **−V/20 at every corner**, a negative heat capacity — the same integral that already makes a quadratic element's gravity load negative at the corners. Coupling is `StructuralModel.ThermalLoad`, which applies `αΔT` as an initial strain AND arms the stress recovery to subtract it **from one stored field**, so there is no second call to forget; the load is self-equilibrated by construction (the shape functions are a partition of unity, so their gradients sum to exactly zero), which is why it adds nothing to the applied resultant and the equilibrium check survives a coupled solve. **Verification**: patch test 2.4e-16/7.3e-16 with flux at 2.3e-15/8.0e-15; 1D slab 1.4e-15/3.8e-15; convective (mixed-BC) slab 9.0e-14/3.9e-13 with the convected heat exact to 2.6e-15; hollow-cylinder log profile 1.0e-3/5.8e-4 relative; manufactured-solution convergence **2.01/1.00 (linear) and 3.05/2.02 (quadratic)** in L2/energy against theory 2/1 and 3/2; lumped capacitance to 3.0e-4 of the initial excess at Bi = 2.1e-3, semi-infinite erfc to 0.184 K on an 80 K step (2.3e-3), **time order 1.05 and 2.00**, whole-run first law 1.3e-14 … 8.5e-13; and the coupled bars exact — free bar expands to 6.9e-15 with stress at 8.5e-15 of `Eαdt`, constrained bar carries **−EαΔT to 2.0e-15**. **Flux at a material interface** rides the structural side's (node, region) slot table — `NodalFluxIn(region, node)` beside the blending `NodalFlux`, `AnalysisMesh.InterfaceNodeCount` reporting how many nodes blend — verified on a PARALLEL bi-material cube (the series arrangement provably cannot see it; see the stress-recovery bullet for the whole finding). **A superconvergent flux recovery and a thermal `ErrorEstimate` landed on the SAME shared ZZ machinery** (`FluxRecovery.Direct`/`Superconvergent`, `Direct` the default; design.md §3d): recovered flux rates 2.34/2.66 (linear/quadratic) against direct 1.43/2.00, 15.3×/7.8× lower nodal error at the finest mesh, effectivity approaching 1 — `SuperconvergentRecovery.Recover<TValue, TField>` monomorphized over a `SymmetricTensor3` stress and a `Vector3d` flux so the structural path is bit-identical, and the same p+1 cap (flux 2.66 below theory 3), whose interior sub-domain study now names the BOUNDARY FILL as the dominant cause (`ThermalFluxRecoveryTests`). **Modal analysis landed** (design.md §3e; docs `docs/examples/fea-modal.md`): `ModalSolver.Solve` → `ModalResults` — natural frequencies, mass-normalised mode shapes, participation factors and effective masses — over the same `AnalysisMesh`, materials and supports, with `ω = √λ` and `f = ω/2π` coming out in rad/s and Hz because the mm/N/tonne system is consistent in seconds (a tonne is a N·s²/mm). **The mass matrix IS the thermal capacity matrix under another name**, both being `∫(constant·N_i·N_j dV)`, so `TetElement.ConsistentMass` is the ONE implementation, `ThermalElement.Capacity` delegates to it, and `TetQuadrature.ForMass` is the one rule selector — which is what makes it impossible for the two physics to disagree about the two-degrees-above rule that is silent to get wrong (see the numerical note: rank 1 of 4 and 4 of 10, totals exactly right, and the rotational-inertia control). **Lumping is offered only in the form that is not wrong**: `MassLumping.RowSum` is refused BY NAME for 10-node elements (row sums `−V/20` at every corner = a negative mass), `Hrz` scales the strictly positive consistent diagonal to preserve the element mass and coincides with row-sum exactly on a 4-node tet, and the reason to offer either is that consistent and lumped **bracket** the truth (+0.134% / −0.186% on a 16-element bar). **This is where the direct factorization finally amortises** — `FeaSolveMethod.Direct` records honestly that factor-once-solve-many does NOT apply to the static solver, which factors and discards; here shift-and-invert Lanczos factorizes `K − σM` once and spends one back-substitution per Lanczos step (18–23 for three to eight modes), so no iterative linear solver is offered on this path at all. **Rigid-body modes are separated, not refused**: `RigidBodyModes.Surviving` was extracted from `StructuralSolver` so one computation serves the refusal there and the listing here, they are deflated out of the Krylov space, `VibrationMode.Number` starts at 1 on the lowest ELASTIC mode, and `RigidBodyMode.Eigenvalue` is the measured Rayleigh quotient of the exact rigid field (a conditioning measurement, not a constant). A fully restrained model's shift is **exactly zero** and its factorization is literally the static solver's. A mode shape has no amplitude and no sign, so both are stated conventions: `Shape` is mass-normalised, the published `MeshField` (`ModalResults.FieldNames.Shape(n)` = "Mode 1", "Mode 2", …, units "mode shape") is rescaled to a peak nodal magnitude of exactly 1 model unit, and the sign is pinned by making the largest component positive so two solves agree bit for bit. **Verification**: axial bar (ν = 0 with transverse DOFs removed, so the 3D and 1D problems are IDENTICAL and there is no modelling gap) matched to **+0.021%/+0.081%/+0.170%** with every value above the exact one as a Rayleigh quotient over a subspace must be, and convergence orders **2.04/2.01/2.00** and **4.13/4.25/4.12** against theory 2 and 4; cantilever bending 1/2/3 at **−0.07%/−4.34%/−9.98%** from Euler–Bernoulli with refinement monotone from above (852.10 → 838.30 → 834.92 → 833.78 Hz) and the degenerate pairs splitting by 0.043%/0.076%/0.132%; simply-supported within **+0.09%/+0.12%/+0.37%/+0.62%** of Timoshenko while diverging from Euler–Bernoulli by up to 8%; free-free six rigid modes at **2.41e-12 of the first elastic eigenvalue** with a seventh at −2.14% of Euler–Bernoulli; orthogonality `φᵢᵀMφⱼ − δᵢⱼ` at **7.1e-15/7.6e-15** and `(φᵢᵀKφⱼ − λᵢδᵢⱼ)/λᵢ` at **5.8e-13/1.6e-11** with both products assembled independently of the solver; and the cantilever's first-mode effective mass at **61.09%** against the classical 0.6132. **Buckling, stress stiffening, damping and frequency response landed** (design.md §3f; docs `docs/examples/fea-buckling.md`): `BucklingSolver.Solve(staticResults)` → `BucklingResults` for `(K + λ·Kg)φ = 0`, `ModalSolveOptions.Prestress`/`PrestressScale` for a preloaded modal solve, `RayleighDamping`/`ModalDamping`, and `HarmonicSolver` → `HarmonicResponse` for a steady-state sweep. **The geometric stiffness has the consistent mass matrix's SHAPE** — `Kg_ab = ∫(grad N_a·σ·grad N_b dV)` is a scalar per node pair replicated onto the 3×3 identity block, because an isotropic inertia couples no two axes and one stress tensor contracts each displacement component identically — so the assembly loop is the mass matrix's with a different integral, `TetQuadrature.ForGeometric` joins `For`/`ForMass` at degree 3(p−1), and the prestress is read through `StructuralResults`' own recovery seam (thermal-strain subtraction included). **The indefinite right-hand side changes the shift STRATEGY, not the shift** (see the numerical lesson below). **Only positive factors are reported** and the load factor multiplies the whole load CASE (a strut pushed from both ends has a zero resultant, so nothing is called "the critical load"). **Damping is a per-mode RATIO on this route and the one damping matrix in the project belongs to `DirectHarmonicSolver`** (`FeaAssembly.Damping`, whose factorization consumes values rather than products — the direct per-frequency solve landed; see the status bullet); what non-proportional damping still excludes is the damped NATURAL MODES — the QUADRATIC eigenproblem (complex modes, a 2n non-symmetric state-space linearization), a different solver, stated rather than implied away. **Truncation is a correction, not a caveat**: `StaticCorrection` switches on the mode-acceleration form whose bracket vanishes at ω = 0. **Verification**: all four Euler end conditions on a 120×6×6 column at ν = 0 (so the prestress is exactly −P/A, 5.45e-13 relative) — pinned-pinned 15 440.3 N / fixed-free 3 879.6 / fixed-pinned 31 338.4 / fixed-fixed 60 550.5, i.e. **−0.181% / −0.046% / −0.458% / −0.696%** from the shear-corrected (Engesser) loads and −0.671% / −0.169% / −1.453% / −2.618% from Euler; refinement monotone from ABOVE (16 122.23 → 15 553.67 → 15 449.65 → 15 438.52 N), the buckled shape a half sine to 2.4e-6, and the factor exactly inverse in the reference load (0.00e0). **Stress stiffening and buckling agree to nine digits**: `ω²(P)/ω²(0) = 1 + P/P_cr` holds on the discrete 3D system to **7.4e-10 relative** from P = −P_cr through +0.9 P_cr, one table checking the geometric stiffness, the load factor and the prestressed modal path against each other. Harmonic: resonant amplification **25.006 against 25.000**, half-power bandwidth 2.0005/4.0034/10.0541% against 2/4/10%, phase 90.073° at resonance, and a ONE-mode corrected response reproducing the static tip to **1.8e-16**. **Linear tetrahedra are unusable for stability** — 171 602 N against a true 15 468 N on a coarse mesh, still +31.9% at 3 550 DOF where the quadratic answer converged to 0.2% with 414. **Transient dynamics landed** (design.md §3g; docs `docs/examples/fea-transient.md`): `TransientSolver.Solve` → `TransientResults` steps `M·a + C·v + K·u = f(t)` by the Newmark / HHT-alpha family, over the same `AnalysisMesh`, materials and `Facets` selectors. **The step is CONSTANT so ONE factorization serves the run** — the effective stiffness `a0·M + (1+α)·a1·C + (1+α)·K` depends on the step, the scheme and the model and on nothing else — which is the third place the direct solver's amortisation argument is genuinely true, after `ThermalSolver.SolveTransient` and `StructuralSolver.SolveAll`. **The filed diagnosis was WRONG on its central claim.** todo.md said a transient is "the ONE consumer that genuinely wants `C` as a matrix rather than as per-mode ratios", and design.md §3f repeated it; proportional damping means every appearance of C is either a PRODUCT `C·x = α(M·x) + β(K·x)`, two matvecs the stepper already performs — and cheaper than one against an assembled C, since a mass matrix's node-pair block is a scalar times the identity while a stiffness's is full — or a scalar MULTIPLE folded into the effective stiffness as `(…)M + (…)K` through `FeaAssembly.Combine`'s new two-coefficient form. So under proportional damping no matrix is ever formed — the one damping matrix that now exists (`FeaAssembly.Damping`, for the direct harmonic solve) is the case whose factorization consumes VALUES rather than products, which is why it is the exception that proves the finding rather than a reversal of it. **The initial acceleration is SOLVED, not assumed**: `a(0) = M⁻¹(f(0) − C·v(0) − K·u(0))` is a solve against **M** rather than K, skipped only when that right-hand side is exactly zero, because a body released from a displaced position or loaded at t = 0 IS accelerating there and starting from zero puts a spurious half-step in whose symptom — a startup wobble that decays — looks exactly like physics. **An unrestrained body is ACCEPTED**, where the static solver refuses one by name: `K` alone is singular for a free body but the effective stiffness carries `a0·M` with `a0 = 1/(βdt²) > 0` and a consistent mass matrix is positive definite, so the stepping matrix is positive definite whatever the supports do — the same exemption the thermal transient makes for an insulated body, pinned by a test so nobody copies the static guard across. `TimeIntegration` is ONE value carrying β/γ/α (an enum beside two loose doubles would let a caller name a member and supply coefficients contradicting it) with both stability conditions enforced at construction: `γ < 1/2` refused as
