NEGATIVE numerical damping (the amplitude grows at every step size), `2β < γ` refused as conditionally stable (the safe step needs the largest eigenvalue of `Kφ = λMφ`, which nothing here computes), and explicit central difference refused for a STRUCTURAL reason — it pays only with a diagonal mass matrix, and row-sum lumping is itself refused for 10-node elements. `HilberHughesTaylor(0)` IS `AverageAcceleration`, asserted as a value AND as **bit-identical output** over 4824 values. **The energy balance is an IDENTITY**: the trapezoidal update relations give `E(n+1) − E(n) = (dt/4)(v(n)+v(n+1))'[M(a(n)+a(n+1)) + K(u(n)+u(n+1))]`, and the equation of motion at both ends turns the bracket into exactly the work minus the dissipation — so `EnergyBalanceResidual` is a defect detector rather than a tolerance (**4.4e-13** relative over 3000 steps undamped, **1.4e-13** with work 1.44 and dissipation 6.28 both large), and for a dissipative scheme the same number becomes the MEASUREMENT of what the algorithm removed. **Verified on a model whose reduced system is exactly 1×1** — every DOF restrained but one, which is the idiom the modal work records as wrong in DYNAMICS and is legitimate here precisely because nothing is being modelled: the scalar oscillator IS the finite element problem, so there is no discretization error at all and its k and ω come from the static and modal solvers. Step amplification exactly **2.0000**, damped step **1.8544825** against 1.8544679, four phase-error cases within **0.1%** of a DERIVED prediction (Newmark's amplification argument is `W − W³/12`, so the algorithmic frequency is `ω(1 − W²/12)` and the error after N periods is `amplitude·2πN·W²/12`), harmonic drive **0.027%** from both the closed form and `HarmonicSolver`, time orders **2.000 / 2.000 / 0.9925 / 2.000** (Newmark / HHT / the first-order γ = 0.6 CONTROL / a real mesh's mode), a single-mode seed leaking **8.5e-11** out of its mode and matching the predicted algorithmic frequency ratio to **eleven digits**, and a free body reproducing `Σ(M·a) = F` to **1.2e-11**. **Two fixture traps, both found because a measured order was impossible.** (a) A convergence error read at a whole number of periods sits at a MAXIMUM of the cosine, where the exact solution is stationary and a phase lag enters as `u0·d²/2` rather than `u0·d` — an O(dt²) scheme measured **3.9997** against theory 2. Moving the probe to a QUARTER period fixes the phase reading and breaks the other half: there the amplitude multiplies zero, so the first-order control — the row whose whole job is to prove the study can tell 1 from 2 — measured **1.344**. The error must be a NORM over the run on a time grid common to every level; generally, **a convergence probe measures the error component its sampling point exposes, and the control that exists to catch a broken study can be broken the same way**. (b) A two-point energy ratio measured HHT's spectral radius **4.138% high at every radius** and converged to that offset rather than drifting, which looked like a wrong α-to-ρ formula; deriving the limit shows the amplification matrix is **DEFECTIVE** — eliminating the acceleration leaves a 2×2 block whose discriminant `trace² − 4det` is IDENTICALLY ZERO for every α, so there is a double real eigenvalue at −ρ∞ with one eigenvector and the state decays as `n·ρⁿ`. Dividing the n² out of the energy is the whole correction and it is exact: `(30/20)^(2/20) = 1.041380`, the measured offset to six digits. The formula was right and the ESTIMATOR was not. **Fatigue post-processing landed** (design.md §3j; docs `docs/examples/fea-fatigue.md`): `FatigueAnalysis.Evaluate(a, b, curve, options)` → `FatigueResults` — S-N life and Goodman/Gerber safety factors over TWO static load cases, the extremes of one proportional history, which is exactly the pair `SolveAll` returns from one factorization; arithmetic over results that already exist, no matrix touched. The per-node scalar is the **signed von Mises** (sign of the hydrostatic trace — the first-principal alternative needs an eigensolve and JUMPS when two nearly equal opposite principals swap magnitude, and in the uniaxial state the data was measured in the two agree exactly), whose blind spot is a finding rather than a defect: **a reversed pure-shear cycle is invisible to ANY scalar signed equivalent** — negating a pure shear tensor is a rotation of it — which is the proof attached to the multiaxial refusal (critical-plane methods are a different computation over a different input). `SnCurve` is Basquin + S_ut with the **endurance limit DERIVED from the line at its own 10⁶ knee** (the fine-pitch tap-drill rule — never a second stored column), flat beyond it; the aluminium rows state no knee because the material has none, and a safety factor for them REQUIRES `FatigueOptions.DesignLife` by name. `FatigueMaterials` transcribes six SAE J1099/Dowling rows in datasheet form (MPa, which is also the model unit — no conversion for a transcription test to hide behind), flagged verify-against-datasheet; the tests pin the line at its own anchors BIT-exactly (`StressAt(0.5)` IS σ'_f since `Math.Pow(1, b)` is 1) and cross-check the derived steel endurance/UTS ratios into the classical band four independent transcriptions must conspire to hit (0.40/0.40/0.34). Corrections: zero mean is the identity exactly (the non-tensile branch returns the amplitude verbatim), allowable at σ_m = S_ut is exactly zero, **compressive mean takes no benefit by default** (crediting it needs confidence the compression survives at the crack-starting surface for the service life), mean ≥ S_ut is STATIC failure. Publishing spellings are the design: **infinite life = NaN** (the VTU no-value convention `FieldRange.Of` already skips, so a mostly-immortal part still ranges a usable legend), **sub-cycle life floors at one cycle** (ranging skips only NaN — a −∞ from log10(0) would poison the minimum), and **life publishes as log10(cycles) with the units string saying so** because `FieldRange.Normalize` is linear (native log-scale display filed). The safety factor is the RADIAL load multiplier and is **verified by its own definition**: re-solving with the loads scaled by the measured factor lands the critical node exactly ON the line (min factor 1.0 to 1e-9, Goodman AND Gerber) — an independently restated formula would agree with a broken implementation making the same mistake; the definition cannot. R = −1 measures a mean of exactly 0.0, and the reason is institutional memory: IEEE round-to-nearest commutes with negation, so negating the load negates every solve and recovery output bit for bit. Full pipeline vs a hand-computed life: ±400 MPa on SAE 1045 → 5.92e3 cycles, log10 within 1e-6 of the Basquin inversion. Refused loudly: mixed mesh instances, mixed `Recovery`/`Averaging` (the gap between recoveries would be booked as alternating stress); refused by NAME as non-goals: welds (detail-class methods), critical-plane multiaxiality. **Variable-amplitude (rainflow) fatigue landed** (`Rainflow.Count` = ASTM E1049's three-point algorithm verbatim with the standard's Fig. 6 example transcribed cycle for cycle; `FatigueAnalysis.Evaluate(TransientResults, curve, options)` = Miner's rule over every node's signed von Mises history, mean correction per counted cycle through the SAME `EquivalentAlternating`): the filed design questions are answered by arithmetic — every node in one pass (8 bytes per stored state per node, small beside the fields each state retains; the counting sees the STORED states, so a fatigue run stores every step) and the open end is an option (`AssumeRepeating` — E1049's one-shot residual halves, or one period rearranged to the largest-magnitude extremum with residual halves paired by EXACT (range, mean) key, a pairing not a tolerance since both flanks of a closed excursion share the same two point values; the rotation is of the RAW samples because the turning-point structure at the seam differs). Oracles: the total-variation identity `Σ2·count·range = Σ|Δ|` exact on a pseudo-random history (it holds for ANY input, so it catches a dropped range on histories nobody hand-checked — but a counting can pair the WRONG points and still pass it, which is why the ASTM example is asserted cycle for cycle), and a constant-amplitude history built through the real `TransientResults` seam degenerating to the static pair BIT-equal (IEEE addition is commutative, so the two `0.5·(a+b)` means cannot differ; damage exactly count/life). **The spectrum SAFETY factor landed** (`FatigueAnalysis.LoadFactor`, published as `RainflowFatigueResults.SafetyFactor`): the multiplier on the whole history at which the counted spectrum reaches its damage target, `RainflowFatigueOptions.DesignRepetitions` picking it exactly as `DesignLife` does (null = infinite life, R = damage 1/R, a knee-less curve refused by name). The filed entry called it "an iteration against a stated target life" — right about the mechanism and wrong about the shape, since there are TWO targets and only one iterates: the infinite-life one is closed form because it is not an accumulation (each cycle reaches the limit at its own static radial factor, so the spectrum reaches it at the smallest — the same helper the static field is built from), and the damage-target one has no closed form precisely where the endurance KNEE or a tensile MEAN engages, which is why `(R·D)^b` is kept as the test ORACLE (bit-equal on a knee-less curve, 4.5%/12.9% out where it does not hold) rather than as the implementation. The single-cycle identity is the check with teeth — a one-cycle spectrum's damage-target factor IS the static radial factor at `StressAt(R)`, agreeing to 1e-9 for every mean and both corrections. **The direct per-frequency harmonic solve landed too** — see the status bullet and design.md §3f for the model-carried damping vocabulary, the one-damping-matrix qualification and the exact-resonance refusal fixture lesson (reproduce the solver's pivot arithmetic VERBATIM or the refusal never fires). **Topology optimisation (SIMP) landed** (design.md §3k; docs `examples/fea-topology.md`): `TopologyOptimizer.Minimize` minimises compliance over one density per element by optimality criteria, and **the feature is the LOOP** — the only change to a shared path is an optional per-element scale on `FeaAssembly.Stiffness` (null SKIPS the multiply, so incumbent callers are bit-identical), and because design-dependent loads are refused the right-hand side is a gather assembled ONCE for the whole run. **The backlog's "the sensitivity IS the element strain energy `StructuralResults` already computes" was half right, and the correction is the useful half**: `ComputeErrorEstimate` accumulates a per-element energy and then DISCARDS it (its per-element array holds the estimated ERROR), and it integrates at `Degree3`/`Degree5` where SIMP needs `u_e' k0 u_e` at the rule the ASSEMBLY used — so the energy is computed in the optimiser at `TetQuadrature.For(order)`, and the reward is worth more than the saving would have been: `sum rho^p·E_e` and `f'u` are then the SAME number (1.5e-15 relative), two constructions checking each other. **Verified against closed forms, never against "it looks like a truss"** — which is this feature's real failure mode, since a plausible picture is persuasive and nothing else here flatters itself so readily; and the published 99-line-paper compliances are **NOT reproducible by a 3D tetrahedral solver** (2D plane-stress on structured quadrilaterals) so they are named as such rather than approximated toward. What IS asserted: a uniform bar at `c = c0/f^p` exact to 9 decimals at p = 1 and 3; a stepped bar reaching the classical fully-stressed design `rho_i ∝ N_i` within 0.04%/0.12% of `(L/2)(N1+N2)²/(2fEA)` (the case that exercises the SEARCH, since the uniform field is a stationary point); the sensitivity against a central finite difference at **1.8e-7** unfiltered and **9.9e-8** through the density filter's chain rule, run through the PRODUCTION evaluator so the gradient and the compliance cannot agree by sharing a mistake; the volume constraint at **7e-16** every iteration; and 0 rises in compliance over 60 iterations on all three filters. **The uniform-bar closed form caught a real defect on a fixture whose answer is a stationary point**, which is why it earns its place: with the volume constraint stated on the DESIGN variables rather than the physical density the run returned **1.79·c0 against the convex optimum's 2.00·c0** — a compliance BELOW the true optimum, reachable only by holding material the constraint said was not there, because a row-normalised filter is not volume-preserving. Fixing it costs nothing since the filter is LINEAR (`V(x) = (W'v)·x` exactly), so one transpose gives both the gradient and an exact evaluator and the bisection never applies the filter. **The filter is not optional and its radius is an ENGINEERING input with no default** (it IS the minimum member size, so a default would be a manufacturing decision made by a library); both defects it prevents are MEASURED rather than asserted — unfiltered leaves each element **0.555** from its face neighbours' mean against **0.020** filtered (28×) and reports a compliance **37% lower for the same volume**, and the structure moves **0.137/0.068** between mesh-refinement levels at fixed `r_min` against **0.014/0.014**. `TopologyFilter.Density` is the default on a VERIFICATION argument rather than a convergence one — it is a genuine change of variables so its sensitivity is the exact gradient of the computed compliance, which is what makes the finite-difference check meaningful — while Sigmund's `Sensitivity` filter is offered as the heuristic it is (the gradient of nothing, but 22 iterations against 89 and crisper), and `None` is the defect kept public so it can be measured; weights carry element VOLUME, which reduces exactly to the published uniform-grid form when volumes are equal and stops a patch of small elements out-voting large neighbours by being numerous. **`ExtractSurface` marches the mesh's OWN tetrahedra rather than going through `Sdf` + Surface Nets**, which the backlog proposed: `SurfaceNets.Polygonize` culls blocks on the **1-Lipschitz** property and a density field has no such bound (0 to 1 across one element), so the cull would drop surface silently — and the field is defined ON this mesh, so marching it is exact for the piecewise-linear nodal field with no second discretisation, while `EngrCAD.Fea` is a leaf that cannot see `EngrCAD.Implicit` anyway. Crossings are derived from the LOWER-INDEXED end of their edge (the exact-boolean seam rule, so every tetrahedron agrees bit for bit) with a crossing landing on a node interned as that NODE, and orientation is read off `grad g` so one dot product winds all sixteen sign cases; a B-Rep is refused by name (a faceted level set has no parametric surface to recover, and offering one would mean fitting). **A density field cannot be displayed on the design space's own mesh** — the first docs render sampled onto a box's EIGHT vertices and came back a smooth gradient reading 0.002 to 0.116 on a field whose true range is 0.001 to 1, a convincing picture of nothing; `TetMesh.BoundaryMesh`, whose vertices ARE analysis nodes, is the mesh that resolves it. Refused by name with the reason each would be WRONG rather than approximate: gravity/body force (self-weight is design-dependent and compliance stops being self-adjoint), a prescribed non-zero displacement (the force moves with the stiffness, so minimising `f'u` maximises compliance — the sign of the problem flips), a thermal load (`alpha·dT` rides `D`, so it scales with `rho^p`), local stress constraints (aggregation's parameter changes the answer) and several load cases (the weighting IS the design decision); nothing publishes a stress, since the stiffness carries a penalised modulus. **Releasing the field into a usable solid is TWO MEASURED stages** (`TopologyResult.Release` → `ReleasedTopology`; design.md §3k): the extracted iso-surface is stair-stepped at element scale and full of slivers, so smoothing FAIRS it and remeshing RE-TRIANGULATES to a uniform edge length, with each stage's volume delta, surface displacement and `TriangleQuality` returned rather than hidden — smoothing deliberately gentle (a full step melts thin members at −42% of the volume; the default costs ≈−6%), remeshing redistributing onto the smoothed surface (within 2e-14 of it) so its whole benefit is triangle shape (slivers 2 950 → 73, mean angle 21° → 48° on the docs MBB beam), the order smooth-then-remesh, feature detection off by the "reads the mesh you give it" lesson, and `Release` returning a `HalfEdgeMesh` because Fea is a leaf that cannot see `Shape` (`Shape.From(released.Mesh)` crosses back). Islands are reported not deleted; verified by trades (`Validate`-clean, closed, STL round-trips by signed-tet volume, the two stage deltas summing exactly) and the docs page now leads with the MBB beam, whose left–right symmetry a wrong support breaks.


The Query layer and Viewer grow alongside each engine as it lands, not as separate phases.

## Conventions

- C# `LangVersion` latest, `Nullable` enabled, `ImplicitUsings` enabled, file-scoped namespaces
- Root namespace `EngrCAD.*`, matching project names
- Tests: xUnit; every geometric algorithm gets tolerance-aware assertions
- Central build props (`Directory.Build.props`) for shared settings once scaffolded
